# Startup protocol and milestones

## Placeholder route implementation (November 4, 2026)

The route audit starts with `DockedCompatibilityHandler` and `AccountCompatibilityHandler`. Empty replies are valid for features with no earned or configured state, but these handlers also conceal missing persistence and feature operations. Work proceeds by client feature, with a single handler owning each implemented service/method pair.

| Feature | Audited routes | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Character settings | `charMgr.GetCharacterSettings`, `SaveCharacterSetting`, `DeleteCharacterSetting` | Implemented with private persistent values |
| Station directory | `map.GetStationInfo` | Implemented for every catalog station, including service masks |
| Personal contacts | `charMgr.GetContactList`, add/edit/delete, block/unblock, labels; `onlineStatus.GetInitialState`, `GetOnlineStatus` | Implemented for local pilots and saved NPC agents, including optional notification messages; organization contacts pending |
| Personal locations | `accessGroupBookmarkMgr.GetMyActiveBookmarks` and private folder/bookmark operations; `beyonce.BookmarkLocation` and bookmark warp | Implemented for personal folders; shared ACLs and scan-result bookmarks pending |
| Ship insurance | `insuranceSvc` quotes, contracts, purchase/replacement and cancellation | Implemented for personal station-owned hulls and NPC-loss payouts; corporation policies pending |
| Task tracking | `monolithTaskTrackerMgr.HasEverWarped`, `GetCompletedAchievementsAndClientEventCount`, `UpdateClientAchievmentsAndCounters` | Implemented with persistent client snapshots and supported server activities; pinned legacy UI is disabled |
| Home station | `home_station` read, candidates, selection, remote-change time and docked clone destruction | Implemented for current NPC station and school HQ; player-corporation remote offices pending |
| Agent directory | `agentMgr.GetAgents`, NPC owner/profile lookups and scalar `charMgr.AddContact` for agents | Implemented from pinned catalog with persistent private agent bookmarks; mission gameplay pending |
| Jump clones | `jumpCloneSvc` state, quote, preflight, install, rename, destroy and jump; `ship.LeaveShip` | Implemented for empty personal bodies in NPC stations; implants, structure/capital bays and Pochven installation pending |
| Personal calendar | `calendarProxy.GetEventList`, details; `calendarMgr` create, edit, invitations, responses and cancellation | Implemented for personal events and local Eden pilot invitees; corporation/alliance and official events pending |
| Notification inbox | `notificationMgr` history, unread/group reads, processed state and deletion | Implemented with durable contact-add/edit messages and native delivery; other gameplay producers pending |
| Contract preparation | `contractProxy.GetItemsInDockableLocation`, `GetItemsInContainer`, `GetNumItemsInContainers`, `SplitStack` | Implemented for personal station assets; escrowed assets hidden; corporation assets pending |
| Personal contracts | `contractProxy` create, details, login, escrow, lists, search, acceptance, rejection, cancellation, collection, courier delivery/failure, package inventory, auction bidding/buyouts and claims | Item exchange, couriers and auctions implemented at NPC stations; corporation/structure contracts, package subcontracting and bulk creation pending |
| Larger feature systems | Agent missions/journal, standings, drones/fighters and player corporations | Compatibility states; gameplay pending |

### Character settings

Inert code-object inspection of build **3662168** `eve/client/script/ui/services/characterSettingsSvc.pyj` confirms `GetCharacterSettings()` returns a dictionary, `SaveCharacterSetting(settingKey, value)` saves an opaque serialized string and `DeleteCharacterSetting(settingKey)` removes it. `Save` limits each value to **102400** characters/bytes and sends empty/None edits through `Delete`. It updates its local cache after successful RPCs; no server notification is required. Eden accepts text and binary payloads without interpreting serialized contents, preserves Unicode and arbitrary bytes, and rejects extra target IDs, invalid shapes, unexpected keywords and oversized values. Keys use an Eden-specific nonblank, no-NUL, 128-character bound. Every operation uses the selected pilot; there is no editable target argument.

World version **40** adds `WorldCharacterSetting`, keyed by pilot and name, with a foreign key and an exclusive text/binary value constraint. Upserts and deletes are transactional. Upgrading version 39 creates empty settings and retains existing attributes, biography and skills; missing keys delete idempotently. Settings apply only to client preferences: saving a preference does not implement any gameplay feature it describes.

Reference: installed `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp at fcbf0c2](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae/src/Marshal.cpp), including separate byte-string and Unicode representations. No codec or transport changes are required.

**Automated validation.** Focused filter: `FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSettings|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSheet|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`: **34 passed**, approximately **3 seconds** runner duration. Tests cover real router/wire requests, selection gating, Unicode/binary values, independent pilots, replacement/deletion, rejected edits with unchanged state, exact size boundaries, restart persistence and version-39 migration. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python policy suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests) and formatting verification passed; final build/test/policy/format checks took approximately 16 seconds combined. Initial development builds exposed a missing test import, and the first focused run exposed a Unicode reply mismatch; both were corrected before the passing run. No full .NET suite was run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Save a client preference using `characterSettings`, reconnect, and confirm it reloads; clear it and confirm it remains cleared after reconnect. Native presentation is unverified.

### Personal contacts and online status

Build **3662168** inert inspection of `eve/client/script/ui/shared/neocom/addressBook/addressbookService.pyj` confirms `GetContactList()` returns a KeyVal with `addresses` and `blocked` lists. Contact entries expose `contactID`, `relationshipID`, `inWatchlist` and `labelMask`; blocked entries expose `senderID`. `AddContact` and `EditContact` receive `(contactID, standing, watchlist, sendNotification, message)`. Bulk standing changes use `EditContactsRelationshipID(ids, standing)`. Deletion, blocking and unblocking take a list of IDs. `GetLabels()` returns a dictionary keyed by each label's bit value, with `labelID`, `name` and `color`. Label operations are `CreateLabel(name, color)`, `EditLabel(id, name, color)`, `DeleteLabel(id)`, `AssignLabels(ids, mask)` and `RemoveLabels(ids, mask)`. The client updates its own contact/label caches after successful edits, so these operations do not send duplicate cache notifications.

Eden now persists these operations for known local pilots. Offline pilots resolve through `config.GetMultiOwnersEx`, rather than disappearing when outside the caller's visible scene. All mutations derive the owner from the selected session. Standings must be finite and between -10 and 10. Eden bounds batches to 1024 entries and label names to 40 nonblank characters without NUL. Up to 63 labels use positive bits in the signed 64-bit mask. Assignment rejects unknown bits, label deletion clears the bit from all of that pilot's contacts, and reused bits never inherit stale assignments. Duplicate contacts/names, unknown pilots, self-targets, malformed arguments and unsupported notification requests fail with unchanged state. Blocks are independent of contact membership. They persist and suppress watchlist presence; chat/mail enforcement awaits those systems.

World version **41** adds `WorldContact`, `WorldBlockedContact` and `WorldContactLabel`, with pilot foreign keys, composite keys and unique per-pilot label names. Every edit/batch and label-bit cleanup commits in one transaction. Migration from version 40 preserves character settings and initializes empty contact data. Reopening does not reset saved contacts, blocks or labels.

`eve/client/script/ui/shared/comtool/onlineStatus.pyj` primes `GetInitialState().Index('contactID')`, fetches missing entries through `GetOnlineStatus(characterID)`, and consumes `OnContactLoggedOn/Off(characterID)`. Eden's online policy exposes selected-session presence for the caller's own local contacts. Initial state includes watchlisted contacts; blocked contacts report offline. The existing connection presence registry now retains in-space pilots as well as docked guests, while station lists still filter by station. A primed watchlist receives each observed online/offline transition once on session ticks, with no docking, undocking or ship-switch flicker. Logoff resets the connection's subscription and disconnect removes its presence. Changes shorter than a session tick can be coalesced. At that milestone, sending contact notifications/CSPA, NPC-agent contacts and corporation/alliance contacts remained unavailable; selecting Send Notification failed explicitly and required clearing it before saving. NPC-agent address-book support was added by the later directory milestone below. The later notification-inbox milestone adds personal contact messages; paid CSPA and organization contacts remain pending.

References: unchanged pinned `code.ccp` hash and Carbon Blue revision **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae** recorded above; the existing rowset, KeyVal and list adapters remain in use. No gameplay Python was executed and no client was launched.

**Automated validation.** Development filter `FullyQualifiedName~Contacts|FullyQualifiedName~OnlineStatusTests|FullyQualifiedName~StationGuestTests|FullyQualifiedName~TypedCallResultTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotServicesTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSettings|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **64 tests** in approximately **6 seconds**. Coverage includes typed state, actual Blue replies, rejection/unchanged-state checks, Unicode labels, label-bit limits and reuse, version-40 migration, restart persistence, offline names, blocks, repeated docking/reconnect cycles and logoff. Early development found a test KeyVal-unwrapping issue and a concrete dictionary adapter mismatch, both corrected before the passing run. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~PilotContactsTests|FullyQualifiedName~StationGuestTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSettings|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **81 tests** in approximately **7 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python policy suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed; final build/test/policy/format checks took approximately **21 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite is authorized or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Add another local pilot with Send Notification cleared, assign a label and reconnect; verify the contact and label remain. With both pilots connected, check watchlist indicators through logoff/reconnect. Native rendering and interaction remain unverified.

### Catalog station directory

The old `map.GetStationInfo` compatibility reply contained one row for the caller's home/current station and a zero service mask. Build **3662168** `eve/client/script/ui/services/uisvc.pyj`, `UI.GetStation`, calls the method without arguments and caches the whole reply through `Index('stationID')`. Market bid lookup and nearest faction-warfare station lookup also consume this directory. Returning one station makes other stations disappear from those consumers, even though Eden can travel and dock there.

The route now returns all pinned catalog stations in stable ID order, using the existing native Rowset descriptor. Each row carries the station's type, formatted name, solar system, constellation, region, owner/corporation, operation, orbit and coordinates. It is independent of the selected pilot's position and station. `evestations/data.pyj` `get_station_service_masks` assigns `2**index` to **sorted service IDs**; `get_station_service_mask` ORs the bits belonging to the station operation's services. The adapter follows this exact rule, including gaps and repeated service IDs. Missing/empty operation services produce zero. It does not assume service IDs are bit positions. These are catalog capabilities; reporting a service does not implement its missing gameplay.

Server loads typed directory entries and service IDs from SQLite once, through a singleton lazy snapshot. Protocol owns service-bit encoding and the native descriptor. No world data or schema changes are needed; world version remains **41**. Selection gating is retained, and arguments/keywords are rejected. The installed catalog is treated as immutable until the server restarts.

References: unchanged build-3662168 archive hash and Carbon Blue revision recorded in the preceding route milestones. Inspection was inert; no gameplay Python executed. The existing Rowset/tuple adapters are unchanged.

**Automated validation.** Development filter `FullyQualifiedName~StationDirectoryTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **9 tests** in approximately **2 seconds**. Coverage includes remote-station rows, every native column, total/unique station counts, selection and request validation, docked/in-space callers, server-provider reopen, gapped service IDs, duplicate services, empty services and signed-mask boundaries. Initial development corrected missing test/model imports and escaped JSON in fixture SQL before the passing run. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~StationDirectoryTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotServicesTests|FullyQualifiedName~TypedCallResultTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **41 tests** in approximately **6 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python policy suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed; final build/test/policy/format checks took approximately **19 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite was run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Inspect information for a remote station and check station/service map filters. Native rendering and interactions remain unverified.

### Personal bookmarks and saved-location warp

Replaced the fixed empty `PersonalBookmarkState` reply with saved personal folders, subfolders and locations. Inert inspection of build **3662168** `eve/client/script/ui/shared/bookmarks/bookmarkSvc.pyj` confirms `GetMyActiveBookmarks()` returns **(folders, bookmarks, subfolders)**. It caches each entry by its named ID. When no folders exist, the client calls `AddFolder(True, localizedName, '', None, None, None, None)` to create its default personal folder. Eden now saves that folder rather than fabricating a constant ID on every login. Folder replies include personal access level **1**, ownership, active state and empty ACL group references.

Implemented `AddFolder`, `UpdateFolder`, `DeleteFolder`, `GetFolderInfo`, `AddToKnownFolders`, `RemoveFromKnownFolders`, `UpdateKnownFolderState`, `SearchFoldersWithAdminAccess`, `CreateSubfolder`, `UpdateSubfolder`, `DeleteSubfolder`, `BookmarkStaticLocation`, `UpdateBookmark`, `DeleteBookmarks`, `MoveBookmarksToFolderAndSubfolder` and `CopyBookmarksToFolderAndSubfolder` on `accessGroupBookmarkMgr`, plus `BookmarkLocation` on the bound `beyonce` park. Known inactive folders remain listed without loading their contents; forgotten folders can be rediscovered and re-added by their owner. Subfolder/folder deletion removes its contained locations in one transaction. Moves and copies validate the entire input before committing. Move replies contain a list and message-None; copy replies contain an **ID-keyed dictionary** and message-None, matching the native `itervalues()`/cache-update path. Creation returns the native eight-field tuple `(bookmarkID, itemID, typeID, x, y, z, locationID, expiryDate)`. Named bookmark rows retain memo, note, creator, folder, subfolder, coordinates, creation time, expiry and None flag.

Static bookmarks resolve stations, catalog celestials (including gates), solar systems, constellations and regions from the pinned catalog. In-flight bookmarks require an active ballpark. Saving the selected ship uses its authoritative checkpointed position; other mobile visible targets become saved coordinate points. Client-supplied coordinates are never accepted. Static locations retain their item/type identity and use existing celestial warp points. Coordinate bookmarks use native `CmdWarpToStuff('bookmark', id, minRange=...)`, which resolves the selected pilot's saved target and enters the existing alignment, tackle, warp, motion-sync and persistence path. Point warp orders use a separate negative order ID to distinguish repeated requests from item warps. The player must already be in the bookmarked solar system; region/constellation/system destinations are map destinations, not warp targets. Deleted, expired, inactive and foreign bookmarks cannot initiate warp. Existing distance/range restrictions and landing clearance remain in force.

`appConst.pyj` defines **100-character names**, **3900-character descriptions**, **3000 locations per personal folder**, **30 created/known folders**, and **five active personal folders**. `bookmarkConst.pyj` defines expiry choices **0 = never, 1 = four hours, 2 = two days, 3 = 24 hours**. Expiry is computed from the host clock and filters reads, edits and warp at the exact deadline. Cancelling expiry persists; expired entries cannot be revived by an edit. Expired capacity is reclaimed when writing into a folder. `BookmarkLocationWindow` initializes `expiryCancel` to integer **0** and later sends the checkbox's boolean; the adapter accepts both forms. The client updates local caches after successful RPCs and `RefreshACLBookmarks` calls `RemoveExpiredBookmarks`, so personal edits and expiry do not need duplicate server notifications.

World version **42** adds `WorldBookmarkFolder`, `WorldBookmarkSubfolder` and `WorldBookmark`, with global generated IDs, owner/creator foreign keys and a composite subfolder/folder reference. All mutations run in a transaction. Version-41 migration retains contacts, settings and existing pilot progress. Every read and mutation checks selected-pilot ownership; mixed-owner batches, invalid subfolders, malformed requests, invalid text/expiry/positions, overfull folders and activation over the limit reject with unchanged state. Shared access-group folders are rejected explicitly. Probe/scan-result bookmarking awaits scanning; no temporary scan results are invented.

References: inert inspection of unchanged build-3662168 `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, the native bookmark service/window and `appConst`/`bookmarkConst`. Consulted [Carbon Destiny Ballpark.cpp at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/src/Ballpark.cpp), `Ballpark::WarpTo`'s coordinate target and finite-value handling. Eden reuses its established warp timing/landing policy and existing Blue list, set, KeyVal and tuple adapters; no codec changes or gameplay Python execution occurred.

**Automated validation.** Development filter `FullyQualifiedName~Bookmark|FullyQualifiedName~FlightSessionTests|FullyQualifiedName~FlightBoundaryTests|FullyQualifiedName~TypedFlightTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **64 tests** in approximately **10 seconds**. Tests cover real SQLite and router/wire shapes, Unicode, exact text limits, private ownership/unchanged-state rejections, batch atomicity, copy identity, folder limits, 3000-location capacity, exact expiry/cancellation boundaries, migration/reopen, repeated warp requests, simulated point and static arrival, saved position, and rejected foreign/inactive/expired/remote targets. Initial development corrected test-side set encoding to use the existing inert `__builtin__.set` adapter. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~Bookmark|FullyQualifiedName~FlightSessionTests|FullyQualifiedName~FlightBoundaryTests|FullyQualifiedName~TypedFlightTests|FullyQualifiedName~TackleTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **158 tests** in approximately **22 seconds**, including the integer expiry-cancel regression. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python policy suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed. Final build/test/policy/format checks took approximately **36 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite is authorized or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

### Personal ship insurance

Replaced insurance compatibility replies with catalog quotes, durable personal policies and wallet settlement. Build **3662168** inert inspection confirms `GetInsurancePrice(typeID)`, `GetInsurancePrices(typeIDs)`, `GetContracts(isCorp=False)`, `GetContractForShip(shipID)`, `InsureShip(shipID, amount, isCorp)` and `UnInsureShip(shipID)`. Native station binding uses the three-field `(stationID, stationID, locationGroup=15)` tuple; mismatched locations and in-space binding are rejected. Station service **21** is required for purchase and cancellation. Native replacement first returns `InsureShipFailedSingleContract` with `ownerName`; the client confirms a second purchase with keyword `voidOld=True`.

`insuranceConst` defines six paid coverages, **50–100%**, with premiums **5–30%** of the quote and a free **40%** baseline. Eden policies last **84 days**. Only owned assembled hulls in the caller's station hangar qualify; carried hulls, other owners, invalid amounts, missing service and insufficient funds fail without mutation. Replacement charges a new full premium without refunding the old one; cancellation has no refund. Contracts expire at their exact end timestamp and do not appear in active queries afterward. Corporation purchase is explicitly unavailable.

**Valuation policy:** Eden uses pinned `client/types.basePrice`, separately from testing-market offers. Missing/zero values, values below 1 ISK and capsules yield zero and cannot be insured. This is a local reproducible policy: [CCP's insurance description](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/the-circle-of-life) uses periodically updated manufacturing-material prices and hull multipliers; Eden has no equivalent live index. In particular, pinned corvettes with zero base price have no default payout. Prices and premiums use integer cents with midpoint-to-even rounding.

World version **43** adds policy history and an insurance ledger. Purchase, debit and journal entry commit together. Fatal NPC damage settles active coverage or the free baseline inside the same transaction as hull destruction, wreck/drop creation and the capsule transition. The payout uses the saved policy valuation when covered. A unique per-hull payout key prevents duplicate credits; failed ledger writes roll back the entire loss. Removing an unpiloted/carried hull closes its policy without a payout. Hull identities and type IDs remain in history after deletion; reopening retains policies and wallet entries. Client balance notifications follow committed payments. Wallet entry type **19** uses Pend Insurance **1000113**, negative ship references for premiums and positive hull-type references for payouts, as required by native `FormatReference`.

References: unchanged pinned archive hash and Carbon Blue revision recorded above; existing KeyVal/list/numeric adapters are reused without codec changes. Native insurance service, insurance constants, moniker binding and wallet formatting were inspected as inert code objects. No gameplay Python executed or client launched. Insurance mail, corporation policies, structures and a retail valuation index remain pending.

**Automated validation.** Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~Insurance|FullyQualifiedName~ShipStorageTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcPursuitTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcOrbitTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcAggressionTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Market|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Repair.RepairTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **215 passed**, approximately **48 seconds**. Coverage includes all six packages, catalog hull variants, native calls/binding and wallet references, replacement/cancellation/expiry, rejection with unchanged state, concurrent purchase and loss settlement, exact expiry, rollback and restart/migration persistence. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python policy suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **69.50 seconds** combined. Early development failures identified no-tracking fixture edits, shield recharge at the 84-day expiry boundary and an obsolete station binding fixture; these were corrected before passing runs. Final client inspection also corrected premium/payout journal reference signs and retained hull type after deletion. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** At an insurance station, buy/replace/cancel a policy on a nonzero-value hull and reconnect to inspect it. Check the wallet premium description and payout description after an NPC loss. Native rendering and interactions remain unverified.

### Persistent task tracking

Build **3662168** `monolith_task_tracker/client/monolithTaskTrackerSvc.pyj` calls `GetCompletedAchievementsAndClientEventCount()` and reads dictionaries named `completedDict` (numeric task IDs to FILETIME timestamps) and `eventDict` (client event names to cumulative counts). `UpdateClientAchievmentsAndCounters(completedDict, dict(stats))` sends the whole snapshot, including at shutdown; the misspelled RPC name is intentional. Eden now saves these per selected pilot instead of returning permanent empty dictionaries and acknowledging discarded writes. Repeated or stale snapshots take the maximum counter value without adding counts twice. The first accepted completion timestamp remains stable.

The pinned catalog supplies **68 task definitions** and **131 event definitions**, including disabled tasks, thresholds and multi-condition tasks. Completion requires every condition on an enabled definition; empty conditions do not auto-complete. Only catalog events prefixed `client_` may be updated by the client. New reported client completions must satisfy their conditions, and timestamp values must be positive and no later than server time. Unknown tasks/events, server-counter writes, unearned completions, invalid types, negative counts, duplicate entries, oversized batches, extra arguments and keywords fail atomically. The client may echo previously fetched server completions, but cannot create them or rewrite their timestamps. Missing entries never erase existing progress. Concurrent snapshots retain the highest cumulative count; server increments saturate at signed 64-bit maximum.

Server-owned activities now record progress in the same transaction as the successful operation: actual warp entry, stargate transfer, docking/undocking, market buy/sell executions, mining activation and successful yield, fitting a new module and its high/medium/low rack, boarding/recovery, skill injection and training starts, manufacturing install/delivery, ore reprocessing, contact creation, NPC destruction and pilot hull loss. Failed actions and no-op retries do not earn duplicate events. Warp orders and alignment alone do not set `HasEverWarped`; authoritative simulation records the first transition into warp, including when another session advances the shared system. `eve/client/script/ui/camera/enterSpaceCamera.pyj` `_OnActivated` uses this boolean to choose between `AnimEnterFirstTimeEverInSpace` and normal `AnimEnter`. History begins when this upgrade is installed; earlier activity is not reconstructed from ambiguous positions.

World version **44** adds pilot-keyed `WorldTaskCounter` and `WorldCompletedTask`; version-43 upgrade preserves contacts, insurance and other saved state. Reads return completed tasks plus client counters, while server counters remain authoritative. Records survive reconnect/reopen. There is no new reward grant or client notification protocol. Server completions are available through the next status fetch. The pinned legacy service's `IsEnabled()` itself returns **False**; server persistence does not re-enable its old achievement interface. Agent missions, player-corporation operations, probing, wormholes and other missing gameplay do not earn server activities merely because a corresponding catalog task exists.

References: unchanged pinned archive SHA-256 recorded above. Inspected the tracker, loader, stats tracker, event constants and entry camera as inert code objects. Consulted [Carbon Blue dictionary decoding at fcbf0c2](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae/src/Marshal.cpp); existing numeric-key and string-key dictionary adapters are reused. No codec changes, gameplay Python execution or client launch occurred.

**Automated validation.** Development checks cover native router/wire shapes, selection gating, cumulative and stale updates, private pilots, concurrent writes, threshold/multi-condition/disabled tasks, repeated warp entry and cancellation, gameplay event counts, rejected requests with unchanged state, forced completion-write rollback and version-43 migration/restart persistence. An early native fixture expected the wrong decoded dictionary type. Added event assertions caught fitting stack-remainder notifications being counted as new fitted modules; the event filter now requires a module entering an actual fitted slot. A subsequent focused development run passed **257 tests** in approximately **44 seconds**, followed by correction-focused checks.

Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~TaskTracking|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Flight|FullyQualifiedName~PilotContactsTests|FullyQualifiedName~MarketTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Mining.MiningTests|FullyQualifiedName~IndustryTests|FullyQualifiedName~FittingTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotFleetTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotTrainingTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotSkillBookTests|FullyQualifiedName~ReprocessingTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~MissileCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~Insurance|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` selected **384 cases**: **383 passed**, one assertion failed, in approximately **65 seconds**. The new competing-kill assertion incorrectly assumed the first pilot would win; it now checks the actual winner and no credit for the loser. The validation sequence stopped before Python/format checks when this failed. Only that test assertion changed afterward. The affected filter `FullyQualifiedName~PilotCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~TaskTracking` then passed **33 tests** in approximately **13 seconds**. Both filters used `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`. Tool/solution restores and builds passed with zero warnings/errors; after the correction, build, affected tests, both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **31.93 seconds** combined. The other 383 passing cases were not repeated after this test-only correction. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** After a pilot's first real warp, reconnect and check normal space-entry camera behavior. The pinned legacy achievement UI is disabled; no task-window acceptance is claimed.

### Persistent home stations and docked clone transfer

The old `home_station.get_home_station()` compatibility reply used `DockedPilot.StationId`, which describes the ship's current/last docked station. Travelling therefore changed the reported medical home without an explicit selection. Eden now keeps the medical home separately from the active ship's docking reference. New characters save their initial school spawn as home. Version-44 saves have no historical medical-home record, so the upgrade captures each pilot's active ship's last station once; later travel/reopen never rewrites it.

Build **3662168** `homestation/client/service.pyj` confirms `get_home_station()` returns KeyVal fields `id`, `type_id`, `solar_system_id` and `is_fallback`. `get_home_station_candidates()` returns a list with `id`, `type_id`, `solar_system_id`, `is_current_station`, `is_school_hq` and numeric `errors`. `set_home_station(stationID, allowRemote)` takes two positional arguments. A successful change sends `OnHomeStationChanged(newID)`, whose native listener clears cached home/candidate data. Eden also sends the wallet balance notification after the committed fee. Same-home requests fail without a second debit. An invalid saved destination falls back to the school HQ with `is_fallback=True`; selecting a valid destination repairs the saved choice.

For current NPC-corporation membership, candidates are the docked NPC station and the school corporation's pinned **headquarters**, which is distinct from the AIR starting station. The school HQ remains eligible from space. `homestation/validation.pyj` treats both current-station and school-HQ changes as exemptions from remote-office cooldown. Thus `get_next_remote_change_time()` correctly returns None for these pilots; `allowRemote=True` does not authorize arbitrary stations. Corporation offices, standings-based faction access and structure homes await their corresponding gameplay. Wormhole systems and Pochven are rejected with the pinned candidate codes **1** and **6**; an already-selected home has code **2**. RPC races and malformed requests fail with a readable validation error.

Home relocation costs **100,000 ISK**, the fee established by [CCP's Rhea clone changes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/a-new-era-of-clones). Eden applies this fixed fee to supported home selections and credits no station wallet. A persistent **Clone Transfer / entry 52** debit identifies the destination station and its operator; the wallet and home commit with that journal entry. This is Eden's fixed fee policy rather than a dynamic retail pricing feed. Insufficient funds, unknown/remote targets, duplicate requests and failed ledger writes leave home and wallet unchanged. The native UI's school lookup also needs `corpRegistry.GetCorporation()` for the caller's NPC corporation and `corpmgr.GetCorporations(corporationID)` for another school corporation. Both now return a native DBRow containing pinned NPC identity, ticker, headquarters, system, faction, CEO, tax and share metadata. These read operations do not implement player-corporation management.

`self_destruct_clone()` is a session-changing RPC with no arguments. Pinned client validation requires a **docked capsule away from its home**. Eden verifies that condition against saved state, creates one standard Capsule **670**, switches the active ship and destroys the retired capsule inside one transaction. The replacement is docked at the saved home; other hangar assets, wallet and skill points are retained. Capsule loss records task progress but pays no insurance, creates no combat wreck and grants no recovery kit. A dedicated transfer audit retains old/new capsule identities, stations and time. The expected old ship and unique retired-capsule key prevent competing/repeated calls from transferring twice. Session handling releases old location bindings, sends retired/new capsule inventory changes and the updated brain before the native ship/station session delta, then replies None. Space self-destruction, hostile capsule combat and implants remain separate work; personal jump clones are implemented below.

World version **45** adds `WorldHomeStation`, `WorldHomeStationPayment` and `WorldHomeCloneTransfer`. Pilot foreign keys and transactional creation preserve existing saves. The retired hull identity has no ship foreign key. References: unchanged pinned archive SHA-256 recorded above; inert inspection covered the home service/types/candidates/validation, school lookup, corporation row loader and `appConst.refCloneTransfer=52`. Consulted Carbon Blue **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae** `Marshal.cpp` and `PyRowSet.cpp` for existing dictionaries and DBRow forms. No codec changes, gameplay Python execution or client launch occurred.

**Automated validation.** Development coverage includes home stability through real docking/travel, school HQ versus current station, payment/journal/notification fields, selection gating, malformed/forged requests, regional error codes, no-funds rejection, concurrent changes and capsule transfer, forced payment/transfer rollback, original assets/skills, fallback repair, old binding invalidation, session-packet ordering, migration and restart persistence. Initial fixtures lacked capsule volume and assumed the wrong starting skill points; both were corrected before passing tests. The focused neighbor run passed **137 tests** in approximately **16 seconds**; the new native transfer and additional transaction cases passed **14 tests** in approximately **4 seconds**. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~HomeStation|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterCreation|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Market|FullyQualifiedName~PilotFleetTests|FullyQualifiedName~ShipStorageTests|FullyQualifiedName~Insurance|FullyQualifiedName~TaskTracking|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **213 passed**, approximately **45 seconds**. ### Catalog agent directory and saved agents

Replaced the permanent empty `agentMgr.GetAgents()` rowset with the complete live agent catalog. Inert inspection of build **3662168** `eve/client/script/ui/station/agents/agents.pyj` `__GetAllAgents` confirms the client clones the rowset, indexes by `agentID` and filters by `stationID`, **`solarsystemID`** (lowercase s), `corporationID`, `factionID` and `agentTypeID`. Eden preserves those columns plus division, level and locator status. The legacy `quality` column uses **20** as the retired payout-quality value; [CCP's Incursion 1.5 notes](https://www.eveonline.com/ja/news/view/patch-notes-for-incursion-1.5-1) removed varying agent quality in 2011. This column does not implement mission payout or access rules.

`client/npccharacters` is authoritative for active status and agent metadata. It contains **10,966 active agents** across all **12 active agent categories**, plus **427 other active NPC characters**. Deleted entries and NonAgent category **1** are excluded from the agent directory. Station/system mappings come from pinned stations, factions from pinned NPC corporations, and absent station/faction values retain None. Space metadata can supply a system where no catalog station resolves; existing station assignments are preserved. Returning an event agent in this directory does not spawn its encounter or offer missions.

NPC identities now resolve through `config.GetMultiOwnersEx`, including name IDs for client localization. English text comes from `client/localization_fsd_en-us`, with `sde/npcCharacters.name.en` as fallback. No NPC receives a fabricated name. All **11,393 active NPCs** in the installed pinned catalog have a valid name. NPC owner and public-name text uses explicit Unicode, preserving accents; player owner replies retain their existing wire representation. `charMgr.GetPublicInfo(NPC)` and `GetCharacterDescription(NPC)` return catalog profile/biography data. Public fields include corporation, race, bloodline, school, ancestry, gender and original UTC creation time when available.

The same native address-book service uses **`charMgr.AddContact(agentID)` with one argument** for an NPC agent, rather than the five-argument player-rating operation. `GetContactList().addresses` includes these IDs; its native consumer detects NPC characters and places them in its `agents` list. Eden saves these as private agent bookmarks with neutral relationship and no watchlist or label bits. `DeleteContacts([ids])` removes agents and player contacts in the same transaction, including mixed batches. Duplicate adds are idempotent, concurrent adds save once, and unknown/deleted/non-agent IDs are rejected. Player rating, label and blocking operations do not edit NPC standings. Saving an agent records the existing contact-added task event once. The client refreshes its own agent/address-book cache after successful edits, so no duplicate notification is sent.

World version **46** adds `WorldAgentContact(PilotId,AgentId)` with an owner foreign key; NPC identities belong to the separate catalog. Existing player-contact foreign keys and home/insurance/task state remain unchanged. Restart retains saved agents. The catalog directory is a singleton lazy snapshot with ID indexes, rebuilt on server restart. A full read-only snapshot check against the installed catalog verified **10,966 unique agents**, valid location/type metadata and **11,393 named NPCs**. The final development snapshot load took approximately **7.50 seconds**; later requests reuse it. Ordinary player owner/profile requests do not initialize this snapshot: pinned `idCheckers.IsNPCCharacter` and `inventorycommon.const` define the inclusive NPC range **3,000,000–3,999,999**. A focused regression proves that player requests do not parse even deliberately invalid NPC data.

References: unchanged pinned archive hash was rechecked; inert inspection covered agent rowset caching, NPC owner classification and the address-book add/delete/load branches. Consulted the existing Carbon Blue `Marshal.cpp` dictionary and rowset adapters at **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae**; no codec changes were needed. No gameplay Python was executed and no client was launched. Mission conversations/offers, journal progression, locator execution, earned NPC standings and static NPC-to-NPC standings remain unfinished routes/systems; a populated directory does not claim those features.

**Automated validation.** Development filter `FullyQualifiedName~AgentDirectory|FullyQualifiedName~Contacts|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSheet|FullyQualifiedName~OnlineStatus` passed **43 tests** in approximately **5 seconds**. Tests cover every active category, native fields and request validation, deleted/non-agent entries, localization/SDE fallback, nullable locations/factions, NPC/public owner profiles, scalar agent add, private persistence, concurrent/repeated adds, mixed deletion with forced rollback, version-45 migration and cold-path snapshot isolation. Early development corrected a record-list comparison assertion and byte-string header expectations; Unicode wire checking also exposed an incorrectly encoded accented fixture and led to explicit NPC Unicode replies. The separate full pinned-catalog check is read-only and does not open the world database. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~AgentDirectory|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~PilotContactsTests|FullyQualifiedName~CharacterSheet|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~StationGuestTests|FullyQualifiedName~TaskTracking|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **139 passed**, approximately **15 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, solution build (zero warnings/errors), both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **36.87 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

### Persistent personal jump clones

Replaced the permanent empty `jumpCloneSvc.GetCloneState()` with per-pilot installed bodies and the last cross-station jump time. `clones` remains a native rowset with `jumpCloneID`, `locationID` and Unicode `cloneName`; `implants` retains its empty `jumpCloneID`/`typeID` rowset because implant installation is not implemented. No clone receives fabricated implants. Names up to **100 characters** and body identities persist when jumping into and later leaving a body. Several bodies may share one NPC station, following [CCP's March 2021 change](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/quality-of-life-updates).

Inert inspection of the pinned `clonejumpsvc.pyj`, `jumpClonesPanel.pyj` and station `base.pyj` establishes these RPCs: `GetPriceForClone()`, `ValidateInstallJumpClone()`, `InstallCloneInStation()`, `SetJumpCloneName(cloneID, name)`, `DestroyInstalledClone(cloneID)` and `CloneJump(destinationLocationID, cloneID, quotedCost, confirmed)`. Preflight returns the pinned localization label or label/arguments tuple for missing skill/capacity; station availability is independently checked, and installation revalidates everything. Both installation and activation charge **900,000 ISK**, from `appConst.costJumpClone`. Native wallet journal types are installation **55** and activation **128**. Charges, balance, body changes, cooldown and capsule replacement commit together. Destroying an installed body never refunds its fee. Malformed arguments, foreign/active/deleted bodies, stale capsules, forged destinations or fees, inadequate funds and jumps in space fail without changing saved state.

Installation requires station service **24**, Jump Clone Facility. Capacity comes from earned Infomorph Psychology **24242**, Advanced **33407** and Elite **73910**, with ranks **1/5/12** from the catalog. Each trained effective level adds one body. Pinned race-specific Alpha grades cap Psychology at I and exclude the other clone skills. Alpha pilots can continue using bodies acquired as Omega, consistent with [CCP's Alpha facts](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/213811425-Alpha-Clones-and-Omega-Clone-lapsing-Quick-Facts). Capacity is checked for new installations rather than deleting existing bodies after a lapse. Character primes and native brain modifiers expose `maxJumpClones` **979** and `cloneJumpCoolDown` **1921** using the same effective skills as the server. Infomorph skill completion also sends character attribute updates to the existing godma cache, so the panel need not reconnect to observe its new cooldown or capacity.

Cross-station jumps require a capsule and a **24-hour** cooldown reduced by one hour per effective Infomorph Synchronizing **33399** level (catalog rank **2**). The exact deadline is allowed. Swapping within the current NPC station bypasses that check and neither clears nor restarts an existing cross-station timer, following [CCP's 20.07 patch](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-version-20-07) and the pinned panel's `validate_jump_to`. A body is left at departure even if that station lacks an installation facility; no capacity is consumed by the swap. Remote wormhole destinations other than **Thera 31000005** and Pochven are rejected, following [CCP's jump-clone restrictions](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203217202-Jump-Clones). Special Pochven standings/installation rules, Upwell services, capital vat bays, implants and their ownership/transfer rules remain unavailable.

The native client automatically calls `ship.LeaveShip(currentShipID)` when starting a jump from a hull. Eden now supports that docked operation, preserving the old ship, modules and cargo and selecting an existing local Capsule **670** or creating one. A clone jump retires only the departure capsule and selects a new capsule at the destination; it does not record a pod death, insurance payout or recovery grant. Home station and other assets stay in place. Actual home-clone self-destruction retires the active named body while preserving stored bodies and the jump timer. Old location bindings are released, and inventory changes, the brain, clone-cache invalidation and balance notifications precede the native session delta. Service bindings require the client's exact current-location/group tuple rather than None or an arbitrary location.

World version **47** adds `WorldJumpClone`, `WorldJumpCloneTimer` and `WorldJumpClonePayment`. Pilot foreign keys, an active-body uniqueness index and a location/active check constrain saved bodies. Payment audit identities survive body/capsule deletion. Version-46 upgrade and repeated opening preserve existing ships, skills, homes and balances. The pinned archive is unchanged from the hash above. Consulted Carbon Blue **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae** `Marshal.cpp` tuple/list handling and `PyRowSet.cpp` descriptor conventions; existing explicit Blue adapters suffice. No game launch or gameplay Python execution occurred.

**Automated validation.** The 23 new focused cases cover wire schemas and selection gating, localized preflight shapes, Unicode names, journal entries, binding validation and session packet ordering; catalog ranks and Alpha/Omega limits; several bodies per station; repeated cross-station cycles at exact timer boundaries; same-station bypass without timer reset; lapse behavior; private ownership, stale/repeated requests, funds, facility and space rejection; concurrent installation; forced fee/swap rollback; ship/cargo preservation, active-body retirement, version-46 migration and restart persistence. Early development corrected catalog-fixture tracking, byte-label assertions and test travel positions outside docking range. The affected remap test was updated to assert the two newly exposed clone attributes alongside the five remappable values. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~JumpClone|FullyQualifiedName~PilotFleetTests|FullyQualifiedName~HomeStation|FullyQualifiedName~PilotBrainTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotTrainingTests|FullyQualifiedName~IndustryTests|FullyQualifiedName~MiningTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~Dogma|FullyQualifiedName~TypedServiceModelsTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Market|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **200 passed**, approximately **31 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, build (zero warnings/errors), both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **52.06 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

### Persistent personal calendar

Replaced the always-empty `calendarProxy.GetEventList(month, year)` with saved personal events visible only to their creator and invited local pilots. The native result is a **list of result groups**, each containing native `blue.DBRow` records; a flat event list is incorrect. Event rows preserve `eventID`, `ownerID`, `eventDateTime`, nullable `eventDuration`, Unicode `eventTitle`, `importance`, `isDeleted` and `dateModified`. `GetEventDetails(eventID, ownerID)` returns `creatorID` and Unicode `eventText`. The owner argument is checked against saved ownership and never grants access. Cancelled events remain marked as cancelled in history; they do not become upcoming live events.

Pinned build **3662168** inert inspection covered `eve/client/script/ui/services/eveCalendarsvc.pyj`, `eve/client/script/ui/shared/eveCalendar.pyj`, `eve/client/script/util/eveMisc.pyj` and `appConst`. The creator uses `calendarMgr.CreatePersonalEvent(dateTime, duration, title, description, important, invitees)` and receives a new event ID. `EditPersonalEvent(eventID, oldDateTime, dateTime, duration, title, description, important)`, `UpdateEventParticipants(eventID, add, remove)`, `SendEventResponse(eventID, ownerID, response)` and `DeleteEvent(eventID, ownerID)` return None. Only the creator can edit, change invitations or cancel. A stale old date prevents a competing reschedule from overwriting the first change. Repeated cancellation and unchanged invitation edits are idempotent.

`GetResponsesForCharacter()` returns `eventID`/`status` rows, including the creator's implicit accepted status. `GetResponsesToEvent(eventID, ownerID)` returns invitee `characterID`/`status` rows after the same visibility check. Pinned statuses are **Uninvited 0**, **Deleted 1**, **Declined 2**, **Undecided 3**, **Accepted 4**, **Maybe 5**. New invitees start undecided. Only invitees can submit accepted, declined or tentative replies. Moving an event resets accepted and tentative replies to undecided while preserving declined replies, matching the native `OnEditCalendarEvent` branch; changing text alone preserves replies. Removing a guest revokes event, details, attendee and reply access; re-invitation starts with a new undecided reply. Replies are saved immediately. Native response calls update their own cache; reopening the uncached attendee query reads current replies rather than forcibly closing other event windows.

The server enforces the pinned **40-character title**, **500-character description**, **50 other invitees**, and **12 calendar months ahead** limits. Duration is None or the native selector's **1–24 whole hours**, stored as minutes. Month queries use UTC month boundaries and preserve history from the client's calendar start year **2003**. New/edited events cannot be moved into the past; the current minute is allowed because the native editor uses minute precision. Unknown, duplicate, self and blocked invitations, unauthorized changes, malformed fields, stale dates and invalid response statuses fail without partial writes. Personal invitations currently have **no configured CSPA charge**. The native CSPA wrapper first tries the ordinary create/update call; it does not require a separate fee query. Only an optional zero `approvedCost` is accepted. Paid communication settings, corporation/alliance event permissions, official CCP events and automated structure events are separate future work.

Create, edit, cancellation and invitation membership changes commit together with a private, ordered change feed. Connected recipients receive native `OnNewCalendarEvent`, `OnEditCalendarEvent` and `OnRemoveCalendarEvent` argument shapes, including the prior date for a move and the cancelled-versus-uninvited removal flag. A connection starts at its current feed cursor; reconnects fetch saved event state without replaying old notifications. Tick delivery drains at most **256 changes** at a time and continues when a batch remains even if no new write occurs. A process-local revision signal avoids SQLite polling on unchanged idle ticks, and publishes only after commit. Pilot logoff resets the observer. No email, chat message or external calendar is sent.

World version **48** adds `WorldCalendarEvent`, `WorldCalendarParticipant` and `WorldCalendarChange`, with pilot/event foreign keys and event-time, membership and per-recipient feed indexes. Version-47 migration is additive. Calendar rules and immutable commands/results live in Gameplay; Server owns transactional persistence and Protocol owns Blue adapters and notifications. Consulted Carbon Blue **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae** `Marshal.cpp` tuple/list reductions and `PyRowSet.cpp` `DBRow::_New`/`InitSeq` for the explicit descriptor/value-sequence constructor. Eden's inbound decoder intentionally refuses executable DBRow reductions; tests inspect the outgoing constructor and encode it, then round-trip each inert data cell. No codec allowlist was expanded. No game launch or gameplay Python execution occurred.

**Automated validation.** The 19 focused development cases passed in approximately **4 seconds**. Coverage includes selected-pilot routing, native grouped rows/details/response fields, Unicode and None, private/offline access, calendar bounds and leap-month ranges, invitation limits and blocks, creator authority, reply persistence and reschedule resets, cancellation history, revoked/reinvited access, no-op updates, concurrent stale-date rejection, forced create/edit/invite/cancel/reply rollback, version-47 migration/restart, connected peer notification order, reconnect initialization and delivery beyond one tick batch. Development corrected an enum member name, assertion analyzer requirements and an outbound-constructor test that incorrectly used the inbound reduction decoder. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~Calendar|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~StationGuestTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotContactsTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **118 passed**, approximately **15 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, build (zero warnings/errors), both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **34.64 seconds** combined. The pinned archive SHA-256 was rechecked and matches the recorded build. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Create and open a personal event, invite another pilot, and inspect the participant response after reopening. Observe rescheduling, cancellation and removal in a second client, then reconnect. Native calendar rendering, editor interaction and notification presentation remain unverified.

### Persistent notification inbox and contact messages

Replaced the always-empty `notificationMgr.GetAllNotifications` route with a private saved inbox. Native history calls use **`GetAllNotifications(fromID=...)`**: pinned `NotificationUIService._NotificationProvider` supplies `lastClearedNotificationId`, so this is an **exclusive newer-than cutoff**, not a request for older pages. None and zero fetch all visible history. Other reads are `GetUnprocessed()` and `GetByGroupID(groupID)`. Replies are lists of explicit KeyVal DTOs with `notificationID`, `typeID`, `senderID`, `receiverID`, Boolean `processed`, FILETIME `created` and typed `data`.

The native write calls `MarkAsProcessed([ids])`, `MarkAllAsProcessed()`, `MarkGroupAsProcessed(groupID)`, `DeleteNotifications([ids])`, `DeleteAllNotifications()` and `DeleteGroupNotifications(groupID)` return None. The pinned selected-ID limit is **100**, from `appConst.notificationsMaxUpdated`; group/all operations are not restricted to that batch size. Each selected batch validates all IDs against the recipient before changing anything. Duplicate owned IDs and repeated read/deletion operations are idempotent. Deletion retains a tombstone, so IDs and delivery cursors do not reset after clearing an inbox. Reads and writes never accept a caller-supplied recipient. Group **0** means unread; contact notifications belong to group **9**. Other valid pinned groups return empty until their gameplay has a producer; unassigned group **13** and malformed IDs are rejected.

The first server producers are the contact form's optional **Send Notification** add/edit actions. `notificationTypeContactAdd` is **89** and `notificationTypeContactEdit` is **90**. Inert inspection of `contactManagementWindow.pyj` establishes the **120-character** single-line message limit. `contactFormatters.pyj` consumes the known `level` and `message` payload. Both history DTOs and live events carry an explicit dictionary with the standing snapshot and Unicode message. Pinned `TryUnYamlNotificationData` only parses string payloads and accepts an already-decoded mapping, so no YAML parser or executable serializer is introduced. Empty messages are valid. Silent add/edit calls still create no notification. NPC agent bookmarks do not emit personal-player notifications.

Contact changes, the contact-added task event and the recipient's notification commit together. A blocked sender/recipient pair, invalid text, failed inbox write, unknown pilot or rejected contact operation leaves all three unchanged. Both existing standing and watchlist flags are retained on a failed edit. The contact remains private to its owner; sending a notification does not add a reciprocal contact. The message is a saved snapshot, so subsequent rating changes do not rewrite earlier notifications. Paid CSPA settings are not configured or implemented, and these sends do not charge ISK.

Connected recipients receive `OnNotificationReceived(notificationID, typeID, senderID, created, data)`. A per-connection observer starts at the current durable ID on pilot selection, resets at logoff and drains at most **256 rows** per tick. Tombstones and already-read rows advance that cursor without being reannounced as unread. A history/unread/group fetch that wins the race with delivery records only its returned IDs, preventing a duplicate push without dropping notifications outside the fetched group. Remaining batches drain without requiring another publication. A process-local revision signal publishes only after a successful contact/notification commit and avoids SQLite polling on unchanged idle ticks. The native read/delete methods update their own client caches, so the server does not send duplicate deletion events. Offline recipients fetch saved history after reconnecting.

Calendar reminders are client-generated type **1050**, as shown by the pinned calendar service and formatter. This milestone does not fabricate server invitation notifications or duplicate those reminders. Other server notification families, paid communication rules and back-office undelete behavior remain separate work. The inbox has no client-authorized create/send endpoint.

World version **49** adds `WorldInboxNotification`, with a recipient foreign key and recipient/ID index. Sender identities and payload snapshots remain audit data; version-48 upgrade is additive. Gameplay owns the immutable notification/query/content models and validation, Server owns persistence inside the existing contact transaction, and Protocol owns the selected handler, native adapters and observer. Inert inspection covered `notificationSvc.pyj`, `notifications/common/notification.pyj`, `notificationconst.pyj`, contact UI/formatters and notification UI history loading. The pinned archive hash was rechecked and matches the value above. Consulted Carbon Blue **fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae** dictionary/Unicode handling in `Marshal.cpp`; existing adapters suffice. No game launch, gameplay Python execution or external message delivery occurred.

**Automated validation.** The focused persistence run passed **11 cases** in approximately **3 seconds**, and the native inbox/contact run passed **6 cases** in approximately **3 seconds**. Coverage includes native DTO/event fields, Unicode and empty messages, selection gating, cutoff direction, processed/unread/group state, private and mixed-ID rejection, repeat operations, message and batch bounds, blocked pairs, silent writes, contact/task/inbox rollback, failed batch/group mutation, competing contact adds, restart/tombstone retention, version-48 migration, fetch-versus-push races and delivery beyond a tick batch. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~Inbox|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotContactsTests|FullyQualifiedName~AgentDirectory|FullyQualifiedName~TaskTracking|FullyQualifiedName~Calendar|FullyQualifiedName~StationGuestTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` and `-- xUnit.MaxParallelThreads=4`: **167 passed**, approximately **17 seconds**. Tool/solution restores, build (zero warnings/errors), both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting tests), formatting and diff checks passed in **39.06 seconds** combined. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Send a contact add/edit message to another pilot and inspect its inbox entry and live indicator. Mark/delete notifications and reconnect to inspect saved state. Native mail-window rendering, message formatting and interaction remain unverified.

### Personal contract asset preparation

Implemented four previously unhandled preparation routes on `contractProxy`. Inert inspection of build **3662168** `contracts.pyj`, `createContractWnd.pyj` and `contractentry.pyj` confirms the calls `GetItemsInDockableLocation(stationID, forCorp)`, `GetItemsInContainer(stationID, itemID, forCorp, flag)`, `GetNumItemsInContainers(stationID, itemIDs, forCorp, flag)` and `SplitStack(stationID, itemID, quantity, forCorp, flag)`. The creation window consumes standard inventory rows, including virtual `singleton` and `stacksize`; container counts are an **integer-keyed dictionary**, and a truthy split reply triggers `OnItemSplit` and refresh. The handler uses the existing nine-column inventory DBRow adapter and returns Boolean True after a committed split. No codec change is needed.

Station reads contain owned personal hangar items and inactive assembled hulls. They exclude installed manufacturing blueprints, active ships, hulls in space and carried hulls. A selected pilot may inspect or split a remote personal station hangar, including from space. Container previews validate the root against the same station selection and traverse owned nested contents, retaining original item IDs, location IDs and flags. This includes fitted modules, loaded charges and carried ships; the count measures rows rather than summed stack quantities. Ordinary packaged items have zero contents. Reads use a world transaction so root validation and nested traversal see one snapshot. Foreign, missing and non-root selections fail rather than exposing another inventory. Corporation asset requests and non-personal-hangar flags are explicitly rejected.

`ContractAssetRules` owns the pinned `contractscommon.MAX_NUM_ITEMS` selection limit of **500**, distinct positive IDs and two-positive-part packaged-stack rules. Splitting requires an owned, available, offline, non-singleton stack directly in the specified hangar. It rejects installed items and items with nested contents, preserves per-stack damage metadata, allocates a durable inventory identity and commits the quantity reduction and separate new stack together. It deliberately does not merge the separated portion. Native `OnItemChange` notifications carry the original quantity for the old stack and an entering-container change for the new stack; blueprint splits invalidate the blueprint cache once. Existing world version **49** and inventory tables suffice; no schema migration or runtime database edit is needed.

This was the preparation milestone only. Its then-pending item-exchange lifecycle and compatibility replies are implemented in the following milestone, followed by courier and auction contracts below. Bulk creation and corporation/structure contracts remain pending. These four preparation routes alone did not establish a usable client contract workflow.

### Personal item-exchange contracts

Implemented `contractProxy.CreateContract`, `GetContract`, `AcceptContract`, `CompleteContract` with rejected status, `DeleteContract`, `DeleteNotification`, `GetLoginInfo`, `GetMyContractEscrow`, `CollectMyPageInfo`, `NumOutstandingContracts`, `GetContractListForOwner`, `GetMyExpiredContractList`, `GetMyCurrentContractList`, `GetMyBids` and `SearchContracts`. Empty contract login and escrow compatibility responses now belong to the persistent feature handler. `corpmgr.GetCorporationIDForCharacter` resolves saved local pilots and catalog NPC characters; the native creation window uses it to determine which contract limit applies to a private assignee. NPC-corporation pilots do not receive the player-corporation limit exemption.

Build **3662168** sends a new-style `contracts.contractStruct.ContractStruct`, with exactly 17 fields: contract type, private flag, assignee, expiry minutes, duration, start station, destination, price, reward, collateral, title, description, offered item/quantity pairs, hangar division, requested type/quantity pairs, corporation flag and bulk flag. The wire adapter validates that inert state before invoking typed gameplay operations. It preserves native list-of-list item pairs and Unicode text; unexpected ownership fields, incomplete state and unsupported modes fail without mutation. The Blue decoder permits this exact class as inert data, using its existing NEWOBJ parser; it does not invoke a Python class, constructor or reduction. Private item exchange is type **1**; outstanding/finished/cancelled/rejected/deleted statuses are **0/4/5/6/8**. Offers accept private assignments to other local Eden pilots or public availability at catalog NPC stations. Remote creation and acceptance deliver into the specified station hangar; docking at that station is not required.

Gameplay validation bounds titles/descriptions to **50/4000** characters, selections to **500** offered-item/requested-type entries with distinct IDs per list, and the complete escrow graph to **2000** rows. Expiry options are **1, 3, 7, 14 and 28 days**; acceptance fails at the exact deadline. Requested items must be available packaged, offline stacks in the acceptor's personal hangar at the start station, and may be supplied from several stacks. Pinned `CanRequestType` excludes General, Frozen, Livestock, Miscellaneous and Commodities groups. Active/foreign ships, installed manufacturing blueprints, invalid locations, wrong quantities, duplicate requests, unknown assignees and foreign nested contents fail atomically. At this milestone, courier/auction/corporation, structure and bulk creation requests rejected explicitly; personal courier support follows below. The current inventory model represents unresearched original blueprints; research and blueprint copies remain pending.

World version **50** adds `WorldContract`, `WorldContractItem`, `WorldContractEscrow`, `WorldContractPayment` and `WorldContractChange`, with persistent identity, history, participant references, per-pilot journal/feed indexes and snapshot payloads for inventory changes. Escrow markers hide held items and ships through the owning world's EF query filters, covering ordinary inventory, fitting, market, industry, fleet and storage queries. The actual item/ship records remain intact, including fitted modules, loaded charges, nested containers, carried hulls, online flags, crystal damage and ship health. A partial offer gets a separate durable stack; whole items and hulls retain their IDs. Contract operations alone explicitly read escrow. Historical migrations create these tables before any item/ship query uses their filters. Fresh creation and version-49 upgrades preserve prior pilot state.

Acceptance validates and transfers requested goods, changes ownership of every held asset, credits/debits wallets, refunds the successful public deposit, charges the quoted sales tax, records journal entries, finishes the contract and releases escrow in one SQLite transaction. Hull ownership participates in Eden's active-ship alternate key, so transfer updates only a verified inactive hull within that same transaction and retains its primary identity. Ownership transfer voids its old insurance policy. Insufficient funds/items, a failed ledger insert, stale ownership and concurrent competing acceptors cannot partially deliver goods or charge twice. Rejection is available only to the private assignee and retains escrow for the issuer to collect. Cancellation or expired/rejected collection returns held assets and reward once; Eden retains the broker fee and the public deposit. Expired offers release a creation slot while retaining escrow until collection. Dismissal removes a settled contract's attention indicator without deleting its audit history.

Fee rules follow native `contractscommon.CalcContractFees` and the creation window's private branch. Private contracts cost **10,000 ISK**, with no deposit or sales tax. Public broker fees start at **0.4%**, reduced by **0.02 percentage points per Broker Relations level**, with a **10,000 ISK** minimum and skill-adjusted **100 million ISK** cap before the native **5% per additional day** duration multiplier. Public deposits are **1%**, bounded to **100,000–100 million ISK**. Public sales tax starts at **1%**, reduced by **0.1 percentage points per Accounting level**, with a **10,000 ISK** minimum and skill-adjusted **1 billion ISK** cap. Native whole-ISK truncation order is retained before the broker-duration multiplier. Contracting and Advanced Contracting provide **1 + 4 × each skill level** outstanding slots; Alpha caps use the pinned clone-grade catalog. ISK values use checked integer cents, reject nonfinite/fractional-cent values and respect the client's **10 trillion ISK** maximum. The ledger uses native contract entry types **66, 68, 71–74, 82 and 83**, with contract IDs as references and a separate journal-ID range beginning at **12 trillion**. Wallet escrow reports held reward/deposit and estimates held item value from catalog base prices, including nested contents once; testing-market prices are not substituted.

Creation returns a **list of contract IDs even for one contract**: native `ShowCreatedContracts` calls `len()` and indexes `[0]`. Details replies contain named `contract`, `items` and empty `bids` members. Owner lists return contract rows plus an ID-keyed item-list dictionary. Native issued-by filtering is **False**, issued-to is **True**, and **None** includes both. Login assignments contain contract/issuer fields; attention entries are native DBRows with `(contractID, forCorp)`, matching the client's simultaneous index and attribute access. Page/limit replies include the native personal/corporation count fields; courier-progress and auction-bid lists were empty at this milestone; courier progress and auction bids are implemented below. Search parses all 26 native keyword fields, including list/set type selections, location hierarchy, issuer, price/reward/collateral/volume ranges, category/group/name filters, security classes, public/private availability, trade/multiple-item exclusions, blueprint hints, sorting and offset. Replies contain detail bundles, found count, measured search time and the native **100-row page / 1000-result cap**. Security classes use the pinned **0 / 0.45 / 0.95** boundaries. Public search never exposes another pilot's private assignment; expired and settled offers are excluded.

Connected sessions receive persisted, ordered inventory changes, current wallet balance and native contract events after commit. Assignment history fetched before its pending event is not announced twice; assignments cancelled/rejected/expired before delivery are skipped while advancing the cursor. The bounded feed drains over repeated ticks without requiring another write. Accepted item exchanges notify the issuer of acceptance and completion, and the acceptor of completion, allowing native contract caches to refresh. Reclaim refreshes escrow; the initiating native window owns its normal cancellation refresh. Financial event delivery reads the current wallet once per batch, so a queued older contract event cannot rewind a later unrelated wallet transaction. Offline pilots recover contracts, goods, journals, assignments and attention state through normal reads after reconnect.
