### Personal courier contracts

Personal courier contracts extend the same persistent creation, cancellation, assignment, search and collection routes with type **3**, in-progress status **1** and failed status **7**. `contractProxy.CompleteContract(contractID, 4)` delivers; status **7** fails the shipment. `GetCourierContractFromItemID(itemID)` returns the owning hauler's package/contract reference. The existing `invbroker.GetInventoryFromId` owner binds an accessible package to its native inventory, providing `GetSelfInvItem` and zero-argument `BreakPlasticWrap`. Bindings expire with the location context; mutations recheck ownership and accessibility.

Creation accepts goods at a catalog NPC station, an NPC-station destination, **1–28 delivery days**, a reward and collateral. Requested goods, a sale price, structures, corporation/bulk contracts and wrapping another courier package reject atomically. The shared graph, selection, expiry, amount and ownership bounds still apply; shipments cannot exceed **1,200,000 m³**. Outstanding and accepted deliveries consume issuer contract slots. Remote acceptance is supported: it debits the hauler's collateral and creates the wrap in the origin station hangar, while the original goods remain hidden in escrow. Public courier broker/deposit rates use the native **0.1** cost multiplier; the broker minimum remains **10,000 ISK**, the public deposit minimum becomes **10,000 ISK**, and courier sales tax is zero. Private contracts retain their fixed **10,000 ISK** fee without a deposit.

The native Plastic Wrap is type **3468**, group **649**, category **2**. Its physical inventory quantity is **negative package volume × 100**: pinned `inventorycommon.util.GetItemVolume` divides that quantity by -100. Its native virtual stack size is one. Eden stores a normal singleton item with an explicit volume override, rounds volume upward to centi-cubic-metres with a positive minimum, and converts only at the wire boundary. Normal cargo capacity, movement, ship-loss persistence and wreck pickup use that actual volume. A surviving wrap can be recovered and delivered; destruction does not recreate its hidden contents.

Delivery requires the accepting pilot docked at the destination with the intact package in that station hangar or the active ship's ordinary cargo. One transaction removes the wrap, releases the original goods to the issuer's destination hangar, pays the hauler's reward and collateral refund, refunds the issuer's public deposit and records completion feeds/journals. Fitted hulls, nested contents, health and identities are preserved. A delivery may arrive late until either party actually marks it failed. The hauler can fail at any time; the issuer can fail only **strictly after** acceptance time plus the agreed days, matching the pinned client's overdue check.

Failure pays collateral and returns the held reward to the issuer, forfeiting the public deposit. If the actual wrap still exists, its goods become the hauler's property but stay sealed until unpacked; transferred hull insurance is voided. If the wrap is gone, held contents are destroyed rather than materialized elsewhere. Breaking an intact package fails an active contract and releases its goods at the package's current station hangar or active cargo location in that same transaction, including ordinary cargo in space. Assembled hulls require moving the wrap into a station hangar before unpacking. Repeated settlement cannot charge or pay twice; missing, foreign, inaccessible and wrong-destination packages reject without mutation.

Details return destination, duration, collateral and crate ID. Accepted courier lists, page counts, issuer outstanding slots, login progress and delivery deadlines now reflect persisted state. Acceptance emits `OnContractAccepted` without prematurely completing the delivery; settlement emits `OnContractCompleted` or `OnContractFailed` after commit. Search supports courier type, origin/destination hierarchy, collateral, reward and volume ranges, plus shared availability, issuer, text, sorting and pagination. Security filtering currently checks the origin system. Courier search and nonissuer details omit the sealed manifest; item/type/blueprint filters cannot discover hidden cargo, and item-exchange multiple-item exclusions do not inspect its contents. The issuer retains its own manifest for inspection/copying. This milestone left auctions and bids pending; they are implemented below. Corporation/structure contracts, bulk creation and package subcontracting remain pending.

World version **51** adds contract type, destination hierarchy, delivery duration, collateral and unique nullable package identity, plus nullable owned-item volume overrides. Existing version-50 contracts default to item exchange and retain their escrow, wallets and history. Fresh worlds and older upgrade paths use the same resulting schema. Tests reopen actual SQLite worlds and exercise migration, ledger-insert rollback, deadline boundaries, capacity boundaries, native wire consumers, nested hull delivery and real simulated NPC destruction with both package survival outcomes.

### Personal auction contracts

Personal auction type **2** now supports native `contractProxy.PlaceBid(contractID, amount, forCorp)`, `FinishAuction(contractID, isIssuer)`, `GetMyBids(forCorp)` and `DeleteContractNotification(contractID, forCorp)`, alongside creation, details, assignment, search, owner lists and cancellation. The native request's `price` is the starting bid and `collateral` is the optional buyout. Auctions require offered goods without requested goods, reward or courier terms. Public auctions start at **1 million ISK**; private auctions have no public starting-price minimum. Auction expiries are **1, 3, 7 or 14 days**: the native auction form removes the final 28-day option. Starting bids and buyouts retain the creation form's two decimal places.

`ContractsSvc.PlaceBid` computes its next displayed minimum from the current bid plus **max(truncate(10% of starting bid), 1,000 ISK)**, caps that candidate to a nonzero buyout, takes the maximum with the starting price, and truncates the final result to whole ISK. Ordinary bid submissions use whole ISK; the buyout checkbox sends the exact buyout, including cents. Eden preserves that distinction, requires a strict increase over any current bid and rejects amounts above the buyout or shared monetary limit. Self-bidding, unauthorized private bidders, expired/closed auctions, insufficient funds and ordinary acceptance of an auction reject without partial changes. Auctions with bids cannot be cancelled or rejected. Unbid auctions use the existing cancellation/expired-escrow collection path.

A bid debits the bidder into contract escrow. A leading bidder increasing its own offer pays only the difference. A replacement bidder funds its complete bid and the previous leader receives its full held amount back in the same SQLite transaction. Immutable bid rows retain identity, bidder, amount and time, ordered highest/newest first; dismissing a losing or collected bidder's own list entry preserves the shared history and all financial records. The native bidder list is unpaged and includes every undismissed auction the pilot bid on. Wallet escrow reports only that pilot's actual outstanding winning hold, plus the existing issuer deposits and courier collateral. Native journal types **63/64/67** represent auction bids, refunds and sold proceeds.

An ordinary ended auction keeps its goods and proceeds held until their respective owners claim them. The winner's remote claim transfers the original goods into that winner's origin-station hangar, retaining nested items, fitted modules, loaded charges, carried hulls and health; hull ownership transfer voids the previous insurance. The issuer's independent claim pays the winning amount, refunds the successful public deposit and charges sales tax on the **final winning price**, using the issuer's effective Accounting level recorded at creation. Private auctions retain their fixed broker fee without public deposit or sales tax. Issuer-only and winner-only collection use native statuses **2** and **3**; both claimed becomes finished status **4**. Repeated claims are idempotent. A buyout immediately delivers goods and closes bidding, leaving only the issuer's money claim. Buyout, refund and delivery feeds commit together with the actual state, and forced ledger/feed failures roll back raw hull-owner updates as well as items, wallets and contract flags.

[CCP's auction guide](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203280371-Auction-Contracts), updated July 15, 2024, confirms separate ordinary claims, immediate buyout delivery and repeated late-bid extensions. It does not publish an exact extension window. Eden uses a documented **15-minute response window**: a successful nonbuyout bid sets the expiry to the later of its current expiry and 15 minutes after that bid. Repeated bids can extend it again. This interval is an Eden policy, not a verified pinned-server constant. Buyouts close immediately instead of extending the deadline.

Native `bids` detail rows expose `bidID`, `bidderID`, `amount` and `dateBid`. Auction search works for type **2**, combined auction/item-exchange type **10** and all type **11**. Price ranges and ordering use the current bid when one exists, otherwise the starting price; collateral ranges use buyout, and `excludeNoBuyout` removes only auctions without a buyout. The existing type/name, item multiplicity, location, availability, security and pagination filters remain available. Lists and page/login attention agree on the issuer's unpaid proceeds and the winner's unclaimed goods, rather than hiding a winner behind the issuer-only expired-list filter. Bid counts and bid-on-by filtering use durable bid participation. Connected pilots receive native `OnContractOutbid`, `OnContractBuyout`, escrow and final completion events; ordinary bids do not falsely complete the auction. Offline pilots reconstruct the same bid, claim, attention and wallet state from persistence.

World version **52** adds auction buyout/current-bid values, the saved Accounting level, independent issuer/winner collection flags and `WorldContractBid`, with participant and contract indexes. Genuine version-51 upgrade coverage removes those fields/table before reopening a courier world, then verifies unchanged packages, goods and held collateral. Existing item exchanges and couriers keep their types, state and feeds. Corporation/structure auctions, bulk creation, package subcontracting and blueprint research/copies remain pending.

## Launcher accounts and developer roles

API v1 adds registration, launcher sessions, rotating remembered sessions, logout and single-use 180-second game tickets. Credentials and token hashes use additive Launcher tables; account/pilot identities and gameplay schema versions are preserved. The RSA/AES/challenge exchange remains unchanged. Manifest signing accepts only the exact fingerprinted 17,769-byte build 3662168 prefix. Private keys never leave the host.

Consulted [Carbon Blue crypto.cpp at af0d65b](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/crypto.cpp) for manifest-backed RSA and SHA-256 behavior. Pinned publicGateway/grpc/connectionConfig.pyj confirms that EVE_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_ADDRESS selects cleartext. The launcher uses a loopback relay with certificate-validated HTTP/2 TLS upstream for remote gateways.

Pinned carbon/common/script/sys/serviceConst.pyj gives WORLDMOD at bit 12, PROGRAMMER at bit 51, QA at bit 52, GMH at bit 53, GML at bit 54, and PLAYER at bit 61. Developer accounts receive those six roles; QA satisfies the Insider elevated-player mask and GML enables GM/WM Extras. The stored mask is sent in both login session_init and SessionInitialStateNotification. Standard accounts receive PLAYER. Developer registration requires a server-controlled invitation. Missing slash commands and privileged services remain unsupported.

Historical automated validation on the launcher branch: all 501 tests passed after integration with the committed hardener release, including launcher accounts, developer roles, HTTP throttling and the QA starter-system compatibility response.

After Computer Use was stopped, subsequent maintainer input produced server records confirming successful CreateCharacterWithDoll and selection of pilot test (90000003), at station 60015154 in ship 2000000000005. The final station scene and Insider/GM menus were not visually observed by the agent.

Historical live observations on the launcher branch: the Avalonia launcher created an invitation-protected developer account over the same PC's LAN HTTPS endpoint. Its prepared client connected on TCP 26200, verified build 3662168 and completed encrypted login/challenge verification. The client displayed character creation. The maintainer advanced to naming, where QA-only GetQAStarterSystemIDs was exposed. Pinned ccSvc._QAPickStarterSystem returns None for an empty system list, preserving normal school allocation; QA callers now receive that empty list, while standard callers remain unsupported. The underlying CreateCharacterWithDoll call retains ten arguments with a None override. Computer Use was stopped by the user with Escape after the fix was rebuilt. Subsequent maintainer input confirmed creation and selection server-side as recorded above; the final station scene, Insider/GM menus and the remaining native acceptance checks were not visually verified. The test topology used a non-loopback address and TLS on one machine, not a second-machine deployment.

### Main integration and test performance (November 4, 2026)

Integrated launcher branch `73da857` into main while retaining the hull audit export and the host's injectable clock. A production-service integration test checks single-use launcher tickets, account roles and expiry at the exact clock boundary. Fixture changes cache immutable base catalog bytes and parsed definitions while preserving independent writable databases and real world initialization. Identity helpers read only the required fields. The test runner now permits twelve conservative workers for the development machine's 14 cores / 20 hardware threads. Scratch databases use `artifacts/test-data` on the repository drive, with `EDEN_TEST_TEMP_DIRECTORY` available as an override, rather than the Windows temporary folder on C:.

**Automated validation:** tool and package restore passed; the final build passed with zero warnings/errors. The final focused run passed 34 tests in four seconds using `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Fixtures.TestCatalogTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.WorldInitializationTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Launcher|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting`. This covers fixture isolation, initialization/migrations, accounts, developer roles, API throttling and host composition. Both Python policy suites passed (three catalog setup cases and nine formatting cases), and formatting verification passed across 394 files at the 140-character limit. A representative pair of Caracal cases, selected with `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.CombatHullTests&DisplayName~type: 621`, passed before and after the fixture optimizations: aggregate case duration fell from 14.69 to 6.17 seconds (about 2.4 times faster). That comparison preceded the scratch-directory move and is not a measurement of full-suite runtime. Two earlier full runs were interrupted at the maintainer's request, first to improve performance and then after C: disk saturation; neither is a pass. The maintainer then changed policy: full .NET runs require an explicit regression-check request. Repository instructions and GitHub validation now honor that opt-in; no full run covers this final integration.

**Live acceptance:** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled. Native rendering, input and gameplay feel remain unverified for this integration.

**Optional maintainer checks:** Sign in and launch through the desktop launcher; check remembered sign-in and tray/relay behavior with a running client.

Launcher registration and the account-password command now accept 6–128 characters. The launcher password hint and hosting guides use the same range. Boundary coverage checks empty, five-, six-, 128- and 129-character passwords for both registration and password changes. ## Startup exchange

The startup configuration identifies release 24.01, codename `V24.01`, region `ccp`, and port 26000. The client's packaged MachoNet version module specifies **496**. The installed GPS transport and the build-matched Blue marshaller supplied the message order, field types, and serialization rules. [Blue marshal implementation](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp)

Each startup frame begins with a four-byte little-endian payload length. Blue marshal version 0 starts with `7e` and a four-byte shared-object count. The verified startup frames use zero shared objects. Eden accepts unflagged frames up to 16 KiB during this phase and reads each frame to completion before processing it. [Carbon transport flags](https://github.com/carbonengine/io/blob/8d8f339fd19f2e8095fcd90ad5415cb4ca00c331/src/protocol.h)

| Order | Sender | Payload |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Eden | Seven-field tuple containing marker 170472, protocol 496, pilot count 0, version 24.01 as a double, build 3662168, byte string `V24.01@ccp`, and update status `Unknown`. |
| 2 | Client | Six-field compatibility tuple with the same first six values. |
| 3 | Client | The tuple `(None, b'QC')` requests queue status. |
| 4 | Eden | Integer zero reports an empty login queue. |

The implementation compares the observed client messages byte for byte. A status request ends the probe. A login request continues into the encrypted account session. The host logs progress without recording arbitrary payloads.

## Acceptance record

On November 1, 2026, two fresh Windows client processes sent the compatibility reply and queue check to the C# listener after receiving its greeting. Each used the isolated installation with the stock executable and script archive. The tests preserve the matching bytes and check a different build, an unsupported command, short reads, and invalid frame headers.

## Encrypted local authentication

The development account uses the client's SSO launch path. After its version reply the client sends a VIP-key request and a pair of RSA-encrypted AES parameters. Eden acknowledges key negotiation, decrypts the local ticket with the session cipher, and validates its signature and expiry. Each side proves receipt of the other's random challenge before the server issues the account session. Two fresh client processes reported `LLV Succeeded` and sent the authenticated `machoNet.GetServiceInfo` call.

The client uses AES-256-CBC with per-packet padding. Challenge hashes cover a Blue-marshaled one-element tuple. The signed client expression is fixed to an empty dictionary. The outer MachoNet envelope may be zlib-compressed and contains another Blue stream for service arguments. The built-in string table comes from MIT-licensed Carbon Blue. [Carbon crypto](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/crypto.cpp) · [Carbon license](carbon-license.md)

## Character selection

After authentication Eden returns service information and an initial player session. The client requests `charUnboundMgr.GetCharacterSelectionData`, primes its owner cache, then builds the pilot card. The seeded roster contains one player account and one pilot with ID 90000001, an Ibis, and the Caldari Navy Assembly Plant at Jita IV – Moon 4. The portrait uses the client silhouette.

Selecting the card checks the single-login policy, resolves the population service and its load-slot request, checks character lock status, and sends `SelectCharacterID`. Eden loads the account’s docked pilot from SQLite and validates the selected ID before sending the world-session transition. Unsupported service calls receive a MachoNet error.

### Live verification

On November 1, 2026, the staged build 3662168 client displayed the account's pilot name, ship, and Jita station. A click on the card sent the selection request, and the C# host accepted character 90000001. A second login to the same running host fetched the same pilot in a fresh session. The character-selection tests cover the roster, valid and invalid selections, and unsupported methods.


## Docked session and inventory

Build 3662168 accepts a command-16 `SessionChangeNotification` before the successful `SelectCharacterID` reply. Its body is `(sessionID, (0, changedFields), nodesOfInterest)`. Each changed field contains its previous and new values. A docked session sets `stationid`, `stationid2`, `locationid` and `solarsystemid2`. The in-space `solarsystemid` stays unset. An invalid selection returns only the call error.

The client binds `dogmaIM.GetAllInfo` to prime pilot and ship state. It uses `stationSvc.GetStationItemBits` for the station model and `map.GetStationInfo` for the station row. The station inventory broker binds to `(60003760, 15)`. Its first call can be nested in the bind request. `GetInventory(10004, None)` returns the hangar handle, while `GetInventoryFromId(shipID, 1)` returns the ship handle. The hangar lists the assembled ship with hangar flag 4. The ship's own hold starts empty. Inventory rows use native `blue.DBRow` and `blue.DBRowDescriptor`. `config.GetMultiLocationsEx` supplies the owned ship's name. All handles are scoped to the authenticated connection.

### Docked acceptance record

The staged client rendered the Ibis inside Jita IV – Moon 4 and opened its native Inventory window. After restarting the server and client, the active inventory entry read **Ibis (Ibis)**. The host reported ship `1000000000001` at station `60003760`. SQLite contained one ship owned by pilot `90000001` and passed its integrity and foreign-key checks. Ancillary services including bookmarks, contracts, and the separate public gateway are incomplete.

## First flight

Undocking the owned Ibis changes `locationid` from the station to Jita, clears both station fields, and sets `solarsystemid`. Docking reverses those changes. The pilot and ship IDs stay the same. In space the client binds its inventory broker to the system with group 5 and binds `beyonce` to the system ID.

Eden sends `DoDestinyUpdate` after the ballpark bind. Its `SetState` carries a full-state stream with the simulation tick, a rigid station and a free ship, plus the corresponding slim items. Jita's station radius is 100,000 m. The Ibis has a radius of 47 m, mass of 1,163,000 kg, agility of 4.5, and base speed of 295 m/s. These values come from the pinned types and typedogma resources listed above. [Destiny stream implementation](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) · [Destiny motion integration](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp)

A direction or speed command changes the local course. Stop commits the current position and sends the ship's position and zero velocity back to the client. When Dock is selected outside interaction range, the client first follows the station and requests docking on arrival. Eden checks the ship, station and surface distance before accepting the request.

SQLite world version 4 adds a flight row keyed by ship ID. The station on the ship identifies its home port and system. A flight row supplies the active space position. Eden checkpoints moving ships about once a second and removes the row on docking. Login starts the ship at rest at the last committed position.

### First-flight acceptance, November 2, 2026

Using staged Windows build 3662168, Eden Pilot undocked the Ibis and double-clicked open space. In one run the station range increased from 453 m to 1,699 m. After **Stop My Ship** and a restart of both processes, Overview still showed 1,699 m and the HUD showed 0.0 m/s. A separate Dock request from 7,394 m completed the approach and opened the hangar.

On the final build the HUD showed 224 m/s during flight and 0.0 m/s after **Stop My Ship**, with the station 2,254 m away. Selecting **Dock** returned the Ibis to the Caldari Navy Assembly Plant. After another server and client restart, the character card showed the Ibis in Jita. Selecting Eden Pilot opened the station with **Ibis (Ibis)** active in Inventory. The host loaded ship `1000000000001` at station `60003760`. SQLite passed integrity and foreign-key checks.

Automated checks passed separately. Restore and build succeeded without warnings, all 29 tests passed, and formatting verification passed. The tests cover movement with a controlled clock, restart in space and in station, approach, docking range, ship identity and the snapshot layout.

## Two pilots in Jita, November 2, 2026

World version 5 adds a second local account, Eden Wing, with its own pilot and Ibis. The signed ticket identifies the account. Both accounts can connect concurrently, and a second session for the same account is rejected.

Each client receives its own ship as the ballpark ego. The initial snapshot includes other connected ships in the same system. After correcting station contact and course updates, Wing observed Pilot at 294 m/s and later at rest 23 km away. Disconnecting Wing removed it from Pilot's Overview, but the displayed station range jumped. That prompted the switch to individual ship arrival and departure messages.

## Build 3662168 game catalog

The import inventory contains **4,913,627 source records across 442 datasets and supporting indexes, schemas, and metadata entries**. The total includes both representations when the client and export describe the same object. The universe projection includes 114 regions, 1,184 constellations, 8,490 systems, 5,210 NPC stations, and 53,299 item types.

| Source family | Entries | Records |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| CCP JSON Lines export, including build metadata | 102 | 676,965 |
| Client definitions, localization, and loose game-data files | 211 | 3,244,505 |
| Client cache indexes | 14 | 55,478 |
| Client map database | 2 | 481,069 |
| Regional planet attribute files | 97 | 409,423 |
| External FSD schema descriptors | 6 | 6 |
| Exoplanet sample light curves | 10 | 46,181 |

Setup verifies the archive and client resource index against pinned SHA-256 digests. It resolves indexed files from the local cache, checks their content hashes, and uses the staged client's loaders for binary definitions. The [source inventory](../tools/catalog/build-3662168.json) records the source, content hash, row count, and ordered key digest for every catalog entry. Models, textures, audio, navigation meshes, and other engine assets remain in the staged installation.

`SourceDataset` identifies each source inside the catalog. `SourceOverlap` counts matching record keys for paired datasets. `SourceDifference` identifies the keys and shared fields with different values. Both representations are available from `SourceRecord`. Client values supply positions, item physics, and gate destinations for gameplay. The export also supplies data absent from the client.

`CatalogDbContext` maps the universe and item relationships and exposes typed views of the decoded datasets. For example, an importer or server service can read a blueprint's manufacturing materials with the following query.

```csharp
using CatalogDbContext catalog = new(catalogPath);
var blueprint = CatalogData.Sde.Blueprints.Get(catalog, 681);
var materials = blueprint.Activities?.Manufacturing?.Materials;
```

The source models are generated from the pinned record shapes. Scalar fields, arrays, nested objects, and language variants retain their types. Heterogeneous source fields use `JsonElement`. To reproduce the model file after verifying the catalog, run `python tools/catalog/generate_models.py "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Eden\catalog.sqlite" src/Eden.Server/Catalog/CatalogData.g.cs` from the repository root.

World schema version 6 stores each owned ship's catalog type and home station by ID. Opening the save verifies its ship types, stations, and pilot corporations. The protocol receives a pilot snapshot with flight and hull attributes, the station designation, and the saved location. The host creates a short-lived EF context for each save operation.

### Catalog acceptance, November 2, 2026

**Automated checks.** Restore, build, and formatting verification passed with zero build warnings or errors. All 41 .NET tests and all three catalog setup tests passed. The documented setup command built and activated the local catalog after its verifier read all 4,913,627 records through their typed mappings.

**Live client.** Eden Pilot and Eden Wing each saw the other Ibis in Overview. Pilot docked at the Caldari Navy Assembly Plant, where Inventory showed **Ibis (Ibis)** with 125 m³ capacity. After undocking, Overview showed the station at 453 m. The maintainer confirmed the final restart check with Pilot docked and Wing in space.

## Within-system warp and braking, November 2, 2026

Each ballpark snapshot includes the current system's catalog celestials and NPC stations. Each active gate carries its destination system and gate IDs. System content supplies secondary suns and disrupted gates in systems that have them. Build 3662168 has 67 static objects in Jita, comprising one star, eight planets, 33 moons, seven gates, and 18 NPC stations. General and All Overview tabs apply the client's own type filters.

The bound flight call is `CmdWarpToStuff("item", itemID, minRange=metres)`. The host resolves the target in the current system, aligns the ship and sends Destiny's `WarpTo` with the catalog warp speed in milli-AU/s. A separate `OnSpecialFX` toggle drives the warp tunnel. Nearby clients receive the course, then lose or acquire the moving ship as it crosses the 8,000 km visibility radius. The host saves positions during flight. A stop command releases thrust, and hull mass and agility determine the remaining drift.

**Staged client observations.** On the tunnel revision, Eden Pilot accelerated to 189 m/s leaving the Perimeter gate. During the return to Jita Trade Hub the blue tunnel and **Warp Drive Active** appeared with the station still 26.2 AU away. Wing followed and saw Pilot's Ibis at 906 m from its own ship, with the station at 1,500 m. The All tab showed Jita IV as a gas planet and listed the nearby moons. Selecting the planet exposed an unsupported structure-directory call. The handler now returns the empty player-structure list for the local world.

**Automated checks on the braking revision.** Restore and build passed with zero warnings or errors. All 44 tests passed and formatting verification passed. Tests cover the system object list, packet order for warp entry, range validation, return to docking range, persisted position, and progressive braking without a final position correction. Live confirmation of the reported stop lurch and planet selection on this revision is pending.

## Shared flight ticks and celestial warp-ins, November 2, 2026

The server stamps both pilots' movement on the same one-second clock. A warp command sets the destination while the ship is still aligning. The pilot receives the warping effect immediately after the movement command. In build 3662168 the effect prepares the tunnel from the ball's destination. Its loop watches the native warp stamp before activating the camera and acceleration effects. Cancelling during alignment stops the effect. The client plays the arrival flash when it loads a remote ship already in warp.

Warp begins above 75% of hull speed with a velocity direction within the reference dot-product tolerance. A ship obstructed for 180 alignment ticks uses the reference timeout. SetState and AddBalls carry the ship's movement mode, velocity and speed fraction. An active warp also carries the original start stamp, distance, destination, minimum range and speed factor. On the first deceleration tick below the exit threshold the server carries the actual exit velocity into sublight motion. A legacy Stop tick damps vertical velocity before normal drag. Station approach recomputes thrust each tick, and sublight collision uses the first rigid surface crossed. [Destiny alignment](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ball.cpp#L1898-L1920) · [Destiny simulation](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp) · [Destiny state stream](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp#L3246-L3302)

Warp-to-zero targets a celestial's nominal warp-in point. Planets use their ID, radius and position, large moons use their radius and position, and ordinary objects use the near edge. The requested range and ship lane offset apply relative to this point. With the installed catalog the nominal altitude is 30,342 km for Jita V and 5,164 km for Jita V – Moon 16. [EVE warp-in formulas](https://developers.eveonline.com/docs/guides/useful-formulae/#warp-in-points)

**Automated checks.** Restore, build, all 54 tests and formatting verification passed. Coverage includes opposite and aligned headings, obstructed alignment and cancellation, full and incremental mid-warp records, short and long warp exits, vertical braking, an oblique approach, saved remote-body contact, and planet and moon coordinates from two origins.

**Staged client observations.** Eden Pilot warped to Jita V and back to Jita V – Moon 16. Selected Item showed 30,343 km at the planet and 5,164 km at the moon. On the tunnel-entry revision, Pilot restarted at the same moon range. On the 11.04 AU flight back to the Trade Hub, Pilot showed **Establishing Warp Vector** and then **Warp Drive Active**. Wing saw Pilot's arriving Ibis in Overview. Selecting **Approach** at the station showed **Approaching** at 37.1 m/s and the station range closed to one metre. On the 39.81 AU Perimeter flight the ship turned while establishing its vector, then the HUD showed active warp and the background streaked during acceleration. The maintainer confirmed the entry transition looked right.

After the final observer-effect change, Wing remained at 50 m from Jita Trade Hub. Pilot warped back from the Perimeter gate, appeared in Wing's Overview as an Ibis, and closed to 2,107 m from Wing during arrival.

## Stargate travel, November 2, 2026

Build 3662168 sends `beyonce.CmdStargateJump(sourceGateID, destinationGateID, shipID)`. Eden checks the reciprocal catalog link, active ship, flight state, and 2,500 m surface range. A gate's `jumps` row exposes named columns for the menu and ordered values for the camera. The gate controller reads the local and destination corruption and suppression pairs when preparing each model.

The departure `OnSpecialFX` carries the ship, source gate, `effects.JumpOut`, and the destination system in `graphicInfo[0]`. The client's `SubwayService` uses that system to select the destination scene and security lighting. The catalog gives the effect a 10,000 ms duration. The four staged resources referenced by `client/traveleffects` for JumpOut matched the build's resource index, including the transition scene, tunnel, ship hiding effect, and multi-effect.

After ten simulation ticks Eden saves the destination system and coordinates together, releases location-bound services and sends the session change. The next ballpark bind supplies the destination objects and ship, followed by `effects.JumpIn`. The client's travel service completes the camera transition. World version 7 keeps existing in-space saves relative to their home system until the first jump.

**Live Windows client.** Eden Pilot selected Jump at the Perimeter gate in Jita. The client showed **Jumping from Jita to Perimeter** and the Ibis entering the tunnel. An early revision loaded Perimeter's Overview over a blank scene. After supplying the destination to the travel effect, the maintainer flew from Perimeter to Jita and back and confirmed the scene was fixed. The server recorded both transfers. The Jita-side gate rendered during approach, and the Ibis, starfield, nebula and Overview were visible during subsequent flight in Perimeter. The final packet cleanup removes a redundant legacy GateActivity call that logged an exception on arrival.

**Automated checks on the committed revision.** Restore and build succeeded with zero warnings and errors. All 56 tests and formatting verification passed. The gate test covers a round trip, rejected requests, the destination in the departure effect, arrival after the new snapshot, approach to the return gate, and reopening the save. A migration test restores a version 6 flight in its original system.

## Account profiles and trained skills, November 2, 2026

Opening the sheet from a fresh client exposed an unsupported `charMgr.GetPublicInfo` call. Reopening the partially constructed window could hide the original failure. The selected pilot's public info now supplies the birth timestamp (Windows FILETIME), race, bloodline, gender, school, ancestry, and corporation. The character card and session transition read their overlapping fields from the same profile. The migration preserves the prior local identity and assigns the catalog's Deteis Merchandisers ancestry and School of Applied Knowledge. The school affiliation is separate from the ship's current location.

World version 10 records trained points by pilot ID and skill type ID. The starter grant reads the race’s skill levels from build 3662168’s `client/races` dataset. The Caldari entry includes 60 injected skills, 49 at level I or higher, for 384,047 total SP. Spaceship Command (3327) starts at III. Migration preserves higher saved SP. Dogma attribute 275 supplies the rank. Build 3662168 rounds each level threshold up after multiplying by rank. The account roster sums the pilot's points. The selected pilot's bound skill service returns a type-ID dictionary for both `GetSkills` and `GetAllSkills`. Each value constructs the client's `characterskills.common.character_skill_entry.CharacterSkillEntry` with the type, derived level, points, rank, and optional virtual level. The client reads trained and effective levels through properties on that object. The standard neural map assigns 19 charisma and 20 each to intelligence, memory, perception and willpower. [CCP default attributes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/learning-skills-are-going-away-1) Queue, free points and history reflect an empty training state.

For wire behavior, Carbon Blue's reduce reader resolves the outbound callable and argument tuple. The skill constructor and public-info fields come from build 3662168's packaged scripts. [Carbon Blue marshaller](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp)

**Live Windows client.** After restarting the host and client, Eden Pilot's character card showed 384,047 SP and the Ibis undocked in Jita. The Character tab displayed a rendered avatar, birth date, total SP of 384,047 and the Jita IV – Moon 4 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant under Current Home Station. The Skills button opened the Skill Catalogue. The My skills filter distinguishes injected skills from the rest of the catalogue. The avatar preview uses the client's fallback appearance. The separate Skill Plan Service reported that it was unavailable.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build, all 59 tests and formatting verification passed. Tests cover profile access before and after selection, migration of older saves, independent pilot SP across a reopen, and the rounded threshold for the second level.

### Character-sheet service roadmap

1. **Training queue and history.** Queue the next level of an injected skill as the first writable training milestone. Trace the queue edit and notification calls in the staged client. Store each pilot's ordered queue, point balance and UTC training timestamps in the world save. Validate prerequisites and rank against the catalog. Commit queue edits and completed levels with their history entries in one transaction. Verify partial progress after restart, one completion, matching SP in the roster and sheet, and independent progress for Wing.
2. **Attributes and clones.** Base attributes, remap availability and shared training/dogma values are implemented with automated coverage below. `CharacterLogoffHandler` now owns this account-phase call. ## Character-sheet attributes and biography, November 4, 2026

Build 3662168's `charsheet/attributesPanel.py`, `neocom/attributes.py` and `services/skillsvc.py` use the pilot-bound skill handler's `GetAttributes`, `GetRespecInfo` and `RespecCharacter`. Remap information is a dictionary containing `freeRespecs` and nullable FILETIME `nextTimedRespec`. Remapping submits one integer-keyed dictionary with exactly the five attribute IDs. The packaged `appConst.py` defines a 17-point minimum, 27-point maximum, 99-point total and 365-day interval. Eden grants both new and migrated pilots two initial bonus remaps. A timed remap takes precedence; bonus remaps leave the timed deadline unchanged. Saving the current map is a no-op. [CCP neural remap rules](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203267991-Neural-Remaps)

World version 39 adds `WorldCharacterSheet`, keyed by pilot ID. An absent row means the standard map (20 intelligence, memory, perception and willpower; 19 charisma), two bonus remaps, no timed deadline and an empty biography. The first write materializes that state, so newly created characters receive the same defaults. Pure rules live in Gameplay. Server persistence commits the new map, remap balance/deadline, banked whole SP, recalculated queue and history in one transaction. Elapsed levels are credited at the old deadlines; an active partial level earns its old rate until the remap timestamp, then the remaining queue uses the new attributes. Paused queues stay paused. Dogma priming and training planning read the same saved map. Protocol emits pilot attribute changes, `OnRespecInfoChanged` (no arguments), any banked skill/brain updates and `OnNewSkillQueueSaved`, updating the session's next completion deadline. Invalid allocations and exhausted remaps return the client's named errors; `RespecTooSoon` includes `nextTime`.

The setter has no character ID and always writes the selected pilot. Public biography reads are available after character selection. The editor's `MAXBIOLENGTH` is 2,500; Eden rejects longer stored text, embedded nulls and malformed requests without changing the save. Formatting markup and Unicode are preserved, including an empty biography. Biography replies explicitly use Python Unicode rather than a UTF-8 byte string. The inert codec also reads native UTF-16, empty and single-character Unicode forms. The inspected archive is `code.ccp`, SHA-256 `37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368`. ## Skill training queue, November 2, 2026

The stock Skill Catalogue calls the selected pilot's bound `SaveNewQueue` with a dictionary mapping zero-based positions to (skill type, target level) and an `activate` keyword. Pause calls `AbortTraining`. The server validates injected skills, sequential levels, queued prerequisites, the 150-entry limit, time horizon and Alpha training restrictions. It calculates UTC start and end times from the catalog's rank and primary and secondary attributes. Saving the same active head preserves its timestamps. Replacing or pausing it banks earned whole SP. The world advances elapsed levels in one SQLite transaction on login or while connected. Each completed level produces one history row. [CCP skill training](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203217062-Skill-Training)

The client's lower Training Time estimate uses whole SP. Its `_UpdateTrainingTimeLabelThread` samples every 500 ms, `GetTrainingLengthOfQueue` totals durations from remaining points, and `get_skill_points_trained_at_sample_time` truncates to an integer. With 20 perception and 20 willpower an Alpha pilot earns 15 SP/minute, so the lower estimate changes every four seconds. The queue entry uses its finish timestamp.

**Live Windows client.** In the Skill Catalogue, Eden Pilot added Amarr Frigate I. The queue showed one of 150 slots, a 500-SP target, and approximately 33 minutes 20 seconds. Pausing showed **Paused**, 488 points remaining and 384,059 total SP. Resuming started the countdown from the remaining points. After restarting the host and client, the queue still showed Amarr Frigate I with 28 minutes remaining and the catalogue showed 384,121 SP.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build, 61 tests and formatting verification passed. A controlled clock verifies partial SP on pause, identical timestamps on retry, multiple levels completed during an offline interval, isolated pilots and exactly-once history. An invalid edit rolls back the entire mutation without disturbing ongoing training.

## Local skill-plan gateway, November 2, 2026

Build 3662168 sends tracked-plan and personal-plan queries over the bidirectional HTTP/2 gRPC stream `eve_public.gateway.Requests/Send`. The launcher selects the cleartext loopback endpoint on port 26001. Eden hosts that endpoint beside MachoNet and returns a correlated `eve_public.Response` containing the matching protobuf `Any` payload. For a pilot with no plans, `GetActiveResponse.skill_plan.uuid` is 16 zero bytes and `GetAllResponse.skill_plans` is empty. Unsupported gateway payloads receive status 501. Plan editing and tracking writes are separate work.

## Persistent cargo and hangar items, November 2, 2026

World version 13 adds pilot-owned item stacks with stable IDs, type, location, inventory flag and positive quantity. Upgrading grants each existing pilot one Civilian Miner at the ship's saved station; character creation grants one at the school station in the same transaction. Reopening a migrated world does not repeat the grant. This is an Eden starter grant, not a claim about the stock server's starting fit.

Build 3662168's `invCache.Add` sends `(itemID, sourceID)` to the destination inventory handle with `flag` and optional `qty` keywords. `MultiAdd` sends a list of item IDs and a source. The client queries `itemLocking.GetItemsByLocation` before moving items; personal items have no corporation locks. Eden implements docked transfers between the active ship's cargo (flag 5) and the current station hangar (flag 4). It rejects foreign items, remote stations, fitting flags, space transfers, duplicate IDs and invalid quantities. Capacity comes from the hull and item volume from the pinned `client/types` record (Civilian Miner 3651: 5 m³). Multi-item moves validate capacity before writing; partial moves preserve the original remainder and create a new stack ID in one transaction. Stacking, fitting, mining and combat remain separate milestones.

The inventory list and `OnItemChange` reuse native `blue.DBRow` reductions with the existing descriptor. The notification takes `(newItemRow, oldValuesByColumnIndex, location)`, with `location=None` for these transfers. The client's `invCache.OnItemChange` requires all three arguments. Omitting the last argument saved the move but left the client's cached views unchanged during the first check. Column indices 3 and 4 carry the previous location and flag; quantity changes carry indices 5 and 9 (corrected after checking the pinned inventory constants). [Carbon Blue marshaller, revision af0d65b](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp). The build-specific call and notification behavior was checked against the pinned `eve/client/script/environment/invCache.pyj` and `eve/client/script/ui/services/corporation/itemLocking.pyj`.
