## Mining-laser fitting, November 2, 2026

World version 14 adds `Singleton` and `Online` to owned items, defaulting existing cargo and hangar stacks to false without repeating the grant. A docked pilot can fit one mining laser from each selected stack into the active ship's high slots. Fitting checks ownership, source location, the hull's high-slot and turret counts, and all six required-skill attribute pairs from the catalog. A split preserves the remainder and creates one assembled module in the same transaction. Unfitting moves that module to the station hangar or cargo, clears its online state, and checks cargo capacity when applicable. Other module families remain unavailable until their passive modifiers are implemented.

The pinned `inventorycommon.const` defines high slots 27 through 34, cargo 5, hangar 4, auto-fit 0, stack-size column 9 and singleton column 10. An assembled module has quantity -1, stack size 1 and singleton 1 on the wire. Catalog mining-laser group 54 includes Civilian Miner 3651, requiring Mining I and consuming 65 CPU and 2 power grid when online. CPU Management 3426 and Power Grid Management 3413 add five percent output per trained level. Eden validates those resource totals before `dogmaIM.SetModuleOnline(shipID, moduleID)` and persists online state; `TakeModuleOffline` releases the load. The new code groups these flags, attributes, effects, skills and service values in named enums and constants.

The station's circled ship icon calls the bound `corpStationMgr.DoStandingCheckForStationService(17)` before opening Fitting. Bindings require the current docked station and expire on departure. The fitting managers return empty saved/community fitting dictionaries; template writes remain unsupported. The pinned `godma`, `clientDogmaLocation`, fitting-window and inventory scripts supply these method signatures and field shapes. `UnloadAmmo` succeeds without changes for supported mining lasers, which have no charges.

`GetAllInfo` includes fitted modules in `shipInfo` and the instance cache. `ItemGetInfo` returns the owned module's inventory row, catalog attributes, online attribute and online effect. Inventory changes use `OnItemChange`; online toggles use `OnGodmaShipEffect`, followed by `OnModuleAttributeChanges` for ship CPU, power and turret availability. An early live refresh sent `OnGodmaPrimeItem`, whose godma row differs from the native inventory descriptor and caused `clientDogmaLocation.IsFitted` to remove the module. The corrected path lets the normal item change fetch module info and updates attributes/effects without replacing that row. Blue reductions reuse the existing codec checked against [Carbon Blue revision af0d65b](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp).

**Live Windows client.** On the isolated cargo test save, Eden Pilot's Inventory showed **Ibis (Ibis)**. Fitting a miner from the cargo stack left 24 miners in cargo and placed one in a high slot. After correcting the refresh path, **Put Offline** dimmed the module while retaining its slot and restored 162/162 CPU and 27.6/27.6 power grid. Its context menu then offered **Put Online**. **Unfit** cleared the slot and showed the assembled miner beside the five-miner stack in Item Hangar. Dragging that assembled miner onto the empty slot brought it online automatically; its tooltip showed 10 km range and 6 m³ per 10 seconds, and remaining resources read 97/162 CPU and 25.6/27.6 power grid. The maintainer confirmed that fitting works. After restarting both host and client on the final build, the circled ship button reopened Fitting with the online miner and the same resource totals. Mining activation and in-space online changes remain unimplemented.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all 92 tests and formatting verification passed. Added checks cover stack splitting, slot limits, missing skills, foreign items, space fitting rejection, online resource rejection and idempotence, offline/unfit state, restart snapshots, native item/effect notifications without the damaging prime refresh, station binding validation/expiry, and version-13 migration preserving cargo without duplicate grants.

## Native target locking, November 2, 2026

The pinned `eve/client/script/parklife/targetMgr.pyj` calls `dogmaIM.AddTarget(itemID)` and unpacks `(pending, targetList)`. True means that the client should wait for `OnTarget("add", itemID, reason)`; a false pending flag restores an existing lock immediately. `RemoveTarget`, `CancelAddTarget`, `GetTargets` and `GetTargeters` use the same selected pilot's connection. The target service accepts visible ships and asteroid-category objects, validates surface range and available target slots including pending scans, and rejects self, unavailable objects and attempts outside a live non-warping ballpark. Asteroid spawning and mining activation remain separate work.

Lock duration in seconds is `40000 / (scanResolution * asinh(signatureRadius)^2)`, using a monotonic clock and the server's one-second session tick. Repeated AddTarget requests retain the original scan start. The pinned Ibis has 27,000 m base range, 475 scan resolution, signature radius 52 and three hull target slots. Long Range Targeting and Signature Analysis add five percent per trained level to range and resolution. The pilot's limit starts at two, adds Target Management and Advanced Target Management levels, and is capped by the hull. The seeded pilot's level-II range and scan skills yield 29,700 m and 522.5 resolution, with approximately 3.55 seconds to lock an Ibis. [CCP target-lock formula](https://developers.eveonline.com/docs/guides/useful-formulae/#target-lock-time). Attribute and skill IDs come from the build 3662168 catalog and are named in the corresponding enums.

A scoped handler owns pending scans and completed targets. A shared lock graph carries only ship IDs across connections; each connection emits its own `OnTarget("otheradd"/"otherlost", shipID, reason)` notifications. Range loss or a missing target cancels the scan or drops the lock. Beginning warp or a gate jump, docking, and disposing the connection remove outgoing and incoming locks. No locks are saved in the world database. Flight visibility and transit status reuse the existing Destiny implementation checked against [Carbon Destiny revision 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp). The notification envelope and nested UserError encoding reuse the existing Carbon Blue codec.

Selecting another pilot also calls `bountyProxy.GetBounties(set(ownerIDs))`. The pinned `bountySvc` expects a list of `(ownerID, bounty)` pairs; each bounty has `targetID` and `bounty` fields so it can populate its cache. Eden's local world has no bounties, so each requested owner receives zero. `GetMyKillRights` returns an empty list. The earlier unsupported lookup interrupted the Selected Item controls; these responses allow the native menu and target controls to finish loading.

**Live Windows clients.** Used a separate copy of the cargo/fitting test save. Selected Eden Pilot and checked **Ibis (Ibis)** in Inventory. Undocked Pilot and Wing at Jita IV – Moon 4 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant. On Pilot, right-clicked Wing at 1,406 m and chose **Lock Target**. The client showed the targeting countdown, then a target portrait labelled **Eden Wing [SAK]**, **Ibis**, **1,406 m**. Wing's Overview showed the yellow targeted indicator on Pilot. Pilot's menu changed to **Unlock Target**; choosing it removed the portrait and cleared Wing's indicator. Reacquired Wing, then docked Wing through its native station menu. Pilot remained in space while Wing's Overview entry, selection and target portrait disappeared. The host recorded AddTarget, RemoveTarget and the docking transition. The corrected build produced no target-manager or bounty-service exception in either client's captured log.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all 103 tests and formatting verification passed. Controlled-clock checks cover delayed completion, duplicate requests retaining their timer, cancellation, target disappearance before completion, slot consumption by pending scans, self and unavailable targets, docked and out-of-range rejection, skill-adjusted limits, both pilots' notifications, unlock, range loss, either ship's warp, docking, gate departure and disconnect cleanup. A protocol regression checks the bounty cache response and empty kill-right list.

## Persistent asteroid fields, November 2, 2026

The pinned catalog's `client/asteroidcountbytypeidbeltid` supplies ore type/count pairs for each belt anchor. Perimeter has three belts containing 93, 123 and 135 rocks (351 total); Jita has none. The fields contain Veldspar, Scordite and their grade variants. `client/types` supplies ore volume. These exports do not supply individual rock IDs, positions or remaining quantities. Eden generates those once per belt and saves them in world version 15. Its local field policy uses a 6–18 km ring with 4 km thickness, 5,000–15,000 m³ deposits, radius proportional to the square root of initial deposit volume, and stable IDs above the 32-bit range. These dimensions and deposits are Eden policy, not asserted stock-server values. Migration preserves pilot and inventory data. Quantity-zero rows remain saved and are omitted from the visible field, preventing regeneration after depletion or restart; no mining operation reduces those quantities yet.

Asteroids use Destiny rigid mode 11 and the massive flag, with local rather than global coordinates. Catalog celestial markers keep their global flag. Both reconnect snapshots and streamed `AddBalls` carry the raw ball bytes, slim items and damage dictionary; `RemoveBall` unloads rocks outside Eden's 250 km visibility range. Streaming does not reset the rest of the ballpark. The coordinate and serialization behavior was checked against [Carbon Destiny Ball.cpp, revision 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ball.cpp), with the existing AddBalls path checked against Thunkers.cpp at the same revision. The pinned `asteroid.pyj` selects graphics and rotation from type, radius and item ID; `asteroidBelt.pyj` supplies the belt marker's ambient effects. Asteroid types lack an explicit signature-radius attribute, so targeting uses dogma attribute 552's catalog default of 100.

The first full-field arrival exposed an unrelated transport limit: encrypted gameplay packets still inherited the 16 KiB startup bound. Sending the field exceeded that bound and disconnected the session. Gameplay frames and decompressed packets now have a separate 1 MiB Eden limit; the startup exchange retains 16 KiB, and unsupported high header flags remain rejected before allocation. The pinned `SocketGPS.pyj` delegates frame I/O to native `recvpacketoob`/`sendpacket`, and `machoNetTransport.pyj` accepts Blue data with optional zlib compression. The larger bound is a local resource limit, not a claim about the native server's maximum. No transport flags or encryption format changed.

**Live Windows client.** Used an isolated copy of the fitting/targeting save, with Eden Pilot placed near Perimeter's Jita gate for this acceptance fixture only. Inventory showed **Ibis (Ibis)** and the saved cargo/miner fit. The Mining overview listed the three belts. After correcting the frame bound and restarting host/client, a full snapshot loaded the 135-rock Perimeter X field. Warping to zero rendered surrounding rocks and showed nearby ore rows. A Veldspar at **6,056 m** completed its native lock countdown and appeared in the target portrait. Warping **13.9 AU** to Perimeter II cleared the lock and streamed its 93-rock field without disconnecting. The Mining overview showed Scordite at about **5.7 km** and Veldspar at about **6.2 km**. Restarting both host and client restored the same visible rocks and ranges; a SHA-256 comparison of all 351 saved rock rows was unchanged. Mining cycles, laser effects and ore collection remain unimplemented.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all 109 tests and formatting verification passed. New checks cover catalog counts, concurrent initialization, stable saved rocks, quantity-zero retention across reopening, default asteroid signature, local visibility and streamed removal/addition without SetState, long IDs and rigid-ball bytes, version-14 migration preserving pilot/cargo/grants, and encrypted snapshot/arrival packets for the catalog's largest 186-rock belt. Startup and gameplay limits also reject oversized or flagged frames before reading their bodies.

## Civilian mining cycles, November 2, 2026

Build 3662168's `godma` calls `dogmaIM.Activate(moduleItemID, "miningLaser", targetID, repeat)` and `Deactivate(moduleItemID, "miningLaser")`. A scoped handler owns each running cycle and uses monotonic elapsed time, so duplicate activation cannot pay capacitor twice or restart its timer. Activation requires the pilot's fitted, assembled, online Civilian Miner, a completed asteroid lock, a non-warping ballpark and a target inside the module's surface range. The persistence layer separately validates ownership, the active ship, system, range and deposit. Activation currently supports Civilian Miners on corvettes; other mining modules and hull modifiers require further work.

The pinned Civilian Miner has range attribute 54 = 10,000 m, duration 73 = 10,000 ms, mining amount 77 = 6 m³ and capacitor need 6 = 5 GJ. Mining and Astrogeology each apply a separate yield multiplier of `1 + 0.05 * trainedLevel`. Whole ore units are rounded down. Critical chance 5967 starts at 0.01; critical bonus yield 5969 starts at 2. Mining Precision increases chance by ten percent per level and Mining Exploitation increases bonus yield by five percent per level. A full-cycle success adds the bonus to normal yield without removing that bonus from the asteroid. These values and skill ranks were checked in `client/typedogma` and its effect modifiers; [CCP's Catalyst notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/catalyst-expansion-notes) confirm that critical bonus ore does not consume the deposit. This milestone rolls critical successes only for completed cycles. Manual deactivation returns whole units for the elapsed portion without a critical roll; this partial-cycle policy still needs comparison with the native server.

Each collection transaction reads the remaining deposit and free cargo space, clips extraction to both limits, adds to an owned ore stack or creates one, and subtracts only the extracted base units from the saved asteroid. Concurrent pilots serialize their writes to the same deposit. Capacity failure and invalid state cannot leave an ore-only or depletion-only write. Quantity-zero rows remain saved and disappear through the existing local `RemoveBall` path. Repeating cycles stop on full cargo, depletion, insufficient capacitor or lost target. Warp, docking, system departure and disconnect do not leave an offline mining job. A delayed tick completes one paid cycle and starts any next cycle at the current time rather than awarding unobserved catch-up cycles.

World version 16 adds `WorldShipEnergy(ShipId, Charge, UpdatedAt)`, initially empty for an existing save. A ship without an entry starts fully charged. Each paid activation saves charge and its UTC timestamp. Passive regeneration uses the pinned `godma.CreateChargedAttribute` and `GetChargeValue` curve: `capacity * (1 + (sqrt(charge/capacity) - 1) * exp(-5 * elapsedMilliseconds/rechargeMilliseconds))^2`. Capacitor Management adds five percent capacity per level; Capacitor Systems Operation reduces recharge time by five percent per level. Login snapshots include the regenerated charge. This milestone charges mining activation; warp capacitor consumption and capacitor warfare remain separate work.

The native HUD receives `OnGodmaShipEffect` and `OnModuleAttributeChanges`. A live check found that the godma state manager accepts an optional trailing `stall`, while `PlayerShip.OnGodmaShipEffect` accepts only eleven external arguments; notifications now use their common eleven-argument shape. `clientDogmaLocation.OnModuleAttributeChanges` unpacks eight fields per change, including wallclock time, so both capacitor and fitting-resource notifications now include that eighth value. Module info snapshots preserve a running mining effect. The client receives `OnSpecialFX` with the module ID/type, asteroid ID and `effects.Laser`; fitted module triples in ship slim items let observers fit the correct turrets. Immutable mining contacts carry beam start/stop to other pilot connections through the existing Destiny updates, whose ballpark format follows [Carbon Destiny revision 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp). Effect method signatures and turret selection come from the pinned Python client.

Returning from the belt exposed the earlier restriction that `CmdDock` accepted only the ship's saved departure station. Docking now accepts a catalog station in the current system within 2,500 m of the station/ship surfaces. The server checks the active ship, current system and range again in a transaction before saving the destination station and removing the space position. The session releases location-bound references and refreshes station identity and launch coordinates. Reconnecting and undocking use the newly saved station. This supports unloading locally after a gate trip instead of requiring a return to the original station.

`OnOreMined` supplies the dictionary expected by `oreMinedNotificationAdapter`: `oreType`, `quantity_added`, `quantity_removed`, `hasRewards`, `amountWasted`, `amountCritBonus`, `itemID` and `moduleItemID`. It drives the native mining message and mining signals. The inventory notification reuses the Carbon Blue DBRow codec. A new stack must include old location/flag zero in `OnItemChange`: quantity-only changes updated the cargo meter but did not insert the unknown stack into the container cache. Existing stacks retain their previous quantity. The same corrected insertion path applies to split cargo stacks and newly assembled fitting items.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all 117 tests and formatting verification passed. Mining tests cover cycle timing, duplicate activation, manual partial stops, auto-repeat, full cargo, depletion, lock loss, warp cancellation, unavailable modules, atomic extraction by competing pilots, critical bonus conservation, peer beam start/stop, active effect snapshots, native notification shapes, catalog skill modifiers, saved capacitor exhaustion/recharge and version-15 migration retaining the deposit. A further regression mines ore, docks in another system, unloads the same item ID, reopens the save and undocks at that station. Existing inventory and fitting tests continue to pass with the shared insertion correction.

**Live Windows client.** On an isolated copy of the field/fitting save, selected Eden Pilot and checked **Ibis (Ibis)** in Inventory. Locked Veldspar at **6,590 m** and pressed F1. The first cycle filled the remaining 5 m³ with **50 Veldspar** and stopped. After correcting new-stack insertion and making room in this fixture only, locked Scordite at **5,689 m**. The ship rendered its mining beam and the client displayed **You mined 46 units of Scordite**. Repeated cycles grew the stack to **133 Scordite** and stopped with less than one ore unit of space left. A host/client restart restored both ore stacks.

On the corrected notification build, prepared a nearby Veldspar deposit with ten remaining units in this isolated save only. Locking it at **6,195 m** and activating F1 lit the module's green active ring. Completion displayed **You mined 10 units of Veldspar**, grew cargo from 50 to **60 Veldspar**, stopped the beam and removed the depleted rock and target portrait. Its saved quantity remained zero after the next host/client restart. No mining effect-arity or module-attribute unpacking error recurred. Warped to **Perimeter II – Moon 1 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant**, then docked through the native menu after extending station support. The station scene and location panel loaded correctly; Inventory retained **133 Scordite**, **60 Veldspar** and the fixture's 18 spare miners at **116.0/125.0 m³**.

**Live unloading and restart follow-up.** Selected the Scordite stack explicitly before starting the drag, then moved it from Ibis cargo onto Item Hangar. This resolved the earlier input problem: the host received `@hangar.Add`, Scordite disappeared from cargo, and used cargo dropped from 116.0 to **96.0/125.0 m³**. Item Hangar immediately showed **133 Scordite**, while cargo retained 60 Veldspar and 18 spare miners. After restarting both host and client, the pilot returned to the same Perimeter station; Inventory showed the active **Ibis (Ibis)** and Item Hangar still showed all 133 Scordite. SQLite retained the same ore item ID at station 60003754, hangar flag 4. This completes live acceptance of the mine/dock/unload/restart loop. Critical successes and another pilot's beam are covered by automated checks rather than live observations in this milestone.


## Station ore reprocessing

Build 3662168's `reprocessing/ui/quotes.pyj`, `inputItemAdder.pyj`, `itemReprocessor.pyj`, `controller.pyj`, `reprocessingWnd.pyj` and `reprocessing/util.pyj` define this exchange. `eveMoniker.GetReprocessingManagerEx` binds `reprocessingSvc` to the station ID. `appConst.stationServiceReprocessingPlant` is service **5**, checked through `corpStationMgr.DoStandingCheckForStationService`. Eden validates the current docked station and its catalog operation's service list, and releases the reprocessing binding on location changes.

`GetQuotes(itemsByID, activeShipID)` receives a dictionary of item IDs to packed inventory rows, rather than a list of IDs in the native window. Eden reads those rows as inert data and uses only the dictionary keys; quantities, types, ownership and locations come from SQLite. The reply is `(stationTax, stationEfficiencyByType, quotesByItemID)`. The efficiency dictionary needs a genuine Python `None` key for its default. Quote KeyVals contain item/type IDs, `quantityToProcess`, `leftOvers`, `portions`, `efficiency`, `recoverables` and `totalISKCost`. Each recoverable exposes `typeID`, `client`, `unrecoverable` and `iskCost`.

Blue decoding now recognizes allowlisted `blue.DBRowDescriptor` reductions and the packed DBRow token. Descriptors specify numeric data length, null/bool bits and separately marshaled object columns. The reader validates the packed zero/literal runs and preserves the data without executing Python globals. Dictionary encoding retains Carbon's value-before-key order, including `None` keys. Wire behavior was checked against [Carbon Blue revision af0d65b, PyRowSet.cpp](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/PyRowSet.cpp) and [Marshal.cpp](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp).

Recipes come from `client/typematerials`; batch size and volume come from `client/types`. The station's `sde/npcStations` record supplies base efficiency and station take. For ores, efficiency is the station value multiplied by the trained Reprocessing, Reprocessing Efficiency and ore-specific skill factors, capped at one. Dogma attribute 379 supplies each skill's percentage per level; ore attribute 790 identifies its specialized skill. Each output is floored after multiplying its catalog quantity by complete portions and efficiency. Leftovers stay in the source stack. At station 60003754, base efficiency is 0.5 and station take is 0.05; one 100-unit Scordite batch has base materials 150 Tritanium and 110 Pyerite, yielding 75 and 55 without reprocessing skills.

The pinned build charges station take in **ISK**, using adjusted average material prices; it does not subtract station tax from mineral quantities. Its fixed-material helper substitutes zero when a price is missing. Eden's existing average-price response is empty, so this milestone reports zero ISK costs and does not debit the prototype wallet. Market pricing, wallet transactions, standing discounts, implants, structure rigs, equipment recycling and randomized output recipes remain separate work. Fixed ore recipes and personal cargo/hangars are the supported scope.

`Reprocess(itemIDs, fromLocation, ownerID, outputLocationID, outputFlagID)` recalculates the quote in a SQLite transaction. It validates the docked station, ownership, active ship, source and output containers, distinct IDs, complete batches, output integer limits and cargo capacity after consuming input. The transaction consumes ore, retains remainders, merges or creates mineral stacks and saves everything together. A stale or repeated request cannot reprocess an incomplete remainder or missing stack. The reply is the processed-ID list paired with actual output quantities by type; the native controller uses this to display its results panel. Inventory changes update ore quantity and insert new mineral stacks through the existing three-argument `OnItemChange`. No world-schema migration or starter grant is needed.


**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **127 tests** and formatting verification passed. Added coverage checks packed-row and None-key wire fixtures, malformed runs and descriptors, native quote/bind/result shapes, ignored client-supplied row fields, catalog station services, skill multipliers, whole-batch flooring, multiple stacks, output merging, complete consumption, leftovers after reopening, retry rejection, atomic overflow rollback, cargo capacity after consumption, foreign/remote items and containers, duplicate IDs, unsupported inputs and undocked calls.

**Live Windows client.** Continued the isolated mining/unloading save at Perimeter station 60003754. Selected Eden Pilot and verified **Ibis (Ibis)** in Inventory. Opened Reprocessing Plant and added the previously mined **133 Scordite** through the native ore menu. The quote showed **50.0%** efficiency, **75 Tritanium**, **55 Pyerite**, **1.30 m³** of output and **0.00 ISK** fees. Pressed **Reprocess** with Item Hangar as the destination. The native **Output results** panel showed both mineral quantities, and Item Hangar immediately showed **33 Scordite**, **75 Tritanium** and **55 Pyerite** at **6.3 m³** total. No reprocessing decode or result-panel exception occurred on the corrected build. Restarted both host and client; the same station, active Ibis and all three quantities remained visible. SQLite retained the original Scordite ID at quantity 33 and the two new mineral IDs at quantities 75 and 55. Reopened Reprocess for the saved 33 Scordite remainder: the native window marked it restricted, showed no mineral output and disabled Reprocess. Skill-enhanced yields, cargo output, complete-stack removal and multi-stack batches are automated coverage rather than live observations for this milestone.

## Immediate station market and personal wallet

World version 17 adds personal wallets in integer ISK cents, finite NPC station orders, a station-seeding marker and a durable trade ledger. Upgrading preserves existing inventory and skills and grants no money. `starter-market.json` is explicit Eden bootstrap policy, rather than a live Tranquility price feed or catalog base-price market. Each visited station with catalog market service 7 seeds once. Demand, stock, prices and issue dates persist; exhausted or expired orders do not regenerate. The Windows client guide lists the policy prices and quantities. A regional book includes seeded stations in that region; execution currently requires the pilot's docked station.

Build 3662168's `marketQuote.pyj`, `market.pyj`, `marketutil/const.pyj`, multi-sell window, buy window and personal wallet panels define the exchange. `marketProxy.GetOrders(typeID)` returns cached `(asks, bids)`, with genuine `carbon.common.script.sys.crowset.CRowset` reductions containing DBRows. The `dbutil.CRowset` GUID is not an importable constructor in this build: using it failed during native unmarshalling. The outer `carbon.common.script.net.objectCaching.CachedMethodCallResult` state is `(details, marshaledResult, version)`, with `versionCheck='always'`, `sessionInfo='regionid'` and a timestamp/version pair. A raw order pair leaves the native cache's version unset and prevents fresh quote selection. The order descriptor includes `issueDate`, duration, range, remaining/entered volume, region, system, station, owner, price and escrow fields. Existing Blue reductions and dictionary encoding follow [Carbon Blue revision af0d65b, Marshal.cpp](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp); the Python class names and state come from the pinned client's `crowset.pyj` and `objectCaching.pyj`.

Native **Sell This Item** sends `PlaceMultiSellOrder(itemKeyVals, False, 0, expectedBrokerFee)`. KeyVals carry item/type/station IDs, quantity and price, plus quote metadata. **Buy This Type** sends `PlaceBuyOrder(stationID, typeID, price, quantity, -1, 1, 0, False, expectedBrokerFee)`. Eden accepts immediate personal station trades, matches the saved NPC price against the client's limit, and validates ownership, type, source, quantity, available demand/stock and funds. Sales accept nonassembled stacks in the current hangar or active cargo; assembled and fitted items are rejected. Partial fills consume only available order volume. Multi-item operations, wallet updates, inventory changes, tax and ledger entries share one SQLite transaction. Invalid batches and integer overflow roll back all of them. Concurrent pilots cannot consume the same demand twice.

The packaged tax fallback is 8%, but this staged client's `market-transaction-tax` runtime flag evaluates to **7.5%**. Its native sell quote displayed 7.5% without Accounting trained. Eden pins this observed rate, with the packaged 11% reduction per Accounting level and cent rounding; local feature-flag hosting remains future work. External flag changes can require revisiting the pin. Immediate orders have no broker fee. The server rounds tax to cents before crediting the net amount. The stock preview rounds its unrounded tax and net independently, so a half-cent tax can make its displayed net one cent higher than the saved proceeds. Reprocessing's adjusted average-price feed is still absent, so introducing bid/ask orders does not change its documented zero-fee fallback.

Successful trades send `OnItemChange`, `OnAccountChange('cash', pilotID, balance)` and completed immediate `OnOwnOrdersChanged` rows to invalidate the native order/transaction caches. New purchased stacks also send `OnMarketItemReceived`. Fully consumed stacks are detached to the pinned `locationGraveyard=8`, retaining their last positive quantity in the notification. The native inventory service reconstructs old quantity but reads its separate stacksize to decide whether the old tile existed; zeroing both makes it skip removal. The database still deletes the consumed row. This correction is shared with complete ore consumption in reprocessing. The shared item-ID allocator now reads WorldItem's existing AUTOINCREMENT sequence instead of MAX(Id), including fitting splits; deleting the highest stack no longer permits a later purchase, split or extraction to reuse its ID. No additional schema change is needed.

`account.GetCashBalance(0)` returns the saved personal wallet. `GetEntryTypes` reads the pinned accounting-entry catalog, including localization IDs. `GetTransactions(1000, year, month, False)` accepts calendar months and `(None, None)` for the native Last 30 Days default. It derives gross market and separate tax journal entries from the durable ledger, retaining the balance after each entry. `marketProxy.CharGetTransactions(fromDate)` returns personal market trade dictionaries for the native Market Transactions panel. NPC-corporation pilots have no private player-corporation membership record, so `charMgr.GetPrivateInfoOnCorpChange` returns None; their currently unearned LP/EverMarks query returns an empty list. These lookups are required while constructing My Wallet. PLEX remains unavailable. Player listings, corporation trading, remote execution, price-history graphs and other market features remain unsupported.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **142 tests** and formatting verification passed. Market coverage checks buying with sale proceeds, saved stock and ledger balances, no repeated seeding or initial cash grant, Accounting skill reductions, authoritative prices, selection gating, native cache/notification/journal shapes, the Last 30 Days filter, invalid prices and batches, insufficient funds, ownership and station validation, assembled/fitted/space rejection, partial fills, expiry, concurrent demand, overflow rollback, never reusing consumed item IDs after restart, and version-16 migration preserving ore.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on an isolated copy of the prior mining/reprocessing save, selected **Eden Pilot**, and verified **Ibis (Ibis)** at Perimeter station 60003754. The wallet started at zero. Sold **75 Tritanium at 2.00 ISK** through the native Immediate sale and confirmation: 150.00 gross, 11.25 tax, 138.75 proceeds. Sold **55 Pyerite at 8.00 ISK**: 440.00 gross, 33.00 tax, 407.00 proceeds. After correcting the wallet's membership/LP lookups and Last 30 Days request, My Wallet displayed the saved balance; its Overview showed **590.00 ISK income** and **44.25 ISK expenses**. Transactions displayed separate market and SCC tax rows. Market Transactions showed both types, quantities, prices, Caldari Navy and the correct station.

Opened **Buy This Type** on Civilian Miner. The native buy dialog showed **100.00 ISK**, quantity one and **100 available**. Confirmed its regional-average warning (history is currently absent); the wallet fell from 545.75 to **445.75 ISK**, and Item Hangar showed the new miner. Sold the remaining **33 Scordite at 5.00 ISK** to check complete-stack removal on the corrected build. Its icon disappeared immediately, leaving only the purchased miner and 5 m³ used. The ledger charged **12.38 ISK** tax, credited **152.62 ISK** and saved **598.37 ISK**. The stock preview independently displayed 152.63 net, exposing the documented half-cent display difference.

Used **Fit to Active Ship** on the purchased miner, then opened Fitting using the station's **circled ship** button. Both high slots showed Civilian Miners. Restarted host and client against the same save: Inventory still showed the active Ibis and empty local Item Hangar, Fitting retained both miners, My Wallet retained its balance, and Market Transactions listed all **four trades**, including the red 100 ISK purchase. SQLite independently confirmed **59,837 cents**, purchased item **2000000000007** in high slot 28 with online state true, **99** remaining Civilian Miners and the reduced mineral/ore demand. The sold rows remained absent; the station was not seeded again. No wallet, market or inventory exception appeared in the final client log. Open transaction panels refresh when reopened; live skill-modified tax, partial fills, competing pilots and multi-item batches remain automated coverage rather than observed UI actions.

**Item-ID follow-up.** Final review found that MAX(Id) could reuse a consumed stack's ID despite the table's saved sequence. After fixing the shared allocator and passing all 142 tests, reopened the native Civilian Miner buy dialog: it retained **99 available**. Bought a second miner for 100 ISK; Item Hangar showed it immediately and the wallet fell to **498.37 ISK**. SQLite assigned **2000000000009**, above the previously consumed high-water ID 2000000000008, and stock fell to **98**. Restarted both processes once more: the new miner remained visible in Item Hangar, My Wallet retained the balance, Market Transactions listed all five trades, and the saved item ID and stock remained unchanged.

## Mining upgrades and regional trade history, November 2, 2026

Regular mining lasers in catalog group 54 now use the same activation, partial-cycle, critical, capacitor and deposit rules as the civilian laser on corvettes. Skill requirements are checked at fitting, activation and collection through the shared saved-skill reader. No training or ISK is granted for an upgrade. Other hull modifiers, strip/deep-core miners and crystals remain unsupported.

The pinned build's Miner I (483) has 10,000 m range, 15,000 ms duration, 10 m³ base yield, 5 GJ capacitor cost, 60 CPU and 2 power grid, and requires Mining I. Miner II (482) has 12,000 m range, 15,000 ms duration, 15 m³ yield, 18 GJ cost, 80 CPU and 4 power grid, and requires Mining IV. These values were read from `client/types` and `TypeAttribute` in the verified build-3662168 catalog. They differ from older mining-cycle values. Eden's starter policy adds 100 units of each at 1,000 and 5,000 ISK respectively. Seeding now adds missing `(typeID, bid)` offers at visited stations, retaining existing empty or expired orders, quantities, prices and issue dates. World version remains 17.

Opening Regional Market exposed the native `marketProxy.GetHistoryForManyTypeIDs(set(typeIDs))` startup call. Build 3662168's `eve/client/script/ui/services/marketsvc.pyj` updates its history cache from a type-ID dictionary whose values are `(oldHistory, newHistory)`. Both are native CRowsets with `historyDate` I8, `lowPrice` R8, `highPrice` R8, `avgPrice` R8, `volume` I8 and `orders` I4. `GetOldPriceHistory(typeID)` returns completed UTC days; `GetNewPriceHistory(typeID)` returns today. `GetPlexHistory()` supplies the same pair for PLEX 44992 with no executions. The client constructs market groups/search from local static data through `eve/client/script/ui/shared/market/quote.pyj`; no fabricated group service is needed. Source is the pinned `code.ccp` SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. The existing Blue DBRow/CRowset codec is unchanged; its Carbon reference revision remains `af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997`.

History aggregates actual durable NPC executions for the pilot's current region over up to 365 completed UTC days plus today, excluding future timestamps. Each UTC day's low/high are execution prices, average is weighted by quantity using decimal cents before wire conversion, volume counts units, and orders counts executions. It includes other local pilots' trades in the region. Untraded types have empty rowsets; Eden does not invent market activity. The native UI supplies its own missing-day and initial-price fallback. Its first-trade warning can therefore compare a new 1,000 ISK laser offer against a 1 ISK fallback. This regional history is separate from the still-missing adjusted material-price feed used for reprocessing fees.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **147 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New coverage checks Miner I/II cycle times, skill-adjusted whole-unit yield, capacitor costs, saved ore, Miner II rejection below Mining IV at fitting and activation, adding offers without restocking existing orders, regional/day filtering, shared-pilot and quantity-weighted history, restart persistence, and native bulk/PLEX row shapes and encoding.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill with an isolated copy of the verified market save, selected **Eden Pilot**, and checked **Ibis (Ibis)**. The wallet began at **498.37 ISK**. Opened Regional Market, searched `miner`, expanded Ship Equipment and selected Miner I: the local offer showed **100 units at 1,000.00 ISK**. Undocked, warped to Perimeter II – Asteroid Belt 1, locked Veldspar at **6,107 m** and ran both civilian lasers. Cargo rose from 60 to **350 Veldspar**, filling the hold. Returned to station 60003754 and unloaded it to Item Hangar; the native Sell This Item menu was available there. Sold all 350 at **3.00 ISK**: **1,050.00** gross, **78.75** tax and **971.25** net, leaving **1,469.62 ISK**.

Double-clicked the Miner I station offer, bought one and confirmed its first-trade price warning. The new miner appeared immediately in Item Hangar. Price History rendered a graph and a table row for **2026.11.02**, **one order**, **quantity one**, and **1,000.00 ISK** low/high/average. Opened Fitting with the station's **circled ship** icon, unfitted the first civilian laser, and used Fit to Active Ship on Miner I. It occupied the first high slot and came online; remaining CPU changed from **32 to 37**. Its fitting tooltip showed the base **10 m³ per 15 seconds**. Fitting's skill-adjusted preview remains incomplete; the in-space HUD showed the server's effective yield rounded to **12 m³ per 15 seconds**.

Undocked again, returned to the same belt, locked Veldspar at **6,107 m** and activated only **F1 (Miner I)**. The mining beam appeared. Cargo, initially containing no ore, reached **345 Veldspar**, then **350** at capacity, and the laser stopped. Restarted host and client against the same isolated save: Inventory retained the active Ibis and 350 Veldspar, the HUD retained Miner I, the history table retained its single execution, and Market Data showed **99** Miner I remaining. SQLite confirmed **46,962 wallet cents**, purchased Miner I item **2000000000010** online in high slot **27**, new ore stack **2000000000011**, retained civilian stock **98**, and reduced Veldspar demand **999,650**. The restarted client's log contained no market, wallet, inventory, dogma or mining exception. Miner II activation and skill rejection, weighted multi-price history and older-day history remain automated coverage; this live run used Miner I and today's executions.


## Venture ownership, boarding and mining holds

The starter market now offers ten Venture hulls (32880) per visited market station for 10,000 ISK each. This is Eden's fixed progression policy, below the catalog's 300,000 ISK base price. Existing wallets receive no grant. Packaged hulls are owned inventory stacks; assembling one atomically removes a unit and creates an owned WorldShip with its name and station. A single packaged unit retains its item ID; splitting a stack allocates a new ID. Both WorldItem and WorldShip AUTOINCREMENT sequences participate in the shared allocator, including character creation, so assembling, consuming or deleting an item cannot make another entity reuse its ID. World schema remains 17.

Pinned `eve/client/script/ui/services/menuSvcExtras/invItemFunctions.pyj` sends `ship.AssembleShip(list(itemIDs), name=..., subSystems=None)`. The response is a list of assembled ship IDs. Eden supports Venture assembly, validates a distinct local owned packaged selection and the native 32-character name limit, and sends singleton/stack `OnItemChange` notifications. Retrying an assembled ID returns that ship without consuming another stack unit. Other purchased hulls, repackaging and assembled-ship trading remain unsupported.

`eve/client/script/ui/station/base.pyj` and `eve/client/script/dogma/clientDogmaLocation.pyj` activate an assembled ship through `ship.Board(shipID, oldShipID)`, returning `(instanceCache, instanceFlagQuantityCache, wbData, heatStates)`. Boarding validates ownership, both hulls at the same docked station, the previous active identity and all catalog skill requirements. The saved active ship changes without relocating either hull's cargo, modules or energy. The session sends its ship-ID change even though station/location stay the same, retains station service bindings, and rebuilds the selected flight context from the new hull. Ship inventory references bind to `@ship:<shipID>`; a cached old-ship inventory never aliases the new ship. Inactive owned local inventories remain readable; item mutations require the active hull. Ship Hangar's `itemShip.pyj` calls inventory `ListByFlags(flags=list(...))`; the response iterates ordinary inventory DBRows for those flags.

The Venture's catalog values include 50 m³ general cargo, attribute 1556 (`generalMiningHoldCapacity`) of 5,000 m³, and `flagGeneralMiningHold=134`. Mining automatically uses that hold; ordinary cargo capacity does not reduce it. Only asteroid-category ore may enter it. Docked moves, immediate sale requests and reprocessing accept the active hold, while reprocessed minerals go to ordinary cargo or Item Hangar. The pinned native menu omits Sell This Item for Mining Hold: unload to Item Hangar to sell through that UI. For direct hold reprocessing, select Item Hangar as output. Catalog effects 5058 and 5139 multiply ore-laser yield by hull attribute 207 (2), then apply attribute 1842 (5 percent) per trained Mining Frigate level (32918), in addition to Mining/Astrogeology. A Mining III / Mining Frigate I Miner I cycle therefore yields floor(10 × 1.15 × 2 × 1.05 / 0.1) = 241 Veldspar. Gas, boosts and unrelated hull modifiers remain separate work.

During this acceptance, the native docking navigator repeated `CmdWarpToStuff('item', stationID, minRange=0)` while the first order was aligning/in warp. Eden now acknowledges a repeat of the same target and range without restarting the course or emitting another warp effect; retargeting remains rejected during warp. This prevents an unsupported-bound-call dialog from cancelling native automatic docking.

Gameplay call shapes, flags, hull attributes and effect operators were read from build 3662168's packaged scripts and verified catalog. `code.ccp` SHA-256 remains `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Blue reductions and Destiny formats reuse the existing implementations checked against Carbon Blue `af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997` and Carbon Destiny `27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f`; this milestone changes neither format.


**Automated checks, November 2, 2026.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **154 tests** and formatting verification passed. Coverage includes buying and splitting packaged hull stacks, assembly retry identity and atomic rollback on an invalid batch, name limits, saved active hulls and names, ownership/station/skill/space boarding rejection, distinct bound inventories and filtered flag reads, local owned ship names without exposing another pilot's hangar, shared item/ship allocation, separate cargo and mining-hold capacity, ore-only input, hold unloading, mining with full ordinary cargo, Venture yield and saved extraction, hold sales, reprocessing from either hold into capacity-checked ordinary cargo, and idempotent warp retries without additional effects.

Selected Eden Pilot with the saved Ibis, 350 Veldspar, 18 spare miners and its two fitted lasers. Returned to Perimeter II – Moon 1 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant. Regional Market search `venture` showed **10 at 10,000.00 ISK**. Bought one, leaving **10,000 ISK**, assembled it through Ship Hangar and named it **prospector**. Double-clicking it changed the active hull and rendered the Venture.

The inactive Ibis's cargo still displayed **350 Veldspar and 18 Civilian Miners**, filling **125/125 m³**. Boarded the Ibis again and opened Fitting with the circled ship icon: Miner I and Civilian Miner remained fitted, with **37/162 CPU available**. Unfitted Miner I into Item Hangar, returned to the Venture and fitted the same item to its first high slot.

Undocked the Venture, warped to Perimeter II – Asteroid Belt 1, locked Veldspar at **5,156 m** and activated F1. The laser beam appeared and Mining Hold reached **723 Veldspar / 72.3 m³** after three full cycles, agreeing with **241 per cycle**. A fourth full cycle and a manually stopped partial cycle left **1,171 ore / 117.1 m³**, while ordinary cargo remained separate. Dragged that remainder to Item Hangar, verified both stacks, and dragged the ore back into Mining Hold.

Restarted host and client on the final build. Inventory retained the active **prospector (Venture)**, **71 Veldspar / 7.1 of 5,000 m³**, and **2,200 Tritanium** in Item Hangar. Fitting retained Miner I and displayed **228/288 CPU available**, **52/54 power grid available**, **335 m/s**, and **16.50 km** targeting. SQLite confirmed active hull **2000000000012**, Miner I **2000000000010** online in flag **27**, ore **2000000000013** in flag **134**, wallet **1,000,000 cents**, and **9** Venture offers left. The corrected mining/docking/reprocessing run and restart had no assembly, boarding, inventory, dogma, mining or reprocessing exception. An unrelated periodic `pingService.Ping` remains unsupported; optional external gateway calls also remain unavailable. Capacity exhaustion and foreign/untrained/remote/space rejection are automated coverage; this live run used a single Venture and Miner I, and direct hold sales were not offered by the native menu.


## Combat calculation groundwork, November 2, 2026

A pure calculation layer now combines turret range and angular tracking penalties, evaluates shot quality from an explicit random draw, and applies the four damage types through shield, armor and structure. Each layer consumes only the raw damage needed to exhaust it before the remaining original damage mixture meets the next layer's own resonances. An intact immune layer stops damage; an empty immune layer does not. Damage and health inputs reject negative or nonfinite values.

The accuracy model is `0.5^(trackingPenalty² + rangePenalty²)`, with `trackingPenalty = angularVelocity * signatureReference / (tracking * targetSignature)` and `rangePenalty = max(0, distance - optimal) / falloff`. Zero tracking or falloff cannot produce NaN at their boundaries. The [CCP accuracy discussion](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/upcoming-balance-changes) describes signature scaling and 50% accuracy at one falloff or matching angular/tracking velocity. It is historical context, not proof of the current server implementation. Eden's initial shot-quality model uses the same draw for hit selection and quality: a successful draw below 0.01 does triple damage, otherwise the multiplier is draw + 0.49. This RNG policy has not been verified against Tranquility's server.

Build 3662168 catalog type 3638, Civilian Gatling Railgun, belongs to Energy Weapon group 53 despite its name. It has a built-in charge (attribute 127, type 20132), a 2,000 ms cycle, 4,800 m optimal, 2,000 m falloff, tracking 500, signature reference 40,000 m, damage multiplier 1.25 and base damage 3 kinetic / 2 thermal. Named enums now identify the relevant weapon, skill, damage, resonance and effect IDs. These are definitions for subsequent integration; this milestone does not fit or activate guns, spawn NPCs, save combat damage, or pay rewards. It changes no Blue or Destiny wire formats; the existing Carbon references remain unchanged.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **158 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New tests cover resistance-aware overflow across three layers, fully resisted hits and misses, health fractions, invalid input, combined range/tracking penalties, and hit-quality boundaries. **Live acceptance:** no player-facing behavior changes in this milestone, so no combat action was performed in the client. Weapon effects, health updates and encounters still require native client acceptance after integration.


## Saved ship health, November 2, 2026

World version 18 adds `WorldShipHealth`, keyed by owned ship ID with shield charge, armor damage, structure damage and a Windows FILETIME. Missing rows mean an undamaged hull; migration grants no damage, repairs, equipment or ISK. Armor and structure store damage amounts so changing maximum HP through skills retains existing damage. Shield charge uses the same elapsed-time curve as capacitor, now shared through `ChargedResource`. Reads do not repeatedly save recharge or heal armor/structure. The internal damage primitive participates in its caller's world transaction and is not exposed as a client RPC. Destruction, weapon activation, repairs and NPC attacks remain separate work.

Defense attributes include Mechanics (3392), Hull Upgrades (3394) and Shield Management (3419), each adding 5% per trained level to structure, armor and shields. Shield Operation (3416) reduces shield recharge duration 5% per level. These use build 3662168's skill attributes and effects: Mechanics 251/392, Hull Upgrades 270/271, Shield Management 280/446, and Shield Operation 283/486. Hull resistances come from the pinned type's dogma attributes; omitted resonance values default to one while an explicit zero means immunity.

Native dogma priming now sends saved structure damage (attribute 3), armor damage (266) and current shield charge (264). `dogma/const.pyj` defines `dgmAttributesByIdx`: online at index 1, structure damage at 2, capacitor charge at 3, skill points at 4, armor damage at 5, shield charge at 6 and incapacitated at 7. `GetAllInfo.shipState` supplies those same saved values for the active ship, rather than hardcoded undamaged instances. Ship capacities retain the skill-adjusted values instead of being overwritten by raw hull data. Ballpark SetState/AddBalls include ship damage fractions; `eveInflight/damageStateValue.pyj` expects `((shieldFraction, tau), armorFraction, structureFraction)`. Tau is the recharge duration divided by five in milliseconds, and `dogma.const.dgmTauConstant=10000` converts FILETIME ticks to milliseconds for client interpolation. No ball byte layout changed. The pinned code.ccp SHA-256 and Carbon Blue/Destiny references remain those recorded above.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **164 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Coverage adds durable layer damage and shield recharge, clock rollback, invalid damage rollback, concurrent hits without lost updates, catalog immunity and skill modifiers, keeping damage on the original hull while boarding another, dogma and Destiny payloads, and version-17 migration retaining ships, items, wallet and capacitor. Existing migration fixtures now remove the newer health table when reconstructing their historical schemas.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `health-20261102`, copied from the verified Venture save. The server migrated it to version 18. Added an explicit acceptance fixture for Venture 2000000000012: 75 shield charge, 100 armor damage and 100 structure damage at the current FILETIME. This was injected test damage, not a combat encounter. Selected Eden Pilot; Inventory retained **prospector (Venture)** and **71 Veldspar / 7.1 m³** in Mining Hold. The ship visibly burned in the hangar. Fitting's Defense section showed trained maxima of **247 hp shield, 192 hp armor and 230 hp structure** (that panel truncates). Undocked; the health tooltip rounded to **248 shield, 193 armor and 230 structure** maximum and showed **93 armor (48%)**, **130 structure (57%)** and **145 shield (58%)** while recharging. The damaged model burned in space.

Restarted both host and client on the final build. The Venture and ore remained present, with **93/193 armor (48%)** and **130/230 structure (57%)** unchanged. Shields had recovered to **213/248 (86%)** while offline and continued recharging. SQLite retained the same original damage row and timestamp; a read/restart did not rewrite it as full health. No ship-health, dogma or damage-state exception appeared in either client log. Optional external gateway features and the existing periodic ping service remain unsupported. Other hull switching and concurrent damage are automated checks; live damage from weapons, NPC retaliation, destruction and rewards are not yet implemented.


## Persistent belt NPC contacts, November 2, 2026

World version 19 adds `WorldNpc`: durable NPC ID, belt/system/type, position, shield charge, armor/structure damage, health timestamp and an optional defeat timestamp. Eden's initial placement policy creates one catalog **Guristas Rookie (30380)** 3,500 m along X from each Perimeter (30000144) belt anchor. Guristas corporation 1000127 owns them. These are local encounter placements, not catalog spawn tables or a reproduction of high-security belt aggression. Concurrent readers serialize initialization through a world transaction and keep the same IDs. Reconnect does not heal armor/structure, replace defeated rows or pay ISK. Shield recharge advances from the saved timestamp. Movement, attacks, defeat/respawn lifecycle, wrecks and rewards still require integration.

The pinned type is group **286 Minor Threat**, category **11 Entity**, with 1,500,100 kg mass, 50 m radius, 440 m/s maximum speed, 20 shield, 15 armor, 20 structure, 33 m signature and 2,000,000 ms shield recharge. Shield/armor resonances are EM 0.7, explosive 0.8, kinetic 1 and thermal 0.9. The type omits agility and its catalog default is zero; the stationary free ball uses an explicit local inertia of 1 pending NPC movement. Build 3662168's `entityShip.pyj.LoadModel` reads `gfxTurretID` and `entityMissileTypeID` from godma's local type catalog. Its `FitHardpoints` fits the entity turret and keys it by the NPC's own ball ID. Eden sends entity slim items with catalog type/group/category, NPC corporation owner, name, null character ID and an empty player-module list.

NPCs use local free-ball STOP records, with mass, corporation, maximum velocity, zero current velocity, agility and formation fields, rather than rigid global celestial records. The existing full-state stream framing follows [Carbon Destiny Thunkers.cpp at 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp), particularly its old-style free-ball arguments and AddBalls loading. The pinned client's EntityShip handling supplies model behavior; Blue serialization continues to use the previously recorded Carbon Blue revision. The code.ccp SHA-256 is unchanged.

Snapshots include NPC slim items and health fractions; streamed AddBalls/RemoveBall add and unload nearby contacts at 250 km without replacing the whole ballpark. Only contacts in the pilot's current system and visibility range can be targeted or named through GetMultiLocationsEx. Native target locks use catalog signature and the existing scan-time, range and target-limit checks. Leaving visibility clears the lock. CmdFollowBall now accepts a visible NPC for Approach/Keep at Range. A live check exposed the belt marker being treated as solid: belt anchors are now nonmassive in the wire record and excluded from server collision/recovery tests. Asteroid rocks, stations and other solid catalog bodies keep their existing behavior.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **171 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New coverage checks shared persistent identity across simultaneous loads and restart, correct catalog stats, retaining damage and defeated entries, free-ball/slim/health wire fields, native lock completion and removal, entering visibility without replacing the ballpark, following across a virtual belt marker, and version-18 migration retaining saved ship health. Existing historical migration fixtures remove the newer NPC table before reconstructing older versions.


**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `combat-20261102`, copied from the undamaged verified Venture save. Selected Eden Pilot and checked Inventory: **prospector (Venture)** with **71 Veldspar / 7.1 m³** in Mining Hold. Undocked and warped to Perimeter II – Asteroid Belt 1. The Mining Overview showed one red **Guristas Rookie**, settling at **4,721 m** after warp coast-down. Its selected-item panel displayed the catalog's **3,000 ISK bounty**. Lock Target completed and showed its portrait, **[G]** corporation ticker and full health rings. Look at rendered the native Rookie ship model. No firing or bounty payment occurred.

The first Approach stopped at **3,451 m** against the invisible belt marker. After the marker collision correction, rebuilt and restarted host/client. The same NPC returned at the saved position, with no duplicate; Approach crossed the marker and closed to about **100 m**. SQLite retained exactly three NPC IDs, **4000040009232**, **4000040009245** and **4000040009249**, one for each Perimeter belt. The active Venture, its ore and **1,000,000 wallet cents** remained unchanged.

For a separate health-display check, stopped both processes, set the first NPC to **10 shield charge, 5 armor damage and 5 structure damage** at the current FILETIME, and restarted on the final build.
