## Civilian weapon firing, NPC destruction and bounties, November 2, 2026

World version 20 adds `WorldWeaponCooldown` and `WorldNpcBounty`. Migration grants one Civilian Gatling Railgun to each existing pilot at the active ship's saved station, retaining inventory, wallets, health, capacitor and NPC records. Newly created pilots receive the same gun with their starter miner. This universal gun grant is explicit Eden policy; race-specific civilian equipment and replacement corvettes remain separate work. Type **3638** is unpublished and has no market group in this catalog, so this milestone exposes the owned item through Inventory rather than fabricating a market listing.

The pinned catalog defines Civilian Gatling Railgun as **Energy Weapon group 53**, with required Gunnery I, effect **10 `targetAttack`**, built-in charge **20132**, 2,000 ms base cycle, 4,800 m optimal, 2,000 m falloff, tracking 500 and signature reference 40,000 m. It deals 3 kinetic and 2 thermal damage with a 1.25 multiplier, costs 1 GJ, and uses 4 CPU and 6 power grid. Despite its name, its requirement does not select Small Hybrid Turret bonuses. Catalog effects 414/582, 290, 2847, 587, 287 and 298 select Gunnery/Rapid Firing cycle reductions, Sharpshooter optimal, Motion Prediction tracking, Surgical Strike damage, Controlled Bursts capacitor reduction and Trajectory Analysis falloff. The fitting preview and firing calculation share these adjusted attributes. Other civilian guns and ammunition-fed weapons are not enabled yet.

One selected-pilot handler owns `dogmaIM.Activate` and `Deactivate`, dispatching `miningLaser` and `targetAttack` to their scoped cycle controllers. A turret shot is resolved at activation, after checkpointing motion and checking a completed visible NPC lock. Repeat cycles use the session clock; they do not catch up missed offline cycles. Manual deactivation stops after the paid cycle. A saved UTC cooldown prevents reconnects from firing the same module early. The server checks ownership, online state, fitted high slot, required skills, active ship, system, targeting range and capacitor. Accuracy uses surface range and transverse angular velocity against the stationary target; resistance-aware overflow retains the original damage mixture between layers. Shot-quality randomness remains the documented initial Eden model, rather than a claim about Tranquility's private server RNG.

Each shot atomically spends the shared ship capacitor, saves the module cooldown and applies NPC shield, armor and structure damage. The fatal shot also records destruction, increments the wallet in integer cents and inserts a bounty row with a unique NPC identity. A competing shot cannot pay the same bounty twice; overflow rolls back damage, resources, cooldown and payment together. Defeated rows remain durable. The local encounter policy creates a replacement with a new AUTOINCREMENT identity after **600 seconds**, without resetting the defeated row or its payment.

Wire behavior was checked against build 3662168's `eve/client/script/remote/michelle.pyj`, `damageStateValue.pyj`, weapon effects and wallet formatters in `code.ccp` (SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`). `ClientBallpark.OnDamageStateChange(shipID, damageState)` is a **Destiny action**: it stores the state with the simulation timestamp and notifies the health UI. Sending it through the ordinary notification service would bypass that ballpark owner. Hits therefore update health without replacing the entire ballpark. `RemoveBall(ballID, terminal)` sets the native explosion destruction effect when terminal is true. Weapon cycles pair `OnGodmaShipEffect` effect 10 with `OnSpecialFX` using `effects.Laser`, turret mode, the module/type IDs and its built-in charge. These Python callbacks extend the existing Destiny envelope; Carbon Destiny reference remains [revision 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f), and the unchanged Blue codec reference remains [revision af0d65b](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/tree/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997).

Build 3662168's `godma.OnDamageMessage` forwards a dictionary to `logger.AddCombatMessageFromDict` in `eve/client/script/ui/shared/uilog.pyj`. Health updates and turret effects alone do not create damage numbers. Eden now sends `OnDamageMessage` for each resolved shot with applied `damage`, integer `hitQuality`, `isBanked=False`, `attackType='me'`, target ball ID, weapon type ID and a dogma-attribute damage dictionary for the native icons. The client localizes quality codes 0 through 6 and rounds the amount for display. The labels' damage bands follow Eden's initial shot-quality model. The session sends combat text before pending Destiny removals so the fatal hit can still resolve the NPC's slim item and name. Duplicate activation and manual deactivation do not create additional shot messages. A session-level regression test decodes the outgoing envelopes and verifies that the fatal message precedes terminal target removal.

The catalog's Guristas Rookie bounty is **3,000 ISK**. Successful kills send `OnAccountChange('cash', pilotID, balance)`. The personal `account.GetTransactions` journal uses entry type **85 Bounty Prizes**, payer **CONCORD 1000125**, recipient pilot and the solar-system ID as the reference argument. The pinned `walletEntryFormat.pyj` formatter resolves that argument as a location; individual bounty type 17 uses a different argument meaning. Eden pays immediately per kill with no fleet sharing or bounty tax. This is local policy, not Tranquility's payout aggregation schedule.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **184 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Added coverage checks native activation and journal shapes, cycle-start damage, repeat/stop and target-loss behavior, restart cooldowns, range misses, angular geometry, gunnery modifiers, capacitor rejection, ownership/online/skill/system/space restrictions, no player targeting, one payment under competing fatal shots, atomic wallet-overflow rollback, terminal removal, delayed replacement identity and migration without repeated grants. Existing mining, fitting, fleet, market and reprocessing tests continue to run with both starter items.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill with an isolated copy of the verified Venture save, allowing migration from version 17 to 20. Selected **Eden Pilot**, checked **prospector (Venture)** in Inventory with its saved **71 Veldspar** in Mining Hold, and fitted the granted railgun through **Fit to Active Ship**. The native Fitting view showed both the Miner I and civilian gun online, **3.7 dps**, 224/288 CPU and 46/54 power grid. Undocked, warped to Perimeter II – Asteroid Belt 1, locked the Rookie at **4,721 m** and pressed **F2**. The gun's active indicator and target weapon icon appeared; repeated shots visibly reduced the target's health rings. The Rookie then disappeared from Overview and the target area, and the gun stopped. My Wallet increased from **10,000 to 13,000 ISK**. Transactions showed one **Bounty Prizes** row for **3,000 ISK**, balance **13,000 ISK**, and the native description “Eden Pilot got bounty prizes for killing pirates in Perimeter.” SQLite independently retained one bounty, a fully destroyed original NPC, the other two undamaged NPCs and the same fitted online gun. No relevant godma, effect, damage-state or wallet exception appeared in the client log. Restarted both host and client against the same save: Inventory retained the active Venture and 71 Veldspar, the HUD retained both fitted modules, My Wallet retained 13,000 ISK and its single bounty entry, and the defeated Rookie remained absent before its respawn delay. At Perimeter VIII – Asteroid Belt 1, locked the second Rookie at 2,775 m and selected Look at before firing. A weapon beam, bright impacts, fire and smoke rendered on its hull as the health rings fell. It was then removed and the camera returned to the Venture. The brief transition did not establish a separate explosion animation. The saved wallet reached 16,000 ISK with two distinct bounty rows. The first belt later received a replacement with a new ID after its delay; that replacement was initially confirmed in persistence rather than the client. These two kills preceded the damage-message correction, whose live verification is recorded below. NPC retaliation, player destruction/recovery, wrecks and loot remain unimplemented.


**Damage-message and mining follow-up.** After adding the separate combat event, restarted both processes and locked the replacement Rookie at Perimeter VIII. Native blue damage popups appeared with amounts including **9** and **4**, kinetic/thermal icons, the target name and localized quality text such as **Smashes**, **Penetrates**, **Hits** and **Glances Off**. The pilot confirmed the messages worked. The replacement died and the wallet reached **19,000 ISK** with a third bounty for its distinct saved identity. No combat-message formatting or missing-target exception appeared. The continuing green ring in the reported screenshot was identified by its tooltip as **Miner I (F1)**; it was collecting Scordite, while the railgun to its right was inactive. No weapon-stop code change was made for that observation.

The pilot also reported missing mining feedback. The native Log and Messages settings already had **Mining Messages** checked. Added notification-name tracing, restarted the host and client, locked Scordite at **4,859 m** and activated Miner I. A full cycle increased Scordite from **1,354 to 1,515** and the native log displayed **You mined 161 units of Scordite**. The host trace showed `OnItemChange`, `OnOreMined` and the cycle effect notifications. The pilot then confirmed the mining messages were working. The existing `OnOreMined` payload was retained; the earlier session's missing display was not isolated to a reproducible server defect. Restore, build, all **184 tests** and formatting verification passed on this build. The new trace records event names without notification contents.

## Native recovery corvettes, November 2, 2026

Build 3662168's `eve/client/script/ui/shared/dockedUI/corvetteButton.pyj` calls `station.CreateNewbieShip`. In `eve/client/script/ui/station/base.pyj`, that method requires a docked location and a current dogma ship item, refuses a corvette, and asks for confirmation when leaving a non-capsule hull. It calls `dogmaIM.CreateNewbieShip(previousShipID, locationID)` and ignores the return value. Eden implements this selected-pilot RPC in the fleet handler, returning Python None and sending the normal active-ship session delta. The pinned code.ccp SHA-256 remains `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. No Blue or Destiny format changes are involved; the previously recorded Carbon revisions remain applicable.

Recovery is one SQLite world transaction. The server checks the owned current ship, docked station and required skills, then reads `shipTypeID` from the pilot's catalog race. Eden's policy reuses the first same-race corvette at that station with undamaged armor/structure and an online Civilian Miner and Civilian Gatling Railgun in high slots 27 and 28. Shield charge is not reset. Stripped or damaged corvettes and ships elsewhere remain intact. If no ready hull exists, the server checks the kit against base high slots, turret hardpoints, CPU and power grid, then allocates one hull and two online singleton modules. No ISK or skills are granted. The previous hull, its contents and health remain saved. The universal railgun and reuse rules are explicit Eden policy, pending other racial weapon families. A repeated request naming the same previous owned hull returns the current recovery corvette without creating more items. Concurrent requests serialize through the transaction. World schema remains version 20.

Live investigation identified two requirements beyond persistence. `hangarView.GetShipItemFromHangar` resolves the active hull from cached station inventory during `ProcessActiveShipChanged`, so inventory additions must precede the ship session delta. Also, `invCache.OnItemChange` rejects any `ixQuantity` change when the new row's quantity is negative. Assembled hulls and modules use quantity -1, singleton 1 and stacksize 1. New singleton notifications now announce prior location/flag and stacksize zero without an `ixQuantity` marker. Ordinary stack notifications retain their quantity behavior. This avoids rejecting the new hull before the hangar can replace its model.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **190 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Added coverage checks zero-ISK recovery, an equipped online kit, preservation of the previous hull's name/cargo/damage and wallet, restart, reuse, competing and repeated requests, ownership/station/space/active-hull rejection, unsupported fitting rollback, selected-pilot routing and Python None. Session-level coverage checks that the three inventory additions precede the ship delta; singleton notifications are checked for stacksize without the rejected quantity marker.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `recovery-final-20261102`, copied from the verified combat/mining save. Prepared an explicit station-start fixture by removing only the active Venture's space-position row; this did not demonstrate travel or docking. The original save remained untouched. Selected **Eden Pilot** and checked active **prospector (Venture)** in Inventory at Perimeter II – Moon 1. Pressed **Board my Corvette** and accepted the native confirmation. Inventory changed to **Ibis (Ibis)**, the station played the Ibis boarding scene, and the button became disabled. Fitting showed both civilian modules online, 3.7 dps and stable capacitor. The earlier stale Venture model and negative-quantity errors were absent on the final build.

Opened Ship Hangar and boarded **prospector** again. The Venture's model returned, and Mining Hold retained **8,599 Scordite** and **71 Veldspar**, using **1,296.9 / 5,000 m³**. Pressed Board my Corvette again: the same Ibis **2000000000018** became active, with no additional hull or modules. My Wallet still showed **19,000 ISK**. Restarted both host and client on the same save; Inventory and the hangar retained the Ibis, Fitting retained both online modules, and the wallet tooltip retained 19,000 ISK. SQLite comparisons confirmed every pre-existing ship/item/wallet row unchanged, exactly one new hull and two modules, and schema 20. No recovery, active-ship, model-loading or negative-quantity exception appeared in the final run or restart log. Optional external gateway features remain unsupported. This live run covered the Caldari Ibis; other race hulls use their catalog definitions but were not tried in the client. NPC retaliation, player destruction and capsule escape remain separate milestones.

## Rookie retaliation and capsule escape, November 2, 2026

World version 21 adds `WorldNpcAggression` and `WorldShipLoss`, without granting items, repairs, skills or ISK. The aggression row has one NPC identity, one target hull, a saved next-shot deadline and optional last-shot time. Eden's initial AI retaliates against the first player to fire on a surviving Rookie; another attacker does not steal that target. Merely arriving does not aggress. The pinned Guristas Rookie type supplies attack range **247 = 3,125 m**, minimum reaction delay **475 = 7,500 ms**, cycle **51 = 2,500 ms**, optimal **54 = 500 m**, falloff **158 = 3,000 m**, tracking **160 = 0.365**, signature reference **620 = 40 m**, graphics turret **245 = 562**, and **2 kinetic + 2 thermal** damage with multiplier **64 = 0.625**. This uses the catalog's minimum delay rather than reproducing a private server AI delay distribution. NPCs remain stationary; pursuit and autonomous aggression are separate work.

NPC shots use the existing Eden accuracy/quality model, relative angular velocity, trained target defense, layer resonances and shield recharge. Ibis shield role effects **5008, 5009, 5011 and 5012** multiply shield resonances by attribute **1829**, whose -8 percent value produces a 0.92 multiplier. Impairor armor resistance uses the corresponding rookie attribute **1825**. These bonuses are multipliers on damage taken, not flat resistance points. The saved shot deadline advances from the current tick, so overdue cycles do not burst. A world transaction serializes competing NPC ticks, damage and fatal loss. Leaving attack range, warping, docking, disconnecting or defeating the NPC ends aggression. Reconnect clears stale aggression; a new player attack is required. Capsules are not NPC targets in this initial policy.

A fatal hit inserts a unique loss record with pilot, original hull identity/type/name, attacker, system, position, time, replacement capsule ID and an inventory manifest. The same transaction creates catalog Capsule **670 / group 29** at the saved ship position, changes the active hull, and removes the destroyed ship plus its fitted, cargo and mining-hold items. Armor/shield/energy state and module cooldowns cascade with the lost hull. The pilot's wallet, skills, hangar and other ships are retained. Eden currently consumes all contents of the lost hull; wrecks, salvage, loot rolls, insurance and native killmail records are not yet implemented. The manifest is a durable audit of that loss, not recoverable inventory. Failed capsule creation rolls the whole loss back. A competing fatal tick cannot create a second capsule or loss row.

The pinned client `entityShip.FitHardpoints` keys NPC turrets by the NPC ball ID. NPC `OnSpecialFX` therefore uses that ID as both source and module, the catalog graphics turret type, no charge, and `effects.Laser`. Saved last-shot data lets observers receive the active beam. Incoming `OnDamageMessage` uses `attackType='otherPlayer'`, the NPC `source`/`attackerID` ball ID, and `owner=(UE_TYPEID, npcTypeID)`. `uilog.AddCombatMessageFromDict` selects received-damage localization, whose owner field requires that typed NPC name. `eveexceptions.const` defines UE_TYPEID as 4. Omitting owner leaves unresolved localization placeholders even though damage numbers appear. `OnDamageStateChange` updates the ship's health rings, while dogma attribute updates retain matching shield charge, armor damage and structure damage.

For loss, the session stops active mining and weapons, loads the capsule with AddBalls and removes the old hull with terminal=True before sending the `shipid` delta. `gameui._HandleSessionChangeInSpace` can adopt a loaded new ship as ego, or wait for its ball if the simulation update is delayed. The existing ballpark is retained through this transition. Targeting sends explicit `OnTarget('lost')` for pending/completed outgoing locks and `OnTarget('otherlost')` for incoming locks before forgetting the old hull. Clearing shared server edges alone left the client's portrait and targeted indicator visible after ejection. Other pilots receive terminal removal of the lost hull, the capsule addition and streamed health changes. Fatal text precedes the Destiny removal, including when the current client RPC is rejected. The source build/hash and Carbon Blue/Destiny revisions remain those recorded in the preceding sections.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **202 tests**, and formatting verification passed after the incoming-message and lock-clear fixes. Coverage includes reaction delay, no aggression on arrival, first-attacker retention, range/docking disengagement, durable cooldowns without overdue bursts, corvette role resistance, serialized competing shots, one atomic loss/capsule, consumed fitted/cargo items, retained wallet, capsule-creation rollback, migration without grants, restart and corvette recovery. Handler/session checks cover both directions of lock removal, stopped weapons, observer health/removal, localized incoming-message fields, capsule AddBalls before terminal removal and the ship delta, including a rejected concurrent RPC.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `retaliation-20261102`, copied from the verified recovery save. Selected **Eden Pilot** and checked active **Ibis (Ibis)** in Inventory. Moved **2,200 Tritanium / 22 m³** into its cargo. Fitting showed both civilian modules online, **3.7 dps**, stable capacitor, **833 EHP**, and shield resistances **8%, 27%, 45%, 54%**. Undocked, warped to Perimeter II - Asteroid Belt 1, kept the Rookie near 1 km and fired. Arrival alone had created no aggression or damage. At normal catalog damage, an incoming beam and declining shield ring appeared; stopping the player gun did not stop the live NPC's retaliation. The initial red text had unresolved owner placeholders, which the typed owner field corrected.

At the pilot's request, accelerated destruction using **100 times NPC damage** in a separate physical copy of this lab's catalog: only type 30380's damage multiplier changed from **0.625 to 62.5**. The normal catalog was independently checked to remain **0.625**. Player weapon damage and ship health were not artificially changed. On the corrected message build, red popups included **186 from Guristas Rookie - Penetrates** and **117 - Glances Off**. The first loss removed Ibis **2000000000018**, its two fitted modules and 2,200 Tritanium; Inventory changed to **Capsule (Capsule)** with zero cargo capacity and no modules. The pilot confirmed ejection, but reported that both target locks remained. That observation led to the explicit lock notifications above.

Restart retained capsule **2000000000021**. The capsule's normal warp/dock path returned to Perimeter II - Moon 1, and the pilot manually boarded recovery Ibis **2000000000022**. Fitting again showed its online miner/gun, stable capacitor and 833 EHP. A second accelerated loss at Perimeter X - Asteroid Belt 1 then showed **CAPSULE EJECTED**, empty Capsule Inventory, no fitted modules, and immediate removal of both the target portrait and the NPC's targeted lock marker without reconnecting. Persistence retained exactly two losses, one capsule per lost hull, both lost kits in their manifests, no surviving aggression rows, and the unchanged **19,000 ISK** wallet. The Venture **prospector** and its **8,599 Scordite / 71 Veldspar** remained saved. No relevant damage-message, active-ship, target-event, capsule or removal exception appeared in the second-loss client log.

Stopped both processes, restored the lab catalog to the unchanged normal catalog and restarted the same save. Capsule **2000000000025** remained active at the saved belt position, with no target locks or resumed attacks. The pilot subsequently reported **"Recovery works I checked"**. Computer Use was stopped by the pilot, so that final recovery is maintainer acceptance rather than an additional agent-observed docking/boarding check. Multi-client observer behavior and other race recovery hulls have automated coverage or catalog support as described above, but were not separately exercised in this native run. Wrecks, loot, insurance, capsule combat and native killmails remain future work.

## Native station repair, November 2, 2026

The pinned `eve/client/script/ui/station/repairshop/base_repairshop.pyj` creates a `repairSvc` moniker with `(stationID, stationID, groupStation)`, calls `GetRepairQuotes(itemIDs)`, and submits `RepairItemsInStation(itemIDs, payment)`. Its quote response is a dictionary from each requested item ID to a list of objects with `itemID`, `typeID`, `groupID`, `damage`, `maxHealth` and `costToRepairOneUnitOfDamage`. The native window rounds each item's damage upward before computing its full price and offers a two-decimal ISK budget for partial ship repairs. The selected-pilot binding requires the current docked station and expires on departure. Pinned `appConst` defines **Repair Facilities service 13**, **Repair Bill reference type 15**, and **station group 15**. Catalog station operation services determine availability. The client build/hash and Carbon Blue/Destiny references remain unchanged; no wire codec or simulation format is added.

World version **22** adds `WorldRepairPayment`, retaining the payer, station and station owner, amount, balance, timestamp and a JSON audit of the armor/hull repaired per ship. Migration grants no money, health, equipment or skills. Quotes require owned assembled ships or supported modules in the current station; inactive hulls use their own catalog stats with the owner's trained skills. Eden does not yet simulate module heat/damage, so assembled modules quote zero damage. Structures, packaged items and inaccessible ships are rejected.

Eden's explicit initial price is **1 ISK per rounded armor-plus-hull damage point per selected ship**. This is local pricing policy, not a claim about Tranquility repair rates. Payment revalidates all items, service availability, damage and wallet in one SQLite transaction. A partial budget removes the same fraction of saved armor and hull damage from all selected ships. A fresh remaining quote rounds upward again. The debit is capped at the recomputed full bill, so a competing or repeated payment for a completed repair cannot charge twice. Insufficient funds reject the entire operation. Zero payment changes nothing. Shield snapshots/timestamps, capacitor, cargo and fitting are retained; natural recharge continues. The active hull receives armor/hull dogma updates and the wallet receives `OnAccountChange`. The personal journal combines saved market, bounty and Repair Bill entries with disjoint IDs.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **215 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Repair coverage checks nonmutating native quotes, partial rounding and layer proportions, full repair, retained shields/capacitor/inventory, restart and receipts, inactive hulls with trained health, zero/insufficient payments, concurrent stale payments, owned assembled modules, whole-batch ownership/location/service rejection, version-21 migration without repairs, selected-pilot binding and expiry, quote serialization, health/wallet notifications and Repair Bill journal fields.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `repair-20261102`, copied from `recovery-final-20261102`. The normal catalog remained unchanged. This was an explicit artificial damage fixture on active Ibis **2000000000018**: **10.2 armor damage, 20.1 hull damage and 50 shield charge**, with a current shield timestamp. It was not weapon-earned damage. Migration from version 20 to 22 retained the ship, its kit, the Venture's ore and the 19,000 ISK wallet. Selected **Eden Pilot**, verified **Ibis (Ibis)** in Inventory, opened the station's crossed-tools **Repairshop** button, and requested the active Ibis quote. The other healthy Ibis correctly quoted zero. The damaged Ibis showed **307/339 HP, 9.2% damaged, 31 ISK**.

The pilot supplied/submitted the native payments while acceptance was in progress. After the first **14.99 ISK** payment, the agent observed the refreshed **322/339 HP, 4.7% damaged, 16 ISK** quote. The second payment was **16 ISK**. Persistence showed zero remaining armor/hull damage, exactly two receipts and **18,969.01 ISK**. The saved shield charge and timestamp were unchanged by payment. Opened My Wallet: both native **Repair Bill** entries displayed the localized description **Repair bill between Eden Pilot and Caldari Navy**. That panel truncates these small values to whole ISK (14 and 16, with a displayed 18,969 balance); SQLite retained the exact cents. Fitting retained the online miner and gun, **3.7 dps**, stable capacitor and **833 EHP**. Comparing the lab to its source confirmed all ship, item, pilot, skill and capacitor rows unchanged.

Restarted host and client on the same lab. Inventory retained the active Ibis; the wallet retained both bills and the same displayed balance. Reopened Repairshop and quoted the active Ibis: **0.0% damaged, Repair Cost 0 ISK**, no repairable rows and no Repair All button. The two receipts and zero saved armor/hull damage remained. No relevant repair, quote, wallet-formatting, account-change or health-attribute exception appeared in either client log. Native acceptance covered the active Ibis and maintainer-submitted partial/full payments. Multiple inactive hulls, service/location rejection and competing payments were covered automatically rather than repeated in the client. Module heat/damage, remote repair modules and player structures remain future work.

## Rookie wrecks and native looting, November 2, 2026

World version **23** adds `WorldWreck`: a separate identity, unique source NPC, owner/corporation, catalog wreck type, name, system/position and creation/expiry times. Fatal NPC damage, the saved defeat, bounty, wreck and loot all commit in the existing weapon transaction. The wreck ID occupies Eden's separate 8-trillion namespace and is derived from the never-reused source NPC ID. Migration does not backfill wrecks for earlier defeats. A new NPC replacement can leave its own wreck independently of the old NPC's two-hour wreck. The rows remain as an identity audit after expiry; expired, uncollected contents are removed lazily and cannot be accessed meanwhile.

Build 3662168's `client/types` record for Guristas Rookie **30380** supplies `wreckTypeID=26557`, catalog **Frigate Wreck / group 186 / category 2**, mass **10,000 kg** and radius **14 m**. Its appearance uses the pinned wreck model. The public catalog does not supply the server's random NPC loot table. Eden's explicit starter policy places **100 Tritanium (34)** in each new Rookie wreck; this is not a Tranquility loot claim. Only the credited killer has rights initially. Fleet sharing, abandonment/theft, salvage, wreck combat, randomized drops and player ship wrecks remain separate work. Existing player-loss manifests and full item consumption are unchanged.

Wrecks stream as non-global, nonmassive, interactive stationary free balls within Eden's 250 km visibility policy. The free-ball layout and AddBalls reuse [Carbon Destiny Thunkers.cpp at 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp), consulted again for the old-style mass/radius/velocity/agility arguments. Blue uses the previously recorded **af0d65b** revision. The pinned `spaceObject/wreck.pyj.Prepare` reads `launcherID`, looks up the defeated ship, adopts its rotation and arranges display after its explosion. Eden therefore adds the wreck before terminal removal of the NPC. Slim items include `launcherID`, name, owner/corporation, `isEmpty` and `lootRights=(ownerID, corpID, None, False)`. `michelle.Park.HaveLootRight` consumes that tuple; `OnSlimItemChange` updates empty markers without removing/readding the ball. Initial snapshots include existing wrecks; expiry streams ordinary removal.

Pinned `MenuSvc.OpenCargo` calls its native approach/navigation flow, then opens an `ItemWreck` inventory. `invCache.GetInventoryFromId` binds the container; `GetSelfInvItem` returns the ordinary inventory row and `List(flag=None)` returns contents. `invControllers.BaseCelestialContainer.LootAll` calls the active `ShipCargo.AddItems`, which uses inventory `Add` or `MultiAdd`; no new LootAll RPC is invented. `appConst.maxCargoContainerTransferDistance` is **2,500 m**. Each server read/transfer rechecks the owned current hull, system, unexpired wreck, rights and surface range; the protocol also rejects transfers in warp/transit. Transfers use the ordinary cargo hold, validate capacity before mutation and retain stack identity on a full move. Partial moves split quantity atomically. `OnItemChange` updates both container caches; a slim change marks the wreck empty. Bound wreck references expire on location changes. Names are available through the existing location lookup.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **226 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Added coverage checks persistent catalog wreck identity/content/rights, full and partial pickup without duplication, concurrent pickups, exact cargo capacity and rollback, ownership/ship/system/range/quantity rejection, expiry retaining collected cargo, native inventory binding/list/move/empty-slim notifications, complete free-ball bytes and loot fields, ordinary expiry removal, and migration without retroactive loot. Existing fatal-shot tests now assert one wreck/loot stack under competing killers, rollback of the wreck when bounty credit fails, and wreck AddBalls before terminal NPC removal.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `loot-20261102`, copied from the repaired Ibis save at version 22. No new HP fixture or damage/catalog override was introduced. Selected **Eden Pilot** and verified **Ibis (Ibis)** with empty **0/125 m³** cargo. Undocked, warped to Perimeter II - Asteroid Belt 1, locked Rookie **4000040009250** at **4,732 m**, enabled the civilian gun's normal auto-repeat and fired with F2. Blue hit popups and hull fire appeared; the NPC disappeared and the gun stopped. The first saved wreck was **8000040009250**, with catalog type **26557**, name **Guristas Rookie Frigate Wreck**, the original NPC position and one **100 Tritanium** stack, item **2000000000021**. Its model rendered in space. One additional 3,000 ISK bounty raised the exact saved balance from **18,969.01 to 21,969.01 ISK**.

The maintainer approached/opened the wreck and transferred its contents during acceptance. The agent then observed **100 Tritanium / 1.0 of 125 m³** in the Ibis cargo, the selected wreck at **661 m**, and the wreck model beside the ship. The host trace recorded native `CmdFollowBall`, `GetInventoryFromId`, wreck `List`/`GetSelfInvItem`, ship `Add`, `OnItemChange` and the Destiny update. Reopened the wreck through Selected Item's **Open Cargo** control: its Inventory displayed **Nothing Found**, zero items and zero used volume. The General Overview showed the empty outlined wreck marker; the Mining preset hid wreck rows. Persistence retained exactly one new stack in cargo, no items in the wreck, one wreck and one additional bounty. All 15 pre-existing item rows were unchanged, and the Ibis retained zero armor/hull damage.

Restarted host and client on the same save. Inventory retained the Ibis and its **100 Tritanium / 1 m³**. The same empty wreck appeared in General Overview at **8 m**, and reopening it again showed **Nothing Found**. The saved stack identity, quantity, wreck, four total bounty records including the three inherited records, and **21,969.01 ISK** balance remained. No relevant wreck model, AddBalls, slim-change, loot/inventory or item-change exception appeared in either client log; the pre-existing optional PLEX gateway errors remained unrelated. The full pickup was maintainer input observed afterward, rather than an agent-submitted Loot All action. Partial quantities, capacity failures, expiry and competing pickups were automated coverage; they were not separately repeated in this native run.

## Player wrecks and surviving inventory, November 2, 2026

World version **24** allows each `WorldWreck` to reference exactly one defeated NPC or saved player `ShipLoss`, with a unique source and checked foreign keys. The upgrade preserves version-23 NPC wrecks and their existing item IDs. Earlier player losses remain historical: migration does not create wrecks or restore consumed contents. Player wreck IDs use a separate **10-trillion offset plus the lost hull ID**, including legacy seeded hulls. The wreck retains the lost hull's name, system/position, owner/corporation, catalog wreck type and two-hour expiry. Build 3662168's Ibis **601** specifies `wreckTypeID=26510`, **Caldari Rookie ship Wreck**, group **186**, radius **14 m**, mass **10,000 kg**.

Eden rolls an independent **50% survival chance per supported fitted module or whole cargo/mining-hold stack**, using two equally likely server RNG outcomes. [CCP's normal-loot explanation](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/a-tale-of-two-reasonable-things) describes the 50% baseline; seasonal modifiers are not modeled. [CCP's ownership guidance](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000597345-Container-and-Wreck-Ownership) assigns a player wreck to the lost ship's owner. Eden initially permits only that pilot to loot it; theft and fleet/corporation sharing remain unavailable. Rigs, nested containers, capsule loss, insurance and native killmails are outside the implemented item families.

The original item manifest is serialized before mutations. `ShipLoss.DroppedItemIdsJson` records the committed survivors independently of their later pickup or expiry. Survivors keep their item IDs, type, quantity and singleton state, move into the wreck as cargo, and become offline; all cooldowns from the old hull are removed. Non-survivors and the hull are deleted. Damage, cooldown, capsule, active ship, loss manifest, drop result and wreck changes share the fatal-shot transaction. Duplicate/concurrent ticks cannot create a second wreck or reroll a committed loss. Even a loss with no surviving contents leaves an empty catalog wreck.

The pinned `spaceObject/wreck.pyj.Wreck.Prepare` was checked again: it resolves `slim.launcherID` to the exploded ship and copies its rotation when a model exists. The fatal-retaliation handler now streams the wreck before adding the capsule and terminally removing the old hull. Observer refresh similarly adds wrecks before removing lost peer ships. This uses the existing free-ball, inventory and loot protocol, with no new codec. Client **3662168** / `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f** and Blue **af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997** remain unchanged.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **230 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Added/extended coverage checks mixed/all/no surviving items, original slot/online state retained in the loss manifest, survivor identity and offline cargo state, removal of old module cooldowns, zero-capacity capsule pickup rejection, ownership, competing fatal ticks, capsule/wreck-definition rollback, restart without rerolls, recovery-hull pickup, wreck-before-hull-removal ordering for pilot and observer, and version-23 migration retaining NPC loot without inventing player drops.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated lab `player-wreck-20261102`, copied from the verified looting save at version 23. Selected **Eden Pilot** and verified active Ibis **2000000000018**, its online civilian kit and **100 Tritanium / 1 of 125 m³** cargo. The test used a separate physical catalog copy with the previously requested **100× NPC damage** override, **0.625 → 62.5**, while player damage stayed normal. No item-survival override or artificial hull damage was used. Disabled gun auto-repeat, locked the replacement Rookie **4000040009253** near **53 m**, and fired once. Incoming red damage messages appeared, the Ibis exploded, Inventory changed to empty Capsule **2000000000022**, both lock indicators cleared, and a **Caldari Rookie ship Wreck** appeared at the lost hull's position.

The saved wreck **12000000000018** used catalog type **26510** and name **Ibis Caldari Rookie ship Wreck**. This natural roll retained Civilian Miner **2000000000019** and Tritanium **2000000000021**, while railgun **2000000000020** was destroyed. Opened the wreck from the capsule: Inventory showed those two entries totaling **6 m³**. Warped/docked the capsule at **Perimeter II - Moon 1 - Caldari Navy Assembly Plant**, used **Board my Corvette**, verified replacement Ibis **2000000000023** in Inventory, undocked and warped back to the first belt. General Overview showed the same wreck near **3,699 m**. **Open Cargo** automatically approached and opened it. Pressed **Loot All**: both original items moved into the recovery Ibis, Inventory showed **6/125 m³**, and reopening the wreck showed **Nothing Found / 0 items** with the empty marker. These actions were agent inputs, not maintainer-submitted pickup.

## Inventory refresh during capsule adoption, November 2, 2026

Investigated the old-hull cargo refresh observed above against the same pinned client/build/hash. `invCache.ProcessSessionChange` discards the previous ship's cached inventory when `shipid` changes in space. `holdTracker.OnCapacityChange(moduleID)` does not filter by the supplied module ID: it calls its still-retiring cargo controller, which can rebind the lost hull after the session already names the capsule. The captured nested call was `invbroker.MachoBindObject((systemID, groupSolarSystem), ('GetInventoryFromId', (lostHullID, 0), {}))`. Selecting/boarding another ship or leaving the location clears this read compatibility state; reconnect does not recreate it. The existing capsule, wreck, health, lock and ship-session event ordering is unchanged. No new schema or wire format is added; Carbon Blue/Destiny source revisions remain unchanged.

## Shield boosters and physical skillbooks, November 2, 2026

Checked the same build **3662168** and pinned `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368**. The catalog's default effect for shield-booster group **40** is **4 / shieldBoosting**, with **effects.ShieldBoosting**, capacitor attribute **6**, duration attribute **73**, and `isWarpSafe=1`. Small Shield Booster I **399** uses a medium slot (effect 13), 25 CPU, 2 power grid, 20 capacitor and 26 shield HP per 2,000 ms, requiring Shield Operation **3416** level I. Civilian Shield Booster **10039** belongs to the same supported family but is not a starter market offer. Shield Compensation **21059**, effects **1499/1500**, multiplies capacitor need by its trained level and attribute **851** (-2 percent per level). Overheating and ancillary charge behavior are not implemented.

The native module button reaches the existing `dogmaIM.Activate(itemID, 'shieldBoosting', targetID, repeat)` call, with no target for this self effect. One activation owner dispatches mining, weapons and shield boosters to their scoped controllers. Fitting allocates medium flags **19–26** separately from high flags **27–34**, preserves turret hardpoints, and accounts for CPU/grid across both racks. Godma item and ship primes include medium-slot attributes, online state and active effect 4.

Each boost is one world transaction: validate the owned online assembled medium-slot module on the active ship in space, check skills and durable cooldown, recharge/read shared capacitor and shields, spend capacitor, clamp the shield increase to capacity and save the next deadline. Armor, structure, cargo and wallet stay unchanged. Eden applies the boost immediately at cycle start; a manual stop finishes that paid cycle. A full shield still consumes capacitor. The session permits this warp-safe effect during warp, stops it on docking, transitions or hull loss, and advances at most one overdue cycle rather than simulating missed boosts. NPC damage and shield boosts serialize through the same SQLite world. The C# cooldown entity is now `ModuleCooldown`, retaining the existing `WorldWeaponCooldown` table and all saved rows; schema remains **24**.

`OnGodmaShipEffect` carries effect 4 and the paid cycle's start/duration/repeats. `OnModuleAttributeChanges` updates capacitor and shield charge, and `OnDamageStateChange` updates the health ring after healing. `OnSpecialFX` uses the native shield effect with the ship as source/target and the fitted module identity. Shared contacts publish the active effect and positive shield changes to observers. These use existing Blue/Destiny shapes; the previously recorded Carbon revisions remain applicable and no codec change is required.

The legacy Eden Pilot lacks Shield Operation, so the starter policy now offers its physical book for **5,000 ISK** and Small Shield Booster I for **1,000 ISK**, each with finite initial stock of 100. These are explicit Eden starter prices, not the catalog's base price or a live market feed. Existing station orders retain their stock and history; only missing offers are added. No pilot receives a skill, book, module or ISK on migration.

Pinned `SkillsSvc.InjectSkillIntoBrain` collects physical item IDs and calls `godma.GetDogmaLM().InjectSkillIntoBrain(skillIDList)`. It checks published types, then raises its own skill-injected notification after a successful response. Eden's selected-pilot skill handler owns that dogma call and returns None. Book consumption and adding `WorldPilotSkill` at **zero SP** are atomic. A stack loses exactly one book; the last book uses the existing junk-location `OnItemChange` removal. `OnServerSkillsChanged` sends `CharacterSkillEntry(typeID, 0, 0, rank, None)`, allowing the existing queue to train level I. Owned ordinary cargo on the current hull or the current docked station hangar is accessible; foreign, remote, specialized-hold, unpublished, non-skill, repeated-ID, repeated-type and already-known requests are rejected as a batch. Injection does not start training, spend free points or bypass the queue's prerequisite/Alpha restrictions. Injection without prerequisites follows [CCP's February 2016 skillbook change](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-for-february-2016-release). Direct `SkillsSvc.PurchaseSkills` remote purchases are separate work.

## Small hybrid turrets and ammunition, November 2, 2026

Checked the same pinned build **3662168**, `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368**, and catalog definitions. Small Tech I hybrid turrets have group **74**, charge size **128 = 1**, and tech level **422 = 1**. 75mm Gatling Rail I **561** has **0.5 m³** magazine capacity, **5,000 ms** reload attribute **1795**, charge group **604 = 85**, and charge rate **56 = 1**. Iron Charge S **215** and Antimatter Charge S **222** each occupy **0.0025 m³**, making 200 rounds per full magazine. The default firing effect is **34 / projectileFired / effects.ProjectileFired**, even for this railgun. These shapes reuse the existing Blue and Destiny codecs and their previously recorded Carbon revisions.

The starter policy adds the 75mm gun at **1,000 ISK**, plus Iron and Antimatter at **1 ISK per round**, with finite stocks of 100 guns and 100,000 of each charge at visited market stations. Existing orders, stock and history stay intact. Other small Tech I hybrid types can use the family implementation when available as owned equipment; the starter market initially offers only the 75mm gun. Required skills, high-slot/turret limits and combined CPU/grid remain enforced.

Pinned `DogmaLocation.LoadChargesToModule` and `LoadAmmoToModules` call `dogmaIM.LoadAmmo(shipID, moduleIDList, chargeItemIDList, ammoLocationID)`. `UnloadAmmoFromModules` passes a module list; `UnloadAmmoToContainer` passes a single module, a `(locationID, ownerID, flagID)` destination and optional quantity. The HUD's native unload menu uses current-ship cargo. One ammunition handler owns both methods; source IDs must be distinct owned stacks of one compatible charge type in ordinary cargo or the current docked station hangar. Unload supports partial quantities for a single module. Invalid batches and capacity failures roll back the entire transfer.

Loaded rounds remain actual owned stacks whose durable `LocationId` is the fitted module's ID. They are absent from ordinary cargo until unloaded. The wire identity is the native **(shipID, flagID, chargeTypeID)** sublocation. Godma primes carry a null `invItem`, that tuple identity, charge attributes including **805 / quantity**, and no active effects. Mixed-key `shipInfo` uses the existing `BlueDictionary` type. `shipState.instanceFlagQuantityCache` contains a four-column native DBRow `(locationID, flagID, typeID, quantity)` per slot, as consumed by `BaseDogmaLocation.LoadSublocations` and `GetQuantityFromCache`. Quantity changes use the tuple as the item key; zero quantity removes the exhausted sublocation. The existing reduction opcode now carries the inventory descriptor's reduce state; no new opcode is introduced.

Loading, topping up or swapping consumes only real selected stacks, returns the old charge type to the source, fills available magazine capacity and commits a reload deadline. `OnGodmaPrimeItem`, `OnModuleAttributeChanges` and `OnChargeBeingLoadedToModule(moduleIDs, chargeTypeID, milliseconds)` drive the native charge/count/reload presentation. Attribute **2729 / nextActivationTime** permits the existing `StateManager.GetReloadTimes` calculation after reconnect. World version **25** adds a named `ModuleCooldownKind` to the retained `WorldWeaponCooldown` table; old deadlines become ordinary cycles without being reset. Reload and firing serialize through that same module deadline. Migration grants no equipment, ammunition, skills or ISK.

Each hybrid shot checks owned fitted online state, skills, same-system target/range, compatible loaded rounds and the saved deadline before spending capacitor or ammunition. It spends charge-rate rounds even on a miss. The magazine, capacitor, next cycle, NPC health, aggression, fatal wreck and bounty share one transaction. The existing hit-quality/resistance calculations and gunnery modifiers apply. Small Hybrid Turret adds damage; Ibis's catalog rookie hybrid bonus adds optimal range. Charge effect **596** multiplies range by attribute **120** (Iron **1.6**, Antimatter **0.5**); effect **804** applies capacitor percent **317** (Iron **-30%**, Antimatter **0%**). Ammo supplies the damage mixture: Iron **3 kinetic + 2 thermal**, Antimatter **7 kinetic + 5 thermal**. The civilian gun retains its built-in charge and effect 10.

Unfitting atomically returns remaining rounds into the module's destination, with combined cargo-capacity validation. Fatal hull loss includes the loaded stacks in its original manifest and rolls their normal independent survival chance; surviving rounds become loose wreck cargo even if the gun itself is destroyed. No nested rounds are orphaned or rerolled. Grouped weapon banks, other turret/launcher families, Tech II hybrid charge groups and heat remain separate work.

## Trained weapon tooltip calculations, November 2, 2026

Investigated the preceding mismatch on the same pinned build and `code.ccp` hash. `ModuleButtonTooltip.LoadTooltip` chooses the current client Dogma location. `clientDogmaLocation.GetAccurateAttributeValue` explicitly uses its local modifier graph when docked, and Godma's server-primed values while in space. Eden sent an empty four-part pilot brain in `GetAllInfo.charInfo`, so the docked graph applied hull and charge effects but lacked earned weapon-skill modifiers. The observed Iron tooltip was **3.2 DPS / 12 km optimal / 136.50 tracking**, while the offense panel and in-space readout used **3.9 DPS / 13 km / 150.15**.

`GetAllInfo.charInfo[1]` now supplies `(version, characterEffects, shipEffects, structureEffects)`. Weapon skill effects occupy `shipEffects` and use the pinned `eve.common.script.dogma.effect.BrainEffect` state: resolved percentage value, source skill IDs, **LRS** location-required-skill modifier, affected attribute, **6 / post-percent** operation and `(requiredSkillID,)` application arguments. Shared gunnery bonuses target modules requiring Gunnery; Small Hybrid Turret's damage bonus targets its own requirement. Surgical Strike uses the catalog's **LG** location-group modifiers for the supported Energy Weapon **53** and Hybrid Weapon **74** families. The catalog confirms effects **414, 582, 290, 2847, 287, 298 and 173** use `shipID`/LocationRequiredSkillModifier, while **587/589** use LocationGroupModifier. Native owner modifiers exclude ordinary fitted modules and are not used for these effects. Zero-level and unrelated skills contribute no weapon effects. The authoritative weapon calculation and native brain use one named definition of each supported bonus. Firing, charge and hull rules remain authoritative in persistence, and this read does not grant skills or change saved SP.

The first live check rejected an old-style `INSTANCE` encoding of this new-style Python class. [Carbon Blue `Marshal.cpp` at af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp) distinguishes `ReadObjectInstance` from `ReadObjectNewobj`: the latter reads `((class,), state)`, calls the class's allocator and then restores its dictionary. Eden now sends BrainEffect with the existing **35 / NEWOBJ** opcode and two empty iterator markers. The existing paper-doll named-tuple form retains its validation. ## Retaliatory NPC pursuit and return, November 2, 2026

Checked the same pinned build 3662168, script hash and catalog. Guristas Rookie **30380** has **37 / maxVelocity = 440**, **508 / entityCruiseSpeed = 220**, **416 / entityFlyRange = 1,250** and **247 / entityAttackRange = 3,125**, in metres/seconds as appropriate. Its minimum reaction delay remains **7,500 ms**. Eden retains first-attacker retaliation without arrival aggression; after the reaction delay the NPC follows toward the catalog's surface stand-off, continuing pursuit outside its weapon range. The weapon retains its own saved firing deadline and only fires inside attack range. Both NPC and player accuracy now use relative velocity rather than assuming the NPC is stationary.

Movement was checked against [Carbon Destiny Ballpark.cpp at 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp), especially `EvolveFollow` and `GotoThrust`. Follow chooses a point separated by both hull radii plus stand-off; thrust tapers within one tick's travel distance. The server uses player flight's exponential drag integration and retains velocity through turns. The Rookie has no positive agility override, so Eden explicitly supplies **agility 1**. A slow arrival ends within **25 m** at no more than **1 m/s**, preserving its actual position rather than snapping to the destination. That stopping tolerance and a **50 km leash from the NPC's home anchor** are local encounter policy, rather than extracted AI rules.

World version **26** adds named Idle/Pursuing/Returning state, three velocity components and `MotionAt` to each existing NPC. Existing identity, coordinates, health timestamps, defeated rows and firing deadlines remain intact. Movement has a separate clock from shield recharge; reads, pursuit and returns do not heal or reset the saved damage. Movement uses shared wall-clock second boundaries, so scheduling jitter does not skip steps. Ticks advance at most one second after a gap, and repeated or competing calls within the same second cannot move the NPC again. Disengagement on warp, docking, disconnect, capsule transition or leash loss returns the same surviving NPC toward its original belt placement. Defeated entries do not return or acquire a new bounty identity. No items, skills, ISK or health are granted by this migration.

Native snapshots and streamed additions now carry FOLLOW or GOTO free-ball records, velocity and speed fraction, with the existing STOP form for idle ships. Existing [Carbon Destiny Thunkers.cpp at 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) confirms `FollowBall`, `GotoPoint`, `SetSpeedFraction` and `SetBallVelocity` signatures. Course and position changes stream through the existing Destiny envelope without replacing the ballpark. Ten-tick corrections bound client drift while moving. Player Approach refreshes a moving NPC's position instead of retaining the point where it was first selected. Blue serialization and its pinned revision are unchanged. Autonomous aggression, orbiting, obstacle avoidance and cross-grid pursuit remain separate work.

## Manufacturing from mined minerals, November 2, 2026

The native gameplay loop now connects ore → reprocessing → minerals → ammunition manufacturing → loading and firing the output. The implementation, automated coverage and Windows client observations are recorded separately below.

Checked build **3662168** and `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. `blueprintSvc`, `facilitySvc`, `industrySvc`, `industryCommon` and `industry/job.py` use `blueprintManager.GetBlueprintData[ByOwner]`, `facilityManager.GetFacilities/GetFacility/GetFacilityLocations`, `industryManager.GetJobsByOwner/GetJob/GetJobCounts/InstallJob/CompleteJob/CancelJob`, and `industryMonitor.ConnectJob/DisconnectJob`. Install receives the native job dump: blueprint/type, activity, facility/system, character/account, runs, product, input/output locations, quoted materials, time, cost and tax. Blueprint and job results are native attribute records; facility results contain activity filters and modifiers. Hangar locations must be `industry.job.Location` new-style objects: a plain KeyVal passes material monitoring but fails the native output view's `GetInvController`. The existing Carbon NEWOBJ format carries this additional allowlisted class as inert dictionary state on Eden; incoming globals never invoke Python code. `OnIndustryJob`, `OnBlueprintsUpdated` and `OnIndustryMaterials` refresh the corresponding native views. Inventory changes invalidate the separately memoized blueprint list, including the empty list cached before a purchase.

`Job.dump` leaves `productTypeID` as Python None for a single-output blueprint. Eden resolves that selection from the owned blueprint's catalog recipe; an explicit different product is rejected. `Activity.job_max_runs` uses the ceiling of 30 days divided by modified single-run time, capped at **1,000,000 runs**. For the Industry I Iron original the native maximum is exactly **9,000 runs**; the exact boundary must not permit run 9,001. These findings came from disassembling the pinned scripts without executing them.

[CCP's manufacturing guide](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203210292-Manufacturing) describes blueprints, materials and facility requirements; [CCP's job-type guide](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203210272-Activities-and-Job-Types) identifies Industry, Mass Production and Advanced Mass Production for manufacturing slots. The pinned catalog supplies Iron Charge S Blueprint **1130**: **27 Tritanium + 21 Pyerite → 100 Iron Charge S**, base **300 seconds**, Industry I. Antimatter Charge S Blueprint **1137** uses **204 Tritanium + 17 Pyerite + 1 Nocxium → 100 Antimatter Charge S**, also 300 seconds and Industry I. The client-cache blueprint keys are JSON strings. Industry's attribute **440 = -4% per level** and Advanced Industry's **1961 = -3% per level** multiply manufacturing time; Mass Production and Advanced Mass Production use **450 = +1 slot per level**. Character brain item modifiers carry the same catalog bonuses to **219 / manufactureTimeMultiplier** and **196 / manufactureSlotLimit**, so native job previews can agree with server calculations. Required-skill time modifiers use attribute **1982** when present. The base character has one manufacturing slot.

The first manufacturing implementation supports original blueprints for small Tech I hybrid charges, with personal Item Hangar input/output at the pilot's current manufacturing station. It validates ownership, station services, skills, a free pilot slot, an unused blueprint, exact catalog materials and the current time quote. Installation atomically consumes real stacks, splits/assembles one original if necessary, reserves it and saves the paid job. World version **27** adds only `WorldIndustryJob`; migration grants no items, minerals, skills or ISK. Blueprint movement is rejected while installed. A saved finish time progresses while disconnected; output is created only by delivery, once. Cancellation releases the original and slot but does not refund consumed materials. Ready undelivered jobs retain their blueprint and slot in this initial policy. The historical job retains its material recipe, output quantity and original identity after completion.

The finite starter market adds Iron and Antimatter originals at **1,000 ISK each**, plus Nocxium at **100 ISK per unit** for the Antimatter recipe. These are Eden prices. Installation currently follows the existing missing-adjusted-price fallback: the client has no adjusted market-price feed, so displayed and charged installation fees are zero. Research, blueprint copies, invention, corporate/remote jobs, optional materials and other output families are separate work. Station factory service **14** is checked against the pinned station-operation catalog for every manufacturing job. `LobbyWnd.CheckCanAccessService` and `stationServiceConst` check both factory **14** and laboratory **15** before opening the shared Industry window, even at a factory-only station. The standing check permits the docked pilot to open that public NPC station window if either catalog service exists; it does not enable research or bypass manufacturing availability. Initial native observation before this implementation: choosing the station's Industry factory icon failed at its unsupported `corpStationMgr.DoStandingCheckForStationService` bind.

The Blue NEWOBJ checks also cover the additional inert class and reject unknown classes or malformed state. ## Afterburner propulsion and Navigation skills

Build **3662168**'s `eve/common/script/dogma/pythonEffects/propulsionModules.pyj`, inspected without executing its code, adds the afterburner's mass before reading ship mass for the speed multiplier. It then applies `1 + speedFactor / 100 * speedBoostFactor / modifiedShipMass` to the unboosted maximum velocity. Stop removes both modifiers; refresh replaces the speed modifier rather than stacking it. The packaged `code.ccp` SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**.

The pinned **1MN Afterburner I / 439**, group **46**, has duration **73 = 10,000 ms**, capacitor need **6 = 20 GJ**, speed factor **20 = 115%**, thrust **567 = 1,500,000**, mass addition **796 = 500,000 kg**, maximum active group count **763 = 1**, CPU **50 = 15**, power **30 = 10**, and requires Afterburner I. Its default activation effect is **6731 / moduleBonusAfterburner**, graphic **effects.Afterburner**. Navigation **3449** supplies **315 = +5% speed per level**; Afterburner **3450** supplies **66 = -5% duration** and **317 = -10% capacitor cost** per level. Fuel Conservation **3451** separately multiplies capacitor cost through attribute 317. Acceleration Control **3452** supplies **318 = +5% speed factor** per level. Brain effects use the catalog's ship item, required-skill and group modifiers so native previews agree with paid server activations.

The Ibis's **295 m/s** base becomes **339.25 m/s** at Navigation III. Adding the module changes its mass **1,163,000 → 1,663,000 kg**, and its limit becomes **691.15 m/s**. Afterburner III produces **8,500 ms / 14 GJ** cycles. Changing earned Navigation updates the unboosted baseline once and reapplies an active module to that baseline. Module costs and bonuses are reread for each new paid cycle.

Destiny updates use **SetBallMass** and **SetMaxSpeed**, retaining actual velocity so acceleration and deceleration follow the changed inertia. Nearby pilots receive the same properties and propulsion effect; joining snapshots retain the active contact. This reuses Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f**, whose thunks accept `(long ID, double mass)` and `(long ID, float speed)` and whose ballpark updates mass/time factor and maximum velocity. [Carbon thunks](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) · [Carbon ballpark](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp)

Activation validates ownership, active hull, in-space state, medium slot, online state, requirements, group limit, health and capacitor. Paying saves capacitor and the UTC cycle deadline atomically in the existing records. World version stays **27**; no equipment, SP or money is granted. A late tick starts at most one new paid cycle. Consecutive paid cycles retain the same flight properties, avoiding a temporary base-speed command at each boundary. Manual deactivation finishes the paid cycle. A warp order prevents another cycle; docking, loss, gate transition and disconnect remove the effect. Reconnecting restores the ordinary Navigation limit and preserves an unexpired cooldown, without simulating offline cycles. The finite starter market adds 100 modules at **1,000 ISK** each as explicit Eden pricing. Microwarpdrives, overheating, implants and other navigation bonuses remain separate work.

New coverage checks catalog/brain agreement, mass-dependent speed, trained cost and speed bonuses, atomic competing activations, persisted cooldown and charge, missing skills/ownership/online/rack/resource rejection, combined fitting limits, one active module, manual stop, velocity continuity, observer updates, non-stacking repeats without transient base-speed commands, insufficient capacitor, invalid targets, loss cleanup, warp/docking cleanup and Navigation training during an active cycle. ## Native player Orbit and moving navigation targets

Build **3662168**'s `menuSvcExtras/movementFunctions._Ship_Orbit` calls **beyonce.CmdOrbit(targetID, followRange)**, with ordinary ranges encoded as integers and fractional ranges below ten metres encoded as floats. The client sets its indication to **DSTBALL_ORBIT** and uses that mode to distinguish an already active orbit from Keep at Range. The packaged script hash remains the build-pinned value above.

Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f** defines Orbit as mode **4**, sends the **Orbit(shipID, targetID, float range)** command, and writes a target ID plus float surface range after the common free-ball data. Its default configuration uses the legacy orbit controller. Eden adapts that controller's precessing plane, tangent approach, seven-decimal truncation and radial correction into a pure steering calculation, then uses the existing inertial integration and rigid-body collisions. Requested surface distance includes both radii. Speed, mass and agility affect the resulting trajectory; a tight orbit can overshoot its requested range. [Carbon mode definitions](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/IDstConstants.h) · [Orbit steering and follower departure](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp) · [Native stream and command](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) · [Reference orbit test](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/python/destiny/test/ballpark/evolve/test_orbit.py) · [Carbon license](carbon-license.md)

Orbit and Keep at Range resolve current NPC, ship, asteroid, wreck and static-object positions, instead of retaining an old point. Targets must be local, present and within the existing **8,000 km** shared-space range. Orbit rejects the active ship and invalid/nonfinite ranges, and requires an active ballpark. The maximum surface range is **1,000 km**. When its target departs, an interactive orbiter continues along its velocity heading, following Carbon's follower-removal behavior; Keep at Range coasts to a stop. Stop, heading changes, warp, docking, jumping and hull replacement discard the old target. A ten-tick position/velocity correction bounds native drift during orbit without replacing the ballpark. Peers and join snapshots carry the same native Orbit mode and range. No schema change or economic grant is involved; reconnecting loads the saved position at rest without replaying an offline orbit. Autonomous NPC orbit tactics and dynamic-orientation/new-style orbit remain separate work.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with **zero warnings/errors**, all **331 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Seven new cases cover the published ten-tick Carbon trajectory, inertial convergence, propulsion changes, stopping, native Orbit stream bytes, peer course changes between Orbit and Follow, departing peer behavior, moving NPC position refresh and destruction, invalid targets/ranges, warp rejection and saved position without a restarted orbit order. Native acceptance is recorded separately below.

The indication changed to **Ship Stopping**, the Orbit label cleared, and speed decayed through **250, 45, 13 and 1 m/s**. The Rookie closed and ordinary incoming damage/miss messages appeared during this stationary interval.

Issued a new five-kilometre Orbit and restarted both host and client on the same save. The returned Ibis showed **0.0 m/s**, no movement indication, no target locks, an inactive afterburner, **114 rounds** and its original cargo. A read-only comparison retained the exact saved position, **(132939577951.85828, 5971342610.509518, 102934488351.42084)**, and every world row except the NPC encounter's ongoing return movement. The position did not advance offline and the old Orbit did not resume. Started a fresh **1,000 m** Orbit with the Selected Item button, without locking the Rookie; the native indication and acceleration returned. Then chose the Navy station's Dock action while Orbit was active.

The indication changed to **Warp Drive Active** and the Ibis docked at **Perimeter II - Moon 1 - Caldari Navy Assembly Plant**. Undocked for a short station round trip; the HUD remained at **0.0 m/s**, with no resumed orbit. Restored gun auto-repeat without firing and verified that its menu offered **Set Auto-Repeat Off**. Redocked and verified the active Ibis's retained cargo in Inventory. The acceptance save is left running there, docked.

Final read-only comparison retained every pre-existing item row except the single round fired, all **121 pilot skill rows**, skill history/queue, wallet, market records and the completed manufacturing job. ## Automatic belt NPC aggression

Idle Perimeter Rookies now detect eligible connected ships within **20 km surface distance**. Selection uses the closest ship, breaking equal-range ties by ship identity, and keeps that target until disengagement. An earlier player attack can still establish retaliation. Detection range, closest-ship selection, capsule exemption and the existing **50 km belt leash** are Eden encounter policy; the catalog does not supply a general belt guard's aggression range. Reaction uses Guristas Rookie **30380**'s pinned attribute **475 / entityAttackDelayMin = 7,500 ms**, shared with retaliation. It does not claim to reproduce Tranquility's private NPC AI.

The connection-owned shared-space samples admit only present ships outside warp/alignment and gate transitions, excluding capsules and destroyed hulls. The persistence operation rechecks the pilot/active-ship pair, current system, capsule group, saved position, surface range and belt leash. One transaction selects unoccupied idle NPCs and creates their existing aggression records. Competing clients cannot select a second target or reset a reaction/cycle deadline. A closer arrival or subsequent player shot retains an occupied NPC's target.

The existing pursuit, incoming target notifications, turret effects and damage pipeline perform the fight. Movement still follows Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f**'s established Follow/inertia path; no new client RPC or movement record is introduced. Returning NPCs finish their journey before detecting again. Warp, docking, gate departure, disconnect and hull loss use the existing disengagement and target-lock cleanup. Reconnect clears the old fight; a guard can then return and acquire with a full new reaction interval, without offline shots or an overdue burst. World version remains **27**, without equipment, ammunition, SP or currency grants.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with **zero warnings/errors**, all **339 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Eight new cases cover unprovoked detection and damage, minimum reaction timing without a player shot, deterministic nearest/tied selection with concurrent viewers, preserving an occupied target/deadline, mismatched or inactive pilot/ship pairs, docking, another system, capsules, surface-range boundaries, destroyed NPCs, completing return before reacquisition, first-attacker priority, invalid delay rejection, shared-space availability filters and native incoming-lock/warp cleanup. Existing pursuit, combat, loss and recovery tests remain green. Native observations follow separately.

## NPC combat orbiting

Rookies now approach distant targets, then enter Orbit inside the pinned **665 / entityChaseMaxDistance = 1,875 m** surface range. Orbit aims at **416 / entityFlyRange = 1,250 m**, using **508 / entityCruiseSpeed = 220 m/s** against **37 / maxVelocity = 440 m/s**. If the target pulls beyond **247 / entityAttackRange = 3,125 m**, the NPC resumes pursuit. The two thresholds provide hysteresis so small radial overshoots do not repeatedly toggle modes. Using these catalog values as AI transition thresholds is Eden policy, not a claim about Tranquility's private decision logic. Rookies retain the existing positive-agility fallback because this type has no positive catalog override.

NPCs and players share the pure legacy Orbit steering adapted from Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f**. The helper takes the target position and center radius, which includes surface range and both object radii. NPC combat receives the connection's shared Destiny stamp, so the precessing plane uses the same clock as native Orbit. Motion retains inertia and velocity through approach/orbit transitions and continues during the weapon's cooldown. One persisted wall-clock movement step prevents competing viewers or a late callback from moving the NPC twice or running an offline journey. [Carbon Orbit steering](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp) · [Native mode definitions](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/IDstConstants.h) · [Carbon license](carbon-license.md)

Saved navigation adds the named **Orbiting** state to the existing integer field. Contacts and native joining streams carry Orbit mode **4**, target identity and float range; ongoing updates use **Orbit** or **FollowBall** as the AI changes course, without replacing the ballpark. The existing relative-velocity accuracy calculation uses the actual orbit velocity for both player and NPC turrets. Lost aggression, warp, docking, disconnect, leash escape and hull loss return the same NPC toward its anchor. Schema stays **27**; this introduces no new tables, equipment, SP, ammunition or economic grants.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with **zero warnings/errors**, all **346 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Seven new cases cover movement between weapon cycles, sustained tangential orbits under catalog inertia/cruise, moving-target hysteresis with velocity continuity, observer commands and exact joining-stream Orbit bytes, competing/late ticks without duplicate movement or shots, saved orbit geometry and orphan-aggression return, coincident geometry and invalid thresholds. The pursuit test now checks transition into sustained Orbit; the published Carbon trajectory and all prior player-flight, combat, loss, recovery and aggression tests still pass. Native observations follow separately.

## Tech I module manufacturing

Manufacturing now accepts the gameplay-supported Tech I module families in addition to small hybrid charges: regular mining lasers, small hybrid turrets, shield boosters and afterburners. Both the facility product list and the authoritative installation check apply the same Tech I/group/weapon-size rules. The existing owned-original, catalog-recipe, trained-skill, station-service, material/quote and slot checks remain authoritative. Delivery creates ordinary packaged inventory, so fitting, online costs, ammunition and activation follow their existing handlers. No new industry RPC, wire object or table is introduced; world version stays **27**.

Pinned build **3662168**'s read-only `client/blueprints` records use these single-module recipes. Each has a **900-second** base duration and requires **Industry I**; its trained -4% time bonus gives **864 seconds / 14m24s**. These are catalog recipes rather than Eden substitutes. The same packaged client and industry protocol references documented under ammunition manufacturing apply; `code.ccp` SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**.

| 75mm Gatling Rail I **1112 → 561** | 231 | 220 | 0 | 0 |

These are explicit starter prices, with no claim of Tranquility pricing or industrial profitability. Installation retains the missing-adjusted-price zero-fee fallback. Tech II production, ships, other weapon families, research and copies remain outside this milestone.
