## Fitting expansion milestone 6: Tech I capacitor boosters

Eight ordinary published Tech I capacitor-booster variants in group 76 now fit in medium slots and activate through effect 48 `powerBooster`. Eligibility checks the pinned publication/meta-group/tech-level profile and normal fitting effect. Loaded cap charges use group 87 and capacitorBonus attribute 67; the Small Capacitor Booster I (3566) has a 12 m³ magazine, chargeRate 56 = 1, a 15,000 ms cycle and a 10,000 ms reload. Cap Booster 100 (3554) occupies 4 m³ and injects 100 GJ. Compatibility is determined by charge group and available magazine volume, not the weapon-only charge-size restriction. All 18 published cap-charge variants are represented in the fixture, including Navy and capital charges; oversized charges still cannot fit a small magazine. Tech II booster fitting and overload activation remain excluded. Manufacturing eligibility and market offers are unchanged.

Magazine reading, priming, reload/unload and native charge quantities now support fitted medium slots as well as weapons. An activation validates the owned online fitted booster, required skills, compatible loaded charge, quantity, hull health and cooldown before atomically consuming the real charge, adding capacitor up to capacity and saving the new cycle deadline. Activating with insufficient missing capacitor still consumes the charge; excess injection is wasted. No public resource-grant RPC is added. Loaded charges participate in existing unfit and loss accounting. Repeats keep the native Godma HUD active, consume another charge only at each successful next cycle and stop on an empty magazine. Manual shutdown finishes the current interval. Docking, loss and disconnect remove the live controller; already injected capacitor, remaining charges and reload/cycle deadlines persist without offline injections. World version stays 30.

References: unchanged build 3662168 `client/typedogma`, `client/dogmaeffects` 48/3002 and `client/dogmaattributes` 56/67/73/604/1795. Effect 48 is warp-safe and has no spatial effect guid, so it uses HUD notifications without inventing a Destiny particle effect. Existing Blue row/sublocation adapters retain the consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b implementation; transport and Destiny stream formats are unchanged. The shared fitting fixture now passes its TestClock through the host's optional clock registration so handler calls and reload deadlines use the same clock. Production still defaults to TimeProvider.System.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **503 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. New tests cover all eight fitting profiles and Tech II exclusion, volume-based charge limits including larger/Navy charges, medium-slot reload deadline priming, mixed-load atomic rollback, oversized rejection, immediate injection and clipping, real charge consumption, duplicate activation, empty-magazine shutdown, stable repeat HUD state, paid manual stop, restart without offline injections, saved reloads, returned charges on unfit, warp-safe activation, hull-loss cancellation, failed starts without mutations, and native active-effect/magazine priming. Existing ammunition, fitting, repair, hardener and persistence regressions remain green.

Maintainer acceptance used the separate `fitting-passives-20261103` save, backed up as `before-capacitor-booster-check.sqlite`, with one supplied Small Capacitor Booster I (2000000000029), three Cap Booster 100 charges in the station hangar and nine in Ibis cargo. No additional skill or wallet grants were made for this check. The agent's Computer Use attempt was stopped with Escape; the maintainer asked that game windows be left alone, then manually tested the module and explicitly reported “the booster works.” The agent did not repeat input or independently observe the capacitor-gain animation after that instruction. Read-only checks corroborated the online booster fitted in the active Ibis's medium slot 19, native LoadAmmo/Activate/Deactivate calls with reload and quantity notifications, and five consumed charges: six remained in ordinary cargo and one in the reloaded magazine. The host error log remained empty. Exact injection amounts, reload duration, empty-magazine handling, restart behavior and other failure cases have automated coverage; the native result is maintainer-confirmed rather than an agent-observed screenshot sequence.

## Fitting expansion milestone 7: unrestricted microwarpdrives across tiers

Group 46 now admits all **62 unrestricted static MWD profiles** in build 3662168: Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, deadspace and officer. All use the complete effect set 13/16/58/3175/6730. Eligibility validates publication, static tech attributes, supported effects and the absence of any canFitShipType/canFitShipGroup restriction. The seven ship-restricted capital profiles are deferred under the maintainer's ordering instruction. Four Abyssal types lack a static tech profile and require per-item mutation persistence before use; they remain rejected. Manufacturing and market offers are unchanged.

Pinned `eve/common/script/dogma/pythonEffects/propulsionModules.pyj` defines Microwarpdrive as Afterburner.MODIFIER_CHANGES plus a ship signatureRadius post-percent modifier from signatureRadiusBonus. The same mass-first thrust calculation therefore owns flight speed/mass. Effect 58 applies the module's capacitorCapacityMultiplier (147) to ship capacitorCapacity (482) while online, including when inactive. The fitting graph applies this penalty and preserves the charged percentage through online/offline changes. Only active MWD markers add signatureRadiusBonus (554) to signatureRadius (552); this includes signature changes from other fitted equipment. Skills are selected by actual required skill or group: Afterburner/Fuel Conservation do not affect MWDs, High Speed Maneuvering affects MWD capacitor need once, and Acceleration Control applies to both propulsion families.

The 5MN Microwarpdrive I has a 25% online capacity penalty, 500% speed factor, 500% active signature bonus, 1,500,000 thrust, 500,000 kg added mass and a base 45 GJ / 10-second cycle. High Speed Maneuvering III reduces cost to 38.25 GJ. Higher tiers retain their distinct catalog values: the 5MN II uses 80% capacity, a 510% speed factor and 475% signature bonus; Domination uses 90% / 512% / 450%; Coreli A-Type 97% / 518% / 410%; Asine's Modified 99% / 520% / 390%. No universal tier multiplier is invented.

Activation pays capacitor and saves its cooldown and live marker in one transaction. Afterburners and MWDs share the single active propulsion limit. Repeats retain one flight/signature bonus and the native HUD effect; manual stop finishes the paid interval. Warp blocks new activation/repeats. Docking, hull loss, connection disposal and explicit host initialization clear the live bonus. World version **31** renames `WorldActiveHardener` to shared `WorldActiveModule`, retaining foreign-key cleanup while preserving paid resources and deadlines. Hardener behavior remains unchanged. Stopping a module never deletes its cooldown, so reconnecting cannot buy an early free cycle. No offline propulsion is simulated.

Native activation uses `moduleBonusMicrowarpdrive` and `effects.MicroWarpDrive`; active-effect priming and peer replay preserve the MWD identity. Blue/Destiny wire formats retain the consulted Carbon Blue revision af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997 and Destiny revision 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f. Effect, attribute, skill and graphics details come from the unchanged build 3662168 catalog and inert inspection of the staged code archive (code.ccp SHA-256 37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368).

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **522 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. Coverage includes full cohort eligibility; deferred capital/Abyssal exclusion; online capacitor penalty and charge-fraction preservation; inactive/active signature; skill separation and single application; four higher-tier payment/attribute profiles; simultaneous propulsion rejection; continuous repeats; paid manual stop; failed payment; scope disposal; warp gating; invalid starts without resource changes; version-30 migration/restart retaining cooldowns; native priming and observer MWD graphics. Historical save fixtures now remove the newer live table before replaying old migrations and capture modern pilot snapshots before downgrading their schema.

Native MWD acceptance remains **pending**. The maintainer instructed that game windows be left alone; no MWD was staged into or activated in a running client, and neither the lab host/save nor the separate launcher/client was restarted for this change. The Windows client guide contains the pending fitting, online capacity, active speed/signature, HUD, shutdown and restart checks. Automated results do not establish visible native acceptance.

The continuing scope now includes every tier of existing module families before additional families, with ship-type/group-restricted modules deferred until unrestricted modules are implemented. This supersedes the original Tech-I-only expansion scope; it does not claim that the remaining tier backfill or module families are complete.

## Fitting expansion milestone 13: ordinary ore-laser tiers, residue and rounding

The existing mining family now validates all **10 published ordinary ore-laser profiles** in group 54, including civilian, Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, ORE faction and the static Habitat profile without a meta-group value. The group contains 18 published definitions: two deep-core variants still need Mercoxit cloud hazards, three static ice variants explicitly require particular ships, and three Abyssal variants need per-item mutation attributes. Those eight profiles are rejected during fitting and activation through stored items. Deep-core work remains part of the unrestricted backlog; ice work follows the maintainer's unrestricted-first ordering. No officer or deadspace ore-laser tiers exist in this pinned cohort.

Effect 67 must be targeted, not warp-safe and use maxRange (54), capacitorNeed (6) and duration (73). Its complete profile is 12/16/42/67 with no additional modifiers. Actual skills, rack, hardpoints, CPU and grid still gate fitting. Activation no longer adds a corvette/Venture-only whitelist to unrestricted equipment. Ordinary ore groups have an explicit typed resource boundary; ice, gas, Mercoxit, decorative and non-interactable rocks cannot pay or produce ore through this path. Destroyed ships cannot start or settle a cycle. This does not expand hull assembly or implement bonuses for every hull.

Pinned profiles include Miner II (482): 12 km / 15 seconds / 15 m³ / 18 GJ / Mining IV; EP-S Gaussian Scoped (5239): 16 km / 15 seconds / 13 m³ / 5 GJ; Gallente (21841): 10 km / 15 seconds / 12 m³ / 2 GJ; Habitat (22921): 12 km / 15 seconds / 14 m³ / 5 GJ; and ORE Miner (28750): 16 km / 15 seconds / 21 m³ / 18 GJ. Mining, Astrogeology, critical skills, supported Venture bonuses and fitted mining upgrades retain their existing ownership. New tiers do not invent manufacturing recipes or grants. World version remains 31.

Mining waste probability (3154) is a percentage: Miner II is 34%, Gallente is 20%, and the other ordinary profiles are 0%. Their waste-volume multiplier (3153) is catalog-driven. Typed cycle inputs supply independent probability and rounding rolls to pure gameplay rules. Cargo bounds the planned base yield; a residue success removes its bounded amount from the deposit before extraction, and a completed-cycle critical success adds bonus cargo without consuming the deposit or increasing residue. Partial manual stops prorate yield and residue but cannot crit. Base yield, bonus and residue use stochastic rounding independently, with floating-point noise suppressed at integer boundaries. Cargo additions and both deposit reductions commit together, including residue-only depletion with no cargo row.

Source references: unchanged build 3662168 catalog, plus inert code-object inspection of the staged `notifications/client/oreMinedNotificationAdapter.pyj` and `mining/client/asteroid_scanning_service.pyj` in code.ccp (SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`). `quantity_removed` means extracted base ore; the native scanner separately adds `amountWasted` when subtracting from its estimate. Including residue in both would double its visible depletion. `quantity_added` includes critical ore, while `amountCritBonus` stays separate. Existing effects.Laser and consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b transport/FX formats are unchanged.

[CCP's Catalyst notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/catalyst-expansion-notes) specify stochastic rounding and critical bonus ore independent of asteroid contents. The residue-first exhaustion rule follows [CCP Swift's design-team clarification](https://forums.eveonline.com/t/from-extraction-to-production/336331/1015). That clarification predates this client; exact residue ordering at exhaustion and cargo clipping still need native comparison. Current probabilities come from the pinned catalog, not the earlier pre-release balance table.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **642 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. The focused mining run passed all **59 tests**. The expanded active fixture contains 658 published module profiles and 13 skill definitions, 47 effects and 84 attribute metadata records; existing active-family definitions and metadata are unchanged. Older minimal mining fixtures now include the targeted effect contract while preserving their artificial values. The 36 new cases audit all 18 profiles, every admitted tier's payment/range/cycle/yield, residue thresholds, stochastic expectations, independent bonus/residue rounding, cargo bounds, zero-yield depletion, persistence/replay rejection, invalid inputs/resources, unrestricted hull activation, destroyed ships and unsupported stored profiles.

Higher-tier native acceptance remains **pending** under the explicit instruction to leave game windows alone. No game input, running-save mutation, deployment or host/client restart occurred. The broader fitting/module goal remains open, including both deep-core lasers, hybrid tier backfills, remaining unrestricted families, instance mutations and subsequent ship-restricted work.

## Fitting expansion milestone 14: hybrid turret and charge tiers

The published group-74 cohort contains **223 hybrid turrets**. All **216 unrestricted profiles** now pass the complete fitting/firing boundary: small, medium, large and capital weapons across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer tiers, including all three polarized blasters. The seven profiles with explicit positive ship restrictions remain deferred under the maintainer's ordering. This cohort has no published deadspace or Abyssal variants; defensive mutation/unknown-effect checks also reject synthetic or changed stored profiles. Capital weapons without ship restrictions still need their real fitting resources and skills. No hull assembly, manufacturing, grants or market policy is expanded; world version remains 31.

Each turret must have 12/16/34/42 and exactly one known overload effect (3001 or 3025); polarized variants additionally have the complete paired 5994/5995 overrides. Effect 34 is targeted, not warp-safe and uses duration 51, capacitor 6, range 54, falloff 158 and tracking 160. Overload remains inactive. Fitting, loading and firing all revalidate the supported profile, including an item already saved in a fitted slot. Destroyed hull checks, transactional ammunition/capacitor/cooldown/NPC updates, the existing hit-quality model and continuous HUD effect ownership apply to every admitted size.

All **224 published hybrid charges** are supported: 208 ordinary/faction group-85 profiles, eight advanced railgun group-373 charges and eight advanced blaster group-377 charges. Loading and firing require matching size and either chargeGroup1 (604) or chargeGroup2 (605). This follows the actual catalog rather than assuming advanced ammunition requires a Tech II gun: several faction, officer and storyline guns also advertise a secondary advanced group. Ammunition skill requirements are checked on load and again on fire, independently of the gun's requirements. A wrong group/size or unsupported charge effect cannot pay, move a stack, consume a round or damage a target.

Charge effects 596/599/600/804 multiply optimal range, falloff, tracking and capacitor respectively, using source attributes 120/517/244/317 and their pinned defaults. Effect 1173 uses hidden source 779 to modify sentry/NPC orbit range (416), whose default is 500 m; it does not replace player turret optimal range. Void S retains its 0.75 optimal / 0.5 falloff / 0.75 tracking / +25% capacitor changes. Spike S retains 1.8 optimal and 0.25 tracking; Javelin S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking. Missing identity multipliers do not zero other attributes. Real magazine volume, partial unloads, paid reload/cycle deadlines and per-shot charge-rate consumption remain shared across all sizes.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Hybrid Turret each grant 5% damage per level only to modules requiring that skill. Matching railgun/blaster specializations grant 2% per level only to guns requiring that specialization; loading advanced ammo into an eligible faction gun does not invent a specialization bonus on the gun. All eight specialization types are represented, including capital. Generic gunnery selectors remain required-skill/group based, and the native brain uses the same table. The existing rookie hybrid optimal bonus is confined to Small Hybrid Turret selectors. Other hull-specific weapon traits remain separate work. Heat sinks continue to affect energy weapons only.

The polarized profiles require post-assignment operation **7**. Re-inspection of build 3662168's inert `dogma/const.pyj` confirms its position after ordinary multipliers and percent modifiers. `dogma/attributes/attribute.pyj`, `Attribute.Update` lines 362–376, clamps both the base and final modified value. The graph previously performed only the first clamp; it now implements final clamping and post-assignment. Polarized shield/armor source 1978 assigns 100 and hull source 1979 assigns 1; the catalog resonance ceilings then clamp all twelve to 1, meaning zero resistance. Damage Control, active hardeners and corvette resistance roles cannot survive that final override. Fitted phase-0 penalties remain while offline. Corvette role resistance bonuses now live in the shared graph before the override instead of being applied again afterward. Unfitting removes the override without healing saved damage.

Representative pinned values: 125mm Railgun II is 2.64 damage multiplier / 3.25 seconds / 1.505 GJ and requires Small Hybrid V plus Small Railgun Specialization I. Shadow Serpentis 200mm is 3.16 / 4.455 seconds / 3.612 GJ; Asine's Light Neutron is 5.145 / 3.5 seconds / 1.4161 GJ. 425mm Railgun II is 3.63 / 8.129 seconds / 21 GJ. Dual 1000mm Railgun II is 8.16756534 / 20 seconds / 300 GJ; Ion Siege Blaster II is 6.125625 / 10 seconds / 100 GJ. At Gunnery V/Rapid Firing V, cycles use 0.9 * 0.8; Controlled Bursts V applies 0.75 before the charge's cost modifier. The existing industry product boundary explicitly retains small Tech I hybrids while fitting expands.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **674 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. The focused hybrid/graph run passed **34 tests**. The 32 new cases cover the tier expansion and attribute corrections; existing ammunition, combat, fitting, industry, mining and other feature tests passed in the final full suite. The dedicated fixture includes 223 turret profiles, 224 charge profiles, 19 skills, 43 effects and 82 attribute definitions including transitive bounds. Legacy fixtures retain artificial attributes while loading complete effect metadata. Exhaustive eligibility, twelve representative paid shots across all sizes/tiers, magazine capacities/reload persistence, group/size rollback, module/ammo skill gates, native brain selectors, polarized offline/online restoration and stored-profile rejection have automated coverage. Larger-weapon shot fixtures explicitly use artificial fitting/capacitor budgets and establish no real Ibis fitting claim.

## Fitting expansion milestone 15: projectile turrets and ammunition

The pinned group-55 cohort contains **167 published projectile turrets**. All **160 unrestricted profiles** now fit and fire through the shared ammunition-fed turret path: small, medium, large and capital autocannons/artillery across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer variants, including all three polarized autocannons. Seven explicit ship-restricted profiles remain deferred. No published deadspace or Abyssal projectile profile occurs in this cohort. Synthetic mutations, unknown effects and changed default firing modes are rejected even through already stored fittings. No market policy, manufacturing product scope or world schema changes; world version remains 31.

Hybrid-specific helpers were generalized into typed turret rules and catalog adapters. Both families use default `projectileFired` effect 34 with the existing wire/FX path, real magazines, reload and paid-cycle cooldowns. Projectile weapons retain **zero capacitor need**, including at empty capacitor, and still consume charge-rate ammunition on misses. Magazine capacity comes from catalog volume: 125mm Gatling I with small charges holds 200; Dual 180mm I with medium charges 200; 1200mm Artillery II with large charges 40; Hexa 2500mm II with XL charges 80. All use ten-second reloads. Hybrid reloads retain their own five-second values.

All **144 published projectile charge profiles** are supported: 128 ordinary/faction group-83 types and eight each in advanced autocannon group 372 and artillery group 376. Catalog chargeGroup1/2 and exact size determine compatibility, including eligible faction/storyline/officer guns with advanced groups. The shared adapter validates the complete expected fitted effect set for each charge group, including optional capacitor modifiers on applicable hybrid charges. Missing or extra effects cannot be loaded or fired. Ordinary projectile charges use 596/600/1173; advanced projectile charges also use falloff effect 599. Four damage components, range, tracking and falloff retain each charge's real values. Hail S uses 0.5 optimal, 0.75 tracking and 0.75 falloff; Quake S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Projectile Turret each grant 5% damage per level only to modules requiring that size skill. Eight autocannon/artillery specialization skills grant 2% per level only to guns requiring the corresponding specialization. Surgical Strike selects projectile group 55 as well as the existing supported turret groups; generic gunnery cycle/range/tracking/falloff selectors retain their own ownership. Loading advanced ammunition requires its specialization independently of the gun and firing rechecks it. Reaper's existing catalog role source `rookieSPTDamageBonus` (1836) supplies 10% damage only to Small Projectile Turret selectors; Ibis's small-hybrid optimal bonus cannot leak into projectile guns. This adds that weapon-role calculation without claiming complete Reaper hull behavior.

Gyrostabilizers now affect an operational projectile weapon path. Their damage/cycle modifiers retain strongest-first stacking penalties: the second and third factors are approximately 0.8691199808003975 and 0.5705831435105602. Heat sinks and magnetic field stabilizers remain confined to their respective other weapon groups. Polarized autocannons reuse the final resistance overrides and catalog ceilings implemented for hybrid polarized weapons, including offline fitted penalties and restoration after unfitting. They do not heal saved damage.

The full projectile effect profile also contains legacy fitted derivations 253/254 and non-default targeted effect 263 (`barrage`). The catalog boundary checks those records and requires effect 34 as the unique default. Derived attributes 326/328 are calculated from the final skilled/fitted damage multiplier and falloff, with mutators 324/325; these stay distinct from advanced Barrage ammunition. A separate legacy barrage activation is not routed. Overload and that alternate activation remain explicit future work. The pure graph now supports pre-assignment -1 with an explicit native operator order; enumeration's unsigned sorting would place -1 last. This ordering is covered by a calculation whose correct result depends on pre-assignment preceding addition and percentages.

Client module priming now dispatches projectile group 55 to the authoritative combat attribute calculator, alongside hybrid group 74. It includes loaded charge modifiers, earned skill effects, legacy derived attributes and the paid reload deadline rather than returning only base fitting attributes. A scoped protocol regression checks the precise 125mm Gatling II/Hail readout: 3.6073125 damage multiplier, 2,268 ms cycle, 528 m optimal, 359.6625 tracking and 3,386.25 m falloff at the fixture's specified skills. The native brain advertises the same required-skill selectors. Existing HUD repeat ownership and paid shutdown are retained.

## Fitting expansion milestone 16: energy turrets and persistent frequency crystals

Build 3662168 contains **216 published group-53 energy weapons**. The **204 functional unrestricted profiles** now fit and fire: small, medium, large and capital beam/pulse weapons across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer variants, including three polarized pulse lasers. Eight explicit ship-restricted entries remain deferred. Lux Kontos 34580, Lux Xiphos 47446, Lux Ballistra 88348 and Lux Kopis 88349 have no normal fitting/firing dogma definitions and remain unavailable; publication alone does not establish a usable module. No published deadspace/Abyssal energy profile occurs in this cohort. Changed mutation, ship-restriction and additional firing-effect profiles are rejected at the authoritative fitting/firing boundary. No market policy, inventory grants or manufacturing product rules were changed.

Ordinary energy weapons use default **10 / targetAttack / effects.Laser**, with cycle 51, range 54, tracking 160, falloff 158 and capacitor 6. This shares the native laser activation/FX with the starter Civilian Gatling Railgun, whose built-in charge exception remains intact. The typed activation/effect names now describe laser firing rather than assuming effect 10 only belongs to civilian equipment. Energy weapons require a real loaded crystal. Their catalog reload value **1795 = 0.01 ms** is retained as a saved 100-FILETIME-tick deadline; it is not replaced with a hybrid/projectile reload. Only one crystal is loaded per weapon, with catalog volume and capacity still enforced. Dual Afocal Light Laser I 6633 also retains a non-default legacy barrage effect 263, validated as metadata while only its default laser firing is routed; alternate barrage activation remains pending. Paid cycle/reload cooldowns and stable repeated HUD effects use the existing owners.

All **200 published frequency crystals** are validated against their complete fitted-effect profiles: 184 ordinary/faction group 86 (596/804/1173), eight advanced beam group 374 (596/599/600/1173), and eight advanced pulse group 375 (those four plus 804). Exact charge size and the weapon's chargeGroup1/2 determine compatibility; eligible faction/officer weapons can use advanced crystals when their catalog allows it. Charge specialization requirements are checked during loading and again during firing. Scorch S uses its actual 1.4 optimal and 0.75 tracking multipliers, retaining hidden 1.5 entity fly-range separately. Gleam S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking. Swaps notify range, tracking, falloff, capacitor and reload attributes.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Energy Turret grant 5% damage per trained level only to their required-skill selectors. Eight beam/pulse specializations grant 2% per level only to weapons requiring the specific skill; loading advanced crystals does not grant a specialization damage bonus to an otherwise ineligible faction gun. Generic Gunnery, Rapid Firing, Controlled Bursts, Sharpshooter, Motion Prediction, Trajectory Analysis and group-selected Surgical Strike apply once. The native pilot brain uses the same typed skill modifiers. Impairor role attributes 1822 (-20% capacitor) and 1823 (+10% damage), from catalog effects 4990/4991, apply only to Small Energy Turret selectors. This implements those weapon role inputs without claiming complete Impairor hull behavior. Heat sinks modify operational energy guns with the existing strongest-first stacking penalties; magnetic field stabilizers and gyrostabilizers do not. Polarized lasers retain the fitted resistance overrides, including while offline.

Crystal condition is separate from ammunition quantity. Catalog **783 crystalVolatilityChance** is a probability, **784 crystalVolatilityDamage** is HP per wear event and **786 crystalsGetDamaged** enables wear; HP is attribute 9. Ordinary Tech I crystals have flag 0 and never wear. Faction crystals have flag 1, chance 1 and damage 0.00025, lasting 4,000 paid shots from pristine HP 1. Tech II crystals have chance 0.1 and damage 0.01; a draw below 0.1 causes wear. Independent server randomness supplies wear draws; pure rules accept explicit draws for deterministic boundary tests. Decimal accumulation prevents floating-point drift from granting a nominal extra shot. Misses still use and can break crystals. Failed activation rolls back resources, condition, damage and deadlines. A breaking crystal pays and resolves its final shot, is removed atomically, and prevents another shot until reloaded.

**World version 32** adds zero-default CrystalDamage to owned items without granting inventory or resetting resources. Loaded wearable crystals are individual singleton items. Unloading, same-type crystal swaps, cargo/hangar moves, unfit and restart retain the individual ID and wear instead of merging with fresh stacks. Existing wreck movement retains the owned row. Native magazine primes and damage notifications carry attribute 3 on the existing charge sublocation; quantity becomes zero when a crystal breaks. Saved worn items expose their condition in the typed inventory model and owned loose crystals answer ItemGetInfo in current cargo or the docked station hangar. This is crystal condition only; module overheating and module repair remain future work.

Pinned client evidence: inert marshal/code-object inspection of `shipModuleButton/moduleButtonTooltip.pyj` shows `GetCrystalDamage` calls `GetAccurateAttributeValue(chargeInfoItem.itemID, const.attributeDamage)`, and `GetChargeText` displays crystal damage as a percentage. `shipmodulebutton.pyj` hides the ordinary quantity label for crystal groups. No game Python was imported or executed. Archive SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. The [Carbon Blue source](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/tree/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997) was consulted again; existing Blue/DBRow and Destiny FX formats remain unchanged (Destiny revision 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f). Wear values come from the exact pinned catalog, rather than current balance documentation.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **759 .NET tests** passed in 11 minutes 8 seconds, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed across 322 files. The 42 new energy cases passed alongside the existing fitting, ammunition, combat, mining, propulsion, repair, inventory, industry and migration regressions. The isolated energy fixture contains 216 weapons, 200 crystals, 43 combat skills, 68 effects and 86 attribute metadata definitions. The hybrid and projectile tier fixtures also now contain all 43 shared combat skills (490 and 354 total type definitions respectively); comparison against their prior JSON preserved every existing weapon, charge and metadata record. This keeps their restart checks valid when training the expanded combat-skill enum. Tests exercise every functional profile, negative controls, representative shots across sizes/tiers, real crystal modifiers/capacitor payments, wear boundaries and 4,000-shot faction lifetime, last-shot destruction, independent miss wear, saved identity, failed transactions, native primes, skills, stacking and schema migration. Large/capital shot fixtures deliberately increase an Ibis's resource budgets; they do not establish a valid real corvette fit.

Native acceptance remains **pending** because the maintainer requested leaving game windows alone. No client window was controlled, running host restarted, normal save changed or deployment made. Laser FX, crystal tooltip wear, swap timing, break feedback and repeated HUD rings still need a copied-save client check when testing resumes.

## Fitting expansion milestone 17: local hull repairers across tiers

All **25 published group-63 Hull Repair Unit profiles** in build 3662168 are unrestricted and now supported: 18 Tech I/named, four Tech II and three storyline profiles across small, medium, large and capital sizes. This cohort contains no published faction, deadspace, officer or Abyssal profiles. Synthetic ship restrictions and mutations are rejected. Hull repairers use medium-power effect 13, online effect 16, default **26 / structureRepair / effects.StructureRepair**, and overload-duration effect 3002. The catalog adapter validates the complete fitting/default/effect profile and rejects malformed amount/duration/cost inputs. No market policy, inventory grants or manufacturing product rules changed.

Hull repair amount is **attribute 83**, separate from armor amount 84. Small Hull Repairer I 524 repairs 25 HP for 30 GJ over 30 seconds; Small Hull Repairer II 2355 repairs 30 HP for 30 GJ over 24 seconds. Small Inefficient 5693 uses 30 HP / 24 seconds at lower CPU; Small Integrative 21504 uses 45 HP / 20 seconds. Medium/large profiles retain their own fitting, amount, cost and duration. Capital Hull Repairer I 41511 uses 1,800 HP / 720 GJ / 60 seconds, Capital Hull Repairer II 41514 uses 2,160 HP / 864 GJ / 60 seconds, and capital Enduring 41512 and Compact 41513 use 1,980 HP with their distinct costs. Tests deliberately increase an Ibis's budgets for large/capital rules; they do not establish a real corvette fit.

Repair Systems 3393 effect 272 applies -5% duration per level through required-skill selector 3393. Capital Repair Systems 21803 effect 1635 applies -5% per level to selector 21803. Capital **hull** repairers require both, so both apply once: V/IV gives 60,000 × 0.75 × 0.8 = **36,000 ms**. Ordinary hull repairers receive only Repair Systems. Existing capital **armor** repairers retain their separate capital-only selector. Native skill brain effects and module primes match the authoritative calculation. Decimal conversion to FILETIME ticks prevents a binary artifact from adding an extra 100 ns tick while preserving legitimate fractional milliseconds.

Armor-specific schedule/controller/contact types were generalized into typed local-repair models carrying **RepairLayer.Armor/Hull**. Gameplay owns validated cycles, deadline conversion and layer-specific clamped health results. Persistence checks ownership, active ship, online singleton, correct rack/group, complete fitting profile, skills, health, resources and paid cooldown before committing the schedule and capacitor payment together. The scoped controller remains the single activation/repeat owner. Duplicate activation cannot buy a second cycle; the other layer's activation/deactivation effect is rejected. Armor call defaults and native armor wire names remain unchanged.

Repairs resolve at the end of the paid cycle. Multiple repairers add independent completions rather than sharing a passive stacking penalty. Excess repair is discarded without a capacitor refund. Settlement writes only the repaired layer, including when another layer's saved damage exceeds its current maximum, and leaves destroyed hull damage untouched. Manual stop finishes the paid cycle; docking/disconnect stop repeats, and restart restores at most that one payment without offline repeats. Warp remains safe for this catalog effect. Moving the module to another ship retains its deadline: a due old-ship repair settles on the original hull before a new cycle can start, preventing duplicate pending module keys or transferring repair to the new ship.

**World version 33** adds a zero-default Layer column to the historical WorldArmorRepair table. Existing rows become armor schedules while retaining module/ship IDs, amount, start/completion times and resources. The table name and indexes remain. A paid armor-cycle migration and repeated initialization verify preservation and idempotence; older migration fixtures reconstruct the pre-33 table before replay.

Pinned native evidence: `client/effects` registers **effects.StructureRepair** as **ImpactEffect**, with animationName **hullrepair**, merge flags [1,2], transform flags [0] and a 10,000 ms base visual duration. Armor's separate registration uses armorrepair. The existing timed FX carrier supplies actual module duration and saved start time for pilot and observer replay. Hull HUD effect 26 and damage attribute 3 remain distinct from armor. The client archive SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Consulted [Carbon Destiny](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny) and [Blue](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue) again; existing carriers require no packet-format changes (previously recorded Destiny 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f and Blue af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997).

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **796 .NET tests** passed in 12 minutes 9 seconds, the Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed across 326 files. The 37 new hull-repair cases passed alongside all existing regressions. Focused armor/hull checks also passed (52 cases), covering every hull profile, representative sizes/tiers, capital skill ownership, exact deadlines, clipping, failure rollback, restart, shutdown, independent layers, native HUD/FX selection, module transfer and version-32 migration. The isolated fixture contains 25 modules, three skills, ten effects and 27 attribute metadata definitions.

Native acceptance remains **pending** under the maintainer's request to leave game windows alone. No game server restart, deployment or normal-save mutation occurred. A copied-save client check still needs to verify fitting, start payment/end repair, repeat rings, manual stop, hull animation/observer replay and restored paid cycles. Record native observations separately from automated results.
## Fitting expansion milestone 18: missile fitting, ammunition and owner modifiers

Build 3662168's ordinary launcher cohort contains 145 published profiles. The 139 unrestricted profiles now have validated fitting and loading rules across groups 506/507/508/509/510/511/524/771/1245/1674, including civilian, Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, officer and polarized variants where present. The Defender launcher and five rapid-torpedo profiles have explicit ship restrictions and remain deferred. Bomb, probe, festival and other special launcher mechanics are separate families. This milestone does not implement missile activation or flight; existing turret firing rejects missile launchers without spending their rounds or damaging an NPC.

All 201 published compatible charges have validated missile effect/warhead profiles, including ordinary, faction, advanced and auto-targeting ammunition. Launchers select from all six native charge-group attributes 604–609, rather than turret charge size. Torpedo Launcher I uses selector 606; Republic Fleet Cruise accepts advanced cruise ammunition through selector 609. Volume determines magazine size: Light I 40 rounds, Rocket I 37, Rapid Light I 19, Torpedo I 30 and XL Cruise I 15. Normal reloads take ten seconds and rapid launchers 35 seconds; the real stacks and reload deadlines survive restart and partial/full unloading. Loading checks the ammunition's own skill requirements independently of the launcher. Civilian Light Missile Launcher consumes a high slot without inventing an effect-40 launcher hardpoint.

The fitting graph now includes loaded charge nodes. Character-domain OwnerRequiredSkill modifiers select implemented missile skills, while ordinary ship LocationRequiredSkill modifiers reach loaded ammunition for guidance enhancers. Six mixed drone upgrades remain unavailable because their drone selectors are still unsupported. Legacy skill effects 660/661/662/668 and specialization effect 1851 are normalized at the catalog boundary. Missile Launcher Operation and Rapid Launch affect launcher cycle; matching specializations affect only launchers requiring them. Base missile skills and Warhead Upgrades modify ammunition damage; auto-targeting ammunition also requires and receives its own skill. Bombardment, Projection, Guided Missile Precision and Target Navigation Prediction modify flight time, speed, explosion radius and explosion velocity. Skills remain exempt from equipment stacking penalties. Ibis role effect 5000/source 1827 adds kinetic damage to missile ammunition without changing other components or claiming full Ibis hull behavior.

Ballistic controls retain the character's missileDamageMultiplier (212) separately from the charge's four damage attributes. Inspection of pinned dogma.const and stackingNurfedAttribute corrects an earlier graph assumption: pre-multiply operator 0 belongs to dgmNurfableOperations. Multiple ballistic-control damage bonuses therefore diminish strongest-first, in their own operator bucket, just as their post-multiplied rate bonuses do. A single Damage Control pre-multiplier stays separate from post-percent resistance bonuses. Heat sinks, hybrid upgrades and gyrostabilizers cannot modify missiles. Guidance equipment shares each applicable charge attribute's stacking sequence, independently of exempt skills. Polarized launchers reuse the shared fitted resistance overrides, including while offline.

Native charge primes now use effective loaded missile attributes and include quantity; the pilot brain emits owner-required-skill effects as `ORS` in CharacterEffects, targeting the pilot. Launcher priming includes its saved reload deadline. Pure application rules cap damage by target signature and speed. Catalog 1353 already stores the transformed reduction exponent (light 0.604, rocket 0.644, torpedo 0.944); it is not transformed through log(drf)/log(5.5) again. These rules are preparation for delayed impacts, not an instant-hit missile implementation.
