## Fitting expansion milestone 19: missile motion, swept contact and native balls

The shared missile flight component now implements launch, guidance, exponential inertia, contact and lifetime independently of hosting/persistence. It is the motion component for the upcoming launcher firing path; no module activation, ammunition payment or NPC damage has been connected to it yet. The existing turret operation continues to reject launchers. This milestone adds no world schema or running-server change; world version remains 33.

Reference: [Carbon Destiny revision 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072), particularly Thunkers.cpp's PyLaunchMissile and WriteBallToStream, Ballpark.cpp's EvolveMissile/GotoThrust/Integrate/StopAllFollowers, IDstConstants.h and python/destiny/test/ballpark/evolve/test_missile.py. Build 3662168's inert missile-space-object inspection and code.ccp hash remain as recorded in milestone 18. There are no new live client observations under the game-window hold.

Unaimed launches inherit platform velocity plus max(150 m/s, platform maximum speed) along its movement direction, or the supplied rear orientation when stationary. Aimed launches start at a unit 1 m/s velocity toward the target, matching Carbon's public tests. MissileFollow's initial absolute guidance point is velocity direction times 1e16. Native evolution uses one-second ticks; the 800 ms straight-launch condition occupies the first tick. Later ticks aim at the target's current position plus its velocity times distance/missile maximum speed. The negative follow range makes this a center interception. Mass times agility / 1,000,000 supplies inertia, with the same analytic exponential-drag solution as Carbon. Stable small-ratio forms avoid cancellation for large inertia. Guided missile thrust remains full near the target; ordinary initial/orphaned Goto retains its fourth-power taper. After losing a target, a missile keeps flying along its velocity, without reacquiring or dealing contact damage, until its original lifetime expires.

Contact follows the analytic missile curve and the target's supplied linear motion within each tick. An adaptive sweep bounds exponential curvature before solving relative segment entry into the sum of the two radii; it searches earlier intervals first. The curve tolerance is 0.1 mm, subject to floating-point coordinate precision. This catches fast and crossing targets between endpoints, avoids false hits where a chord cuts inside a curved trajectory, and permits a faster target to escape. Fractional final ticks stop at the exact lifetime budget. Impact and expiry are terminal states. This is Eden's explicit contact policy for nonmassive missiles; it does not claim to implement Carbon's full general-purpose collision response. The core accepts the target's availability from the owning world operation, which still needs integration.

The missile charge catalog now builds a typed drive from catalog mass/radius and effective speed/agility/lifetime, validating finite positive motor inputs before loading. All 201 published charge profiles pass the stronger admission check. A malformed motor with a valid warhead cannot move rounds or create a reload deadline.

Native serialization supports navigation mode 5 / MISSILE. After the normal free-ball header/kinematics and formation byte, it writes target ID (int64), negative combined follow range (float32), launching ship ID (int64), launch stamp (int32), and absolute guidance point (three float64 values). Projectiles use the free flag and have no health entry. The slim uses charge category 8, pilot ownerID, sourceShipID and a list-valued launchModules field. Snapshot and incremental additions share the same writer. A lost-target missile uses Goto mode with only its guidance point, retaining no stale follower payload. Completed or nonfinite states cannot be streamed as live balls. Grouped launcher operation remains pending despite the native field's list shape.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **886 .NET tests** passed in 15 minutes 27 seconds. All 35 focused motion/native/catalog checks passed. The 34 new cases cover Carbon's public launch and twenty-tick guidance expectations, inherited platform speed/orientation, velocity-led guidance, moving/crossing targets, tunneling, false chord hits and small curved-path contacts, escape and fractional lifetime boundaries, lost-target coasting, stable large inertia, invalid geometry/motors, exact native byte fields/slim types, orphan payloads, terminal-state exclusion, and snapshot/addition agreement. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 337 files. All 201 missile charge profiles retain admission under motor validation.

Remaining integration includes atomic launch/payment/cooldown/projectile storage, shared NPC impact damage and single bounty/wreck ownership, auto-target aggressor acquisition, HUD cycle ownership, world ticking, observer visibility/removal, and transition/restart behavior. No operational missile-fire acceptance is claimed from the component tests. Native validation remains pending under the maintainer's instruction to leave game windows alone.

## Fitting expansion milestone 20: paid missile firing and shared NPC impacts

The launcher firing path now connects the 139 unrestricted ordinary launcher profiles and 201 validated charges from milestone 18 to milestone 19's flight rules. This includes the supported static tiers and sizes; restricted launchers and special bomb/probe/festival mechanics remain deferred. Existing module and ammunition skills, magazine volumes, guidance modifiers, Ibis kinetic role and diminished ballistic-control bonuses feed actual shots. Launcher activation requires a real owned online high-slot module, sufficient compatible rounds, completed reload/cycle deadlines, a live source hull and an available NPC. Ordinary ammunition requires an authoritative target lock and targeting/visibility range. Missiles consume no capacitor. Rejected operations preserve ammunition and prior deadlines.

One SQLite transaction pays the round, saves the next launcher deadline, snapshots the effective motor/warhead and inserts the projectile. Later skill or fitting changes do not rewrite an in-flight shot. Catalog attribute 644 (`aimedLaunch`) selects Carbon's launch mode. Flight ages start at shared FILETIME one-second boundaries, while paid cycle deadlines retain their actual activation time. Connection ticks advance shared world projectiles under a transaction, independently of which observer triggers the tick. Each saved boundary runs once. Target positions use saved NPC motion and linear velocity within the missile tick; contact uses the swept analytic path. Impact damage then applies the target's current signature, speed, defenses and remaining health. Losing a lock stops future ordinary shots but does not erase an already launched missile; losing the target makes the missile coast without reacquiring.

Automatic ammunition effect 104 acquires only a live same-system NPC whose saved aggression targets the launching hull. Attribute 2700 supplies its acquisition range, capped by Eden's visibility range. The nearest eligible aggressor wins, with ID as a deterministic tie break. An idle nearer NPC is not eligible. The native call may omit a target lock/ID for this ammunition. This is the current NPC encounter policy; player combat and arbitrary non-NPC targets are not introduced.

World version **34** adds `WorldMissile` with source, target, launch times, typed flight/warhead snapshot and an optional typed impact outcome. Source-generated JSON serialization uses explicit model metadata, excluding computed vector properties. Projectile IDs start at 6,000,000,000,000 and use SQLite AUTOINCREMENT so cleanup cannot reuse them. Migration retains fitting, charges and existing paid deadlines, without granting items or skills. A projectile keeps its launching module ID even if inventory later removes the module; it deliberately has no inventory foreign key. Completed reported/empty outcomes can be removed after 120 seconds while a system has active missile work; unreported impacts remain available to their owner.

NPC damage, recharge, retaliation, kill rewards and wreck creation now share one persistence helper with turret combat. A launch starts retaliation rather than waiting for contact. Terminal steps are settled by contact time then projectile ID inside the shared transaction. Simultaneous arrivals cannot destroy the same NPC twice, pay two bounties or create duplicate wrecks. Impact outcomes are claimed once by the firing pilot and queued before flight updates; the damage log identifies the ammunition as the weapon. Observers receive health, wreck/NPC changes and projectile state without claiming another pilot's combat log or reward.

The scoped missile controller owns the paid HUD cycle. Duplicate activation does not buy another shot, repeated payments do not send idle/restart effect pairs, and manual stop finishes its paid interval. Every repeat rechecks the saved magazine, fitting/skills and target. Native module primes can read the active effect from the same controller. Flight snapshots and incremental additions include projectiles with the correct source/module/target identities; subsequent updates reconcile position/velocity, switch a lost-target ball to Goto and remove impact balls with the terminal flag. No turret firing effect is substituted for missile flight.

Disconnect, docking, warp initiation, accepted gate jump, hull loss and restart cancel pending projectiles without refunding rounds or cooldowns. The shared store also checks that the source is still the active in-space hull in the same system, so a stale observer cannot settle shots after a saved docking/loss transition. A lifetime that passed without observation does not replay retrospective damage. Boot expires remaining flying rows; completed impacts/rewards cannot be applied again. These are explicit Eden transition/offline policies, not a claim of the full cluster simulation behavior.

References: [Carbon Destiny revision 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072), including Thunkers.cpp, Ballpark.cpp, Ball.cpp and the public missile tests. Inert inspection of pinned build 3662168's ModuleButton.ActivateEffect/GetRelevantEffect and godma EffectWrapper/StateManager confirms the launcher Activate call and charge-selected targeting requirement. The missile space object's Prepare reads sourceShipID, launchModules and followId; its native model schedules its warheads from time-to-target. Michelle's RemoveBall terminal flag selects destruction handling. The previously recorded code.ccp hash is unchanged. Automated ball/removal checks do not establish visible launch, trail, collision callback or explosion behavior; those need direct native observation.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **916 .NET tests** passed in **17 minutes 13 seconds**, covering the 886 existing cases and 30 new operational missile cases. All 30 focused missile cases also passed independently, including representative ordinary/named/Tech II/faction/officer and XL shots, real payment/cooldown rollback, delayed contact, signature application, automatic aggressor filtering, mixed-tier ballistic diminishing returns at zero capacitor, immutable launch skills, restart/cancellation, v33 migration, concurrent kill settlement and production dispatch/HUD/observer packets. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 343 files. Tests used isolated temporary worlds/catalogs; larger launchers used artificial fitting budgets rather than claiming their Ibis fixture is a legal in-game fit.

Native/deployment acceptance remains pending because the maintainer instructed us to leave game windows alone. No server/client restart, staging, game input or normal-save mutation was performed. Required visible checks are launcher/ammunition behavior, moving missile/warhead/impact effects, expiry/lost-target behavior, observers and late joins, uninterrupted repeat circles, reward/wreck ownership and paid-state conservation through transitions. The Windows client guide records the reproducible checks for when testing resumes.

## Fitting expansion milestone 21: scripted tracking, guidance and sensor modules

Build 3662168 contains **32 published modules** across these three unrestricted families: group 213 has 11 tracking computers, group 1396 has five missile guidance computers, and group 212 has 16 sensor boosters. All are now admitted through their complete fitted/online/default/overload-metadata profiles, across their actual Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer tiers. This cohort has no published deadspace or Abyssal profiles. Synthetic ship restrictions, Abyssal metadata, missing defaults and unsupported active modifier functions are rejected. Remote tracking/sensor boosters, guidance disruptors and drone tracking modules remain separate families. No inventory/skill/money grants, market policy changes or manufacturing additions are made.

The default effects are **4559 / gunneryMaxRangeFalloffTrackingSpeedBonus**, **6135 / missileGuidanceComputerBonus4**, and **2670 / sensorBoosterActivePercentage**. Each is category 1, warp-safe, pays capacitor attribute 6 and uses duration 73. Tracking Computer I costs 7 GJ per ten seconds and adds 5% optimal, 10% falloff and 10% tracking. Tech II costs 10 GJ and adds 7.5%/15%/15%; Cormack's costs 14 GJ and adds 9.9%/19.8%/21%. Missile Guidance Computer I costs 7 GJ and adds 4% missile speed/lifetime, 5.5% explosion velocity and -5.5% explosion radius. Tech II retains 7 GJ and uses 5.5%/5.5%/8.25%/-8.25%; the Caldari Navy and Republic Fleet variants retain their distinct range/application strengths. Sensor Booster I costs 10 GJ and adds 25% lock range/scan resolution and 40% sensor strength; Tech II costs 12 GJ and gives 30%/30%/48%. All other profiles retain their own catalog values rather than inheriting a tier-wide multiplier.

The existing typed dogma graph applies tracking computers only to equipment requiring Gunnery, and guidance computers to loaded ammunition requiring Missile Launcher Operation. Sensor boosters modify the hull's target range, scan resolution and four sensor strengths. Their equipment modifiers share the existing attribute/operator stacking sequences with passive tracking enhancers, guidance enhancers and signal amplifiers; earned skills remain exempt. Manual firing/target-lock operations read these effective values. Sensor-strength attributes are primed, but this does not implement the separate ECM/jamming family.

All **seven published scripts** are supported as real owned, reusable one-unit sublocations: Optimal Range/Tracking Speed (group 907), Targeting Range/Scan Resolution/ECCM (910), and Missile Range/Missile Precision (1400). Complete script effect sets are validated, including native `otherID` ItemModifier targets and post-percent operator 6. The graph adapts those modifiers explicitly to the containing module, without widening the general passive modifier parser. A script doubles selected source bonuses and suppresses alternatives by 100%; script changes themselves do not acquire an equipment stacking penalty. Tracking scripts retain their additional missile-related metadata rather than silently dropping it. Group, charge size and launcher compatibility prevent using a script in the wrong family. Empty modules work with their unscripted base bonuses.

Script loading/unloading uses the existing owned-magazine transactions, source/destination checks and saved deadlines. Capacity and the explicit one-script limit prevent stacked scripts in a module. Catalog reload attribute 1795 is **0.01 ms**, retained as 100 FILETIME ticks rather than replaced with a gun reload. Repeats never consume or damage scripts. Changing a running module's script is rejected even if its previous deadline has passed but its active marker has not yet been removed; stop and complete the paid interval first. Wrong-family/size/effect requests preserve stacks, the existing magazine and its deadline. Swap/unload/restart conserve all script units.

The scoped support controller pays capacitor, adds a connection-owned `ActiveFittedModule` marker and saves a cycle deadline in one transaction. It retains one HUD active effect across ordinary repeats, rechecks each payment, finishes the paid interval on manual stop, and removes the bonus when another interval cannot be paid. Docking, gate/hull transitions and disconnect stop the live effect; warp remains valid for these effects. Restart clears active markers while preserving scripts, capacitor expenditure and deadlines. Existing schema **34** already owns those tables; no migration is needed. Duplicate activation does not pay twice. Invalid wire shapes are declined before advancing the controller. The current pilot, fitted slot, online state, skill requirements, live hull and requested effect are checked before payment.

Native module primes include effective scripted source bonuses and the controller's active effect. Start/stop also notify effective turret, loaded missile and hull sensor attributes. Repeats update timed space effects without resetting the HUD effect. Shared-space contacts and snapshots carry typed support contacts, so existing observers and fresh snapshots replay the same effect. Tracking/guidance use catalog GUID `effects.TurretWeaponRangeTrackingSpeedMultiplyActivate`; sensors use `effects.ElectronicAttributeModifyActivate`, through the existing timed OnSpecialFX adapter. Source/module/ship identities and paid start time remain explicit. This reuses the established Blue/Destiny wire path and does not change Carbon simulation or ball encoding.

References: read-only pinned catalog types/typedogma/dogmaeffects/dogmaattributes, plus the inert build-3662168 ModuleButton/EffectWrapper activation inspection recorded in milestones 18–20. The same code.ccp SHA-256 and Carbon Blue/Destiny source revisions recorded there remain in use. The static fixtures contain 43 types (32 modules, seven scripts, three prerequisite skills and Ibis), 32 effects and 149 attribute definitions; they contain no normal world/save data. Integration fixtures use artificial medium-slot/CPU/power budgets where multiple large test combinations require them, and place ships outside physical celestial/station collision volumes for targeting acceptance.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **957 .NET tests** passed in **18 minutes**, including the 916 existing cases and 41 new support-module cases. All 41 focused support-module tests also passed independently. Coverage includes full catalog admission, representative tier costs/bonuses, all seven scripts, gun/miner isolation, loaded missile flight/application, mixed active/passive stacking in both activation orders, script conservation and active-swap rejection, unsupported-profile/skill/payment rollback, restart cleanup, unpaid repeats, production dispatch/primes, stable HUD effects, extended target lock/removal, and observer/snapshot effects. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 350 files. All changed behavior was tested in isolated temporary worlds/catalogs.

Deployment and native acceptance remain pending under the maintainer's game-window hold. No game-window input, staged deployment, running-server/client restart or normal-save mutation occurred. Native checks still need to cover actual fit/script drag-and-drop, tooltip/target-lock/application values, tier-specific costs, continuous repeat rings, warp and paid stop, observer/snapshot visuals, and reconnect/restart conservation. The Windows client guide records those checks separately from the automated evidence.


## Fitting expansion milestone 22: tackle and warp navigation

The resumed module goal prioritizes unrestricted tackle/navigation before ship-restricted equipment. Build 3662168 has **82 published unrestricted static profiles** in this cohort: 18 webifiers (group 65), 53 disruptors/scramblers (52), eight warp core stabilizers (315) and three hyperspatial accelerators (1289). Every static Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, officer and heavy profile present in these groups is admitted through its complete catalog effects. Five Abyssal profiles remain excluded because per-item mutations are not implemented. Cloaks and micro jump systems remain separate work. No normal-save grants, market changes or manufacturing offers are added.

Targeted defaults are **6426 / remoteWebifierFalloff**, **39 / warpDisrupt**, and **5934 / warpScrambleBlockMWDWithNPCEffect**. They require an online medium-slot module, its earned skills, a live source hull, an actual locked player ship or NPC in the same system, target range and module range. Payment, the live contact and the saved cycle deadline commit together. Each repeat revalidates and pays its actual catalog capacitor cost; Propulsion Jamming reduces qualifying costs by 5% per earned level. Civilian web/disruptor profiles do not require that skill and receive no discount. Civilian disruption strength is genuinely zero. Manual stop completes the paid cycle. Unpaid repeats, target-lock loss, transitions, hull loss and disconnect remove live effects; restart clears effects while retaining capacitor expenditure and deadlines.

Web strength uses source speedFactor (20) multiplied by target stasisWebifierResistance (2115, default 1). Max velocity (37) shares the existing strongest-first dogma stacking sequence, with independent positive/negative sequences and skills exempt. Tech I is -50%, Tech II -60%, X5 -55%, and the civilian module -35%; every other profile retains its own values. All 18 static web profiles have zero falloff (2044). Nonzero or unsupported falloff metadata is rejected. NPC maximum and cruise speed are scaled together to preserve the catalog cruise/max ratio; actual NPC integration and native SetMaxSpeed both consume those values. Player motion retains inertia rather than truncating velocity when a web starts or ends.

Disruption strength (105) adds to target warpScrambleStatus (104) without a stacking penalty. Warp is denied when the total is positive: equal core and hostile strength permits it. Scramblers also add activationBlocked (1349) to MWDs requiring High Speed Maneuvering, cancel a running MWD, and restore its online signature/mass/speed policy. A disruptor does not block MWDs. Targeting remains available during warp alignment, so new points cancel an existing alignment order; actual warp entry clears targeting. Subcapital gate travel and ordinary station docking remain available. Micro jump stoppers remain catalog validation metadata until that module family is implemented.

Warp core effect **8121 / warpCoreStabilizerActive** is a self-only, non-warp-safe low-slot activation. It pays 80 GJ, grants -2 disruption status for its own duration (10 seconds Tech I, 15 Tech II, 18 Aura), forces a single cycle, and retains the 150-second post-cycle reactivation delay (669) through stop/restart. Shared sweeps expire core strength even if the owner's controller has not advanced. Online scan resolution, targeting range and drone bandwidth penalties use the catalog's own modifiers. Only one stabilizer can fit; offline removes its online penalties. Drone bandwidth math does not implement drone combat. Core strength does not bypass scrambler MWD blocking.

The three hyperspatial accelerators use online effect 5900 to add source warpSpeedAddition (1950) to warpSpeedMultiplier (600), with a maximum of three fitted. The effective baseWarpSpeed (1281) times multiplier now drives both saved pilot flight and in-session fitting refresh; raw hull values previously hid this fitted bonus from actual warp timing. Basic/Experimental/Prototype add 0.2/0.25/0.3 AU/s on the Ibis's base of 1.

Typed source contacts feed the existing timed native effects: `effects.ModifyTargetSpeed`, `effects.WarpDisrupt`, `effects.WarpScramble`, and the core's catalog `effects.Invulnerability`. Source repeats preserve the single HUD active effect. The victim also receives named `OnEwarStart`/`OnEwarEnd` notifications with exactly `(sourceBallID, moduleID, targetBallID, buffSlot)`. Pinned buffBarConst names are `webify`, `warpScrambler` and `warpScramblerMWD`; these differ from the module effect names. Continuous repeats do not churn victim status indicators. Effective speed, disruption status, MWD activation block and capacitor/cooldown values use explicit module attribute notifications.

Schema **35** adds WorldTackle with owned source/module identities, polymorphic ship/NPC target, paid attributes and start/expiry. Migration from version 34 is transactional. Live rows have no polymorphic target foreign key; sweeps validate target existence, system and hull state. Source item/ship/pilot references use normal ownership foreign keys. Restart removes live rows and active markers while retaining resource/cooldown rows. Native Blue/Destiny encoding is reused.

References: read-only pinned catalog types/typedogma/dogmaeffects/dogmaattributes and inert inspection of tactical.OnEwarStart/End, OnEwarOnConnect, dogma.effects.GetEwarTypeByEffectID and buffBarConst in unchanged code.ccp SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Carbon Destiny revision `114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072`, Ballpark.cpp SetMaxSpeed and Thunkers.cpp PySetMaxSpeed, confirms the native speed update and inertia boundary. CCP's [warp scrambling/disruption reference](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/115004925705-Warp-Scrambling-and-Warp-Disruption) supports additive strength, equality and the distinct MWD policy; its [stacking reference](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203280381-Bonuses-and-Stacking-Penalties) distinguishes absolute warp strength from penalized bonuses. The isolated fixture contains 91 types, 26 effects and 135 attribute definitions. Automated combinations use artificial CPU/power/capacitor and rack budgets; live acceptance uses legal Ibis fits in a copied save.

During manual acceptance, the maintainer found that the safety selector could not change to yellow. The host trace confirmed native `crimewatch.SetSafetyLevel` calls reached Eden but had no handler. The existing GetClientStates response also hardcoded full safety. The handler now validates the exact 0/1/2 wire values, saves a typed per-pilot safety preference and returns Python None; GetClientStates returns that saved preference. Pinned crimewatchSvc.SetSafetyLevel updates its own state and scatters OnSafetyLevelChanged after a successful reply, so the server does not duplicate that notification. OnSessionChanged reads the fourth GetClientStates member after ship/system transitions. Schema **36** adds a default-full SafetyLevel to WorldPilot; migration from 35 and new saves preserve full safety until a pilot chooses otherwise. Settings survive restart/reconnect and are isolated between pilots. This enables the native selector; full criminal/suspect timers, CONCORD, security penalties and duel mechanics remain unimplemented. The local player-versus-player test requires red safety because yellow still prevents criminal actions.

The next manual check exposed the pinned client's independent **Alpha clone** restriction: Alpha cannot disable safety in high security space even when the selector RPC works. `clonegrade.const` in the same pinned archive defines Alpha/Omega as 0/1 and the Alpha training multiplier as 0.5. Schema **37** adds a saved per-account CloneGrade, defaulting to Alpha for both migrated and new accounts. Native subscriptionMgr.GetCloneGrade and pilot/account reads now use that saved grade. Training uses half rate and the existing skill/SP caps for Alpha; Omega uses full rate and removes those two Alpha limits while preserving prerequisites, level validation and queue limits. The two accounts in the isolated copied acceptance save were explicitly set to Omega, and fresh development clients were launched. No normal account was upgraded and no external subscription was changed.

The maintainer then observed severe input delay and periodic recovery of the webbed ship's speed. The broad regression run was stopped to separate development load from live behavior; it had been using the runner's default processor-based concurrency on a 20-logical-processor machine. The test assembly now copies an explicit xunit.runner.json with four conservative workers, following [xUnit's configuration reference](https://xunit.net/docs/config-xunit-runner-json). This limits later test load; it does not by itself prove that every reported live delay is resolved. Slow native RPC processing and pilot updates (250 ms or more) now log elapsed time, with RPC dispatch and subsequent updates separated, without logging payloads.

The web discontinuity was a connection-order race: shared cleanup deleted a row at its nominal paid end before the source connection renewed the next paid interval. Remote rows now remain owned by their source controller, which atomically renews the row or explicitly stops it. Shared cleanup still removes invalid hull/module/location/target states, and disconnect/restart clears connection-owned effects. NPC web reads use the same ownership rule. This follows the lifetime already used by other connection-owned active module markers. The source captures wall and monotonic cycle starts before payment so its row and native phase share a start time. A regression deliberately advances the victim first at three consecutive cycle boundaries, then advances the source: actual webbed speed and scrambler strength remain unchanged, EWAR does not end/start, and explicit cancellation restores both. All 40 focused tackle cases passed after this correction. The updated host and fresh Omega clients were staged on the copied acceptance save for a further manual check.

With the broad regression runner stopped, timing logs still showed real multi-second pilot updates (including 6,136 ms) and module dispatches over 1,300 ms. A bounded read-only probe against the copied save and full catalog measured two consecutive pilots' fitting calculations at 419-504 ms. The nonpooled catalog connection was being reopened for individual queries throughout a fitting graph. FittingCalculation now explicitly keeps that connection open for its owning catalog context; disposal still closes it, without introducing pooled handles or cached mutable gameplay state. The same probe measured 53-70 ms for two warm reads after the change (110 ms on the first measured pair). This is a measured calculation improvement, distinct from native end-to-end latency. The broad regression run is bounded to four workers; it is not run during the active manual acceptance check.

Native acceptance: selected **Eden Pilot**, opened the existing Inventory view and selected its active **Ibis**; its cargo view showed 0/125 m3. Opened Fitting and observed Webifier II/Scrambler I plus the Prototype Hyperspatial Accelerator, with **3.30 AU/s** warp speed and **368.8 m/s** maximum speed. Undocked Pilot and Wing, selected the other ship, and issued Wing's native **1,000 m Orbit** before manual takeover. The maintainer then performed the module checks, identified the safety/Alpha restrictions and repeat-boundary gap described above, and after the updated build explicitly confirmed: **“The modules work as intended.”** Host traces showed the saved red safety selection, native module activations and victim OnEwarStart/End notifications. That report confirms the exercised test fits; it does not establish individual native acceptance of all 82 tiers, NPC web motion, every observer/snapshot path or all transitions. Automated coverage remains separate. All staging, fixture skills and module loadouts used the copied test save; the normal save was not changed.

Final automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **1,010 .NET tests** passed in **8 minutes 10 seconds**, including the 957 previous cases, 40 tackle cases, eight safety cases and five clone-grade cases. The focused tackle run also passed all 40 cases independently after the cycle-boundary correction; the account/safety/training focus passed all 20 selected cases. Existing warp-order assertions were updated to exercise actual warp entry, with synthetic ships clear of collision volumes, while retaining the paid-cycle and hull-loss checks. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy cases); formatting verification passed across 361 files and the 140-character width policy. The full run used the four-worker configuration. Read-only production-catalog performance measurements and the maintainer's native acceptance are recorded separately above. Native end-to-end latency after the final connection-lifetime improvement remains unmeasured; the measured fitting bottleneck is substantially reduced.

The maintainer requested stopping after this milestone. The module goal is paused after committing and pushing this verified milestone; no subsequent module family is started.

## Shared ship motion and collision correction

The supplied 2026-11-03 recording shows Eden Pilot approaching Eden Wing in two Ibises in Jita. At approximately four seconds their overviews report 245 m and 64 m, at five seconds 110 m and 27 m, and near nine seconds 0 m and 55 m. These independently refreshed labels demonstrate sustained disagreement, without identifying the exact packet responsible for an individual visual jump.

The investigation found three related causes. Player movement ran separately for each connection and considered static collision volumes, while native player/NPC records enabled Massive collisions. NPC pursuit and return movement advanced from combat calls and encounter reads. Shared player samples carried no source stamp, so an older sample could be labeled with the observer's newer simulation time. A stopped peer also bypassed periodic position reconciliation.

The shared coordinator now advances all registered players in a system from a frozen start-of-tick sample. Server persistence loads NPCs and their effective catalog attributes in the same transaction, invokes Gameplay navigation and collision rules, and commits all affected player/NPC positions and NPC velocities before publishing the result. A stopped ship remains a collision participant. The coordinator retains 32 ticks of movement history, recovers a delayed owner from a complete current sample when that history expires, and rejects old connection publications after a newer tick commits. Snapshot and peer reads share the movement lock. NPC wall-clock movement retains its existing one-second catch-up limit. Combat payments, weapon cooldowns, detection and aggression remain owned by their existing operations.

The collision adaptation uses the staged client's exact carbon_destiny revision, **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f**, recorded in tq/carbon.json. Carbon's legacy Gradient, Potential, CollideTwoSpheres and Integrate determine moving-sphere contact, base-mass radial velocity exchange, mass-times-agility integration, the 0.85 collision acceleration multiplier and overlap recovery with one metre of clearance. Coincident centers use the reference's deterministic ID-based normal. Course orders survive a ship bump. Existing rigid-object stopping remains in the player navigation path. Ships in warp and non-solid entities are excluded from ship contact. [Carbon legacy collision response](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp#L2748-L3010) | [Sphere contact](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Collision.cpp#L106-L145) | [Legacy defaults](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Settings.h#L7-L12) | [Carbon license](carbon-license.md)

Read-only inspection of build 3662168's michelle.Park.RealFlushState confirms SynchroniseToSimulationTime(eventStamp) precedes ordinary Destiny event application. Player samples and NPC contacts now carry their authoritative source tick. Native Destiny predicts the common collision trajectory for owners and observers, including stationary ships. Absolute position and velocity restoration is reserved for missed samples and discontinuities, so normal contact keeps the native interpolation and rotation history. Ordinary Follow/Orbit commands are sent when the course changes, with a current course replay for a viewer that missed samples. Moving NPCs receive stamped course changes and missed-sample recovery. Initial AddBalls/SetState includes the current course and velocity, including a drifting idle NPC.

Set the server process environment variable EDEN_TRACE_MOTION=1 to print committed tick-stamped surface ranges for nearby player/player and player/NPC pairs. This diagnostic is disabled by default.

During initial native acceptance the maintainer reported twitches near 0.0 m/s and sudden angle changes at contact. Carbon's SetBallVelocity calls CalculateYawPitchRoll(true), immediately snapping yaw/pitch to velocity. A zero velocity instead faces the last goto point, which can lie behind a ship that just overshot an approach. Ordinary course updates avoid SetBallVelocity, and normal predicted motion sends no periodic or per-contact position/velocity reset. Exact velocity restoration is retained for missed-history recovery, warp initialization and the pre-existing rigid-object stop. The maintainer then reported a remaining twitch in observed ships. Carbon SetBallPosition writes both mOldPos and mNewPos, collapsing the interpolation interval even when the supplied coordinate is correct. Per-contact and periodic position resets were removed too. Regression coverage asserts that ongoing bumps emit neither position nor velocity setters and that two resting ships remain quiet through 25 ticks. [Position interpolation reset](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp#L4742-L4772) [Velocity setter and orientation snap](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp#L4774-L4809) | [Legacy facing calculation](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ball.cpp#L333-L435)

The first no-reset build still diverged during live contact: the supplied side-by-side screenshot showed **98 m** in Pilot and **2,485 m** in Wing. The maintainer described Wing repeating the approach/bump sequence. Inspection found that the one-second connection timer includes operation time, so it can legitimately skip a sampled Destiny tick. Treating any two-tick sample gap as missing commands triggered an unnecessary state recovery and course replay. Inferred observer courses could also carry the later sample tick instead of the original input tick.

SharedSpace now retains sequenced, stamped navigation orders independently of movement samples. The observer consumes each original order once, at the owner's original tick. Shared simulation transitions enter the same order stream once, and owner adoption avoids republishing them. A delayed observer re-seeds only when command history was actually lost or its movement sample exceeds the retained 32-tick window. NPC sample gaps use that same retention limit. New snapshots seed the current state and the corresponding order cursor. A regression delays the observer by two ticks repeatedly and verifies original-tick delivery with no position/velocity reset.

On the event-stream build the diagnostic trace showed matching heading commands at tick **13**, Follow at **28**, Stop at **53**, and the next Follow at **56** for both clients. No position/velocity setter appeared during ordinary contact. The maintainer subsequently reported that the behavior was much better. The maintainer retained the game controls for the Perimeter NPC check, then requested that the same recovery correction be assumed to cover NPCs while this milestone is finished. NPC visual acceptance remains unverified. Automated coverage includes player/NPC and NPC/NPC contact, atomic saved positions, no duplicate NPC step from a second viewer, and benign skipped NPC samples.

Automated acceptance on 2026-11-03 passed pinned tool/solution restore, build with zero warnings/errors, and all **1,021 .NET tests** in **8 minutes 31 seconds**. The focused navigation/collision/NPC suite passed **61 cases** before the final NPC sample-gap regression extension, which is included in the full passing run. Coverage includes the Carbon head-on response, glancing/unequal-mass contact, coincident overlap, non-contact flybys, warp exclusion, stationary ships being pushed, connection update-order independence, committed peer positions, persistent player/NPC and NPC/NPC contact, repeated-tick deduplication, expired movement history, stale publication rejection, exact original command stamps, skipped observer/NPC samples and quiet settled ships.

Native acceptance used two build-3662168 clients against a copied save under the local Eden Lab collision-20261103 directory. Eden Pilot approached the stopped Eden Wing in Jita, visibly pushed it, and stopped using the native Stop Ship control. Subsequent maintainer-controlled approach/stop checks exposed and helped isolate the interpolation resets and sample-gap replay described above. The maintainer reported that the event-stream revision was much better. Perimeter NPC testing was started by the maintainer, who then explicitly asked to assume the same recovery fix applies and finish. NPC visual acceptance remains **assumed at the maintainer's request**. The final NPC-only adjustment replaces the two-tick reset trigger with the retained-history limit and is covered by the full automated run. World version remains 33.

## Tech I hull expansion milestone 1

The accepted goal covers all 275 published ship-category types whose build-3662168 client type definition has techLevel 1. Faction and special editions remain included even in specialist groups. The reproducible inventory is embedded as TechOneHulls.json with type IDs, groups, acquisition routes and effect IDs. There are 272 market routes and three recovery/developer-fixture routes. Export-HullCatalog.py reads the installed catalog in read-only mode and also exports the minimal first-cohort definitions and prerequisite skills. It includes no world/save data.

The first cohort adds Merlin (603), Punisher (597), Incursus (594) and Rifter (587) to Venture assembly. Gameplay owns the typed assembly admission rule. The existing station transaction retains single-item identity, shared allocation for stack splitting, all-or-nothing batch validation and idempotent assembled-ID retries. Boarding still validates owner, station, active-ship expectation and earned catalog skills. No world schema change is needed.

Hull fitted effects enter the existing exempt dogma graph. The hull's required skills supply catalog pre-multiplication of its racial bonus source attributes, including a zero-level source. Existing weapon and repair operations consume those evaluated module attributes. This gives Merlin small-hybrid damage and shield resistance, Punisher small-energy capacitor and armor resistance, Incursus small-hybrid damage and local armor repair amount, and Rifter small-projectile falloff and rate of fire. Required-skill selectors keep other weapon sizes/families separate. Existing corvette and Venture bonus owners retain their behavior.

| Hull | Delivered capability | Remaining role gaps |
|---|---|---|
| Merlin | Assembly, boarding, native fitting/holds/flight and small-hybrid/shield bonuses | Existing rigs, heat and unsupported equipment |
| Punisher | Assembly, boarding, native fitting/holds/flight and energy-cost/armor bonuses | Existing rigs, heat and unsupported equipment |
| Incursus | Assembly, boarding, native fitting/holds/flight and hybrid/local-repair bonuses | Drone control, existing rigs, heat and unsupported equipment |
| Rifter | Assembly, boarding, native fitting/holds/flight and projectile cycle/falloff bonuses | Existing rigs, heat and unsupported equipment |

References are the installed client/types, client/typedogma, client/dogmaeffects and client/dogmaattributes records for build 3662168. This milestone reuses the existing assembly, boarding, Blue and Destiny paths. The Carbon revisions and code.ccp hash recorded in the preceding milestones remain applicable.

Native acceptance first exposed an existing binding lifetime error. The stock client obtains the incoming ship's inventory before calling ship.Board, then calls GetAvailableTurretSlots and List on that same reference. ChangeShip retired every location reference, causing an Invalid Packet Destination dialog after successful boarding. Boarding within the same docked station now retains location references. Actual location transitions still retire them, and inventory operations retain their owner, station and active-hull validation. A regression replays the incoming binding, production Board dispatch, turret-slot call and inventory read, then verifies departure retires the reference.

Native acceptance on 2026-11-03 used stock build 3662168, Eden Pilot and a copied save in the local Eden Lab t1-hulls-20261103 directory. The fixture relocated that pilot and its original Ibis to Perimeter II – Moon 1 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant, trained the four racial Frigate skills to III and the test equipment prerequisites, and supplied test equipment in Item Hangar. Normal fitting budgets remained in effect. After automated dragging did not move ammunition, the stopped copied save was prepared with 1,000 Iron Charge S in each hybrid frigate, 1,000 Nuclear S in the Rifter and four Multifrequency S in the Punisher. The weapons were then loaded through the native flight module menus. The normal world save and client settings profile were not changed.

| Hull | Actions observed in the stock client | Bonus readout |
|---|---|---|
| Merlin | Bought for 1 ISK, assembled/named/boarded, fitted two 75mm Gatling Rail I, loaded Iron S, undocked, warped to Perimeter VIII belt 1, destroyed the Rookie and redocked | Loaded rail tooltip 5.5 DPS, consistent with 1.65 base multiplier × 1.25 Small Hybrid V × 1.15 hull bonus, five charge damage and 2.1528-second skilled cycle |
| Punisher | Bought for 1 ISK, assembled/named/boarded, fitted two Gatling Pulse Laser I, loaded Multifrequency S, destroyed the Perimeter II belt Rookie and redocked | Fitted activation cost 1.15 GJ, rounded from 1.82 × 0.9 Controlled Bursts II × 0.7 hull bonus |
| Incursus | Bought for 1 ISK, assembled/named/boarded, fitted two rails and Small Armor Repairer II, loaded Iron S, destroyed the respawned Perimeter II Rookie, cycled the repairer and redocked | 113 HP per 4.5 seconds, rounded from 92 × 1.225 hull bonus, with Repair Systems V duration; capacitor visibly fell during operation |
| Rifter | Bought for 1 ISK, assembled/named/boarded, fitted three 125mm Gatling AutoCannon I, loaded Nuclear S, destroyed the Perimeter X belt Rookie and redocked | 1.9-second cycle, rounded from 3 × 0.9 Gunnery V × 0.92 Rapid Firing II × 0.775 hull bonus; 5,869 m falloff from 4,300 × 1.05 Trajectory Analysis I × 1.3 hull bonus |

Inventory showed each active named hull. Returning to the original Ibis showed its expected empty 0/125 m³ cargo. Reboarding the Rifter retained 400 spare rounds and its fitted guns. The Punisher's active selection, name and two fitted lasers survived an earlier host/client restart. Repairing missing armor, exact resistance/application calculations, negative skill/fitting/ownership cases and selector combinations have automated coverage separately from these observations. No missing armor was injected for the native repair check. The Incursus repairer visibly cycled against undamaged armor.

Merlin's first outward alignment contacted the station's collision volume, waited for the existing alignment escape and then completed warp. A manual heading attempted during alignment produced the existing unsupported movement response; the later flight and return completed. This is separate from hull bonuses. Opening assembled-ship context menus also exposed the existing unimplemented insurance query. Boarding by double-clicking the Ship Hangar entry worked. These observations do not establish insurance or new obstacle-avoidance support.

The final host/client restart retained the named active Rifter. Inventory displayed 400 Nuclear S in its 140 m³ hold; Fitting displayed three loaded guns and 20.1 DPS. A read-only comparison against the stopped pre-restart save confirmed identical identities/names for all five owned test ships and all 23 ship/module inventory records, including 198 rounds in each Rifter gun. Armor and structure damage fields also matched; nonzero damage persistence is exercised by the automated hull cases. Shields retain their existing passive recharge behavior.

Automated acceptance passed pinned tool/solution restore, build with zero warnings/errors and all **1,040 .NET tests** in **10 minutes 21 seconds**. After extending the four hull cases with explicit undock, native ballpark initialization and redocking assertions, the focused hull/boarding/binding suite passed all **25 cases** again. Its 19 hull cases cover the 275-ID inventory, assembly identity/splitting/retries/atomic rejection, skills, every first-cohort bonus, wrong weapon family/size exclusions, actual combat/paid repair inputs, cargo/magazines, flight initialization and saved health. The full suite also retains Venture, corvette and capsule regressions. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy cases), and formatting verification passed across 367 files with the 140-character policy. Re-exporting the 275-ID inventory and 28-type first-cohort fixture produced byte-identical files. This verifies the first milestone; the remaining hull cohorts and final 275-ID capability audit remain outstanding.


## Tech I hull expansion milestone 2

The small-hull cohort contains **99** published build-3662168 Tech I types: 51 frigates, 24 destroyers, 11 shuttles, nine corvettes, and one each in Covert Ops (Chremoas), Interceptor (Imp), Electronic Attack (Raiju) and Prototype Exploration (Zephyr). Eligibility uses the type's publication and Tech I metadata together with a typed hull class. The usual tech level of a group does not exclude its qualifying special edition.

`HullBonusRules` owns the capability classification in Gameplay. Server loads every admitted hull effect and applies supported selectors to the existing fitting graph. Operational effects must be fitted effects with a supported operation; unknown selectors fail explicitly. Hull and skill modifiers remain exempt from equipment stacking penalties. Required hull skills supply their own earned-level pre-multipliers, including zero, and retain normal boarding requirements. Modules and owned missile charges receive only matching group or required-skill effects. The catalog route also owns corvette resistance, weapon and mining roles and Venture yield; historical fixtures without Tech I metadata retain their prior compatibility route, avoiding duplicate role multiplication.

Weapon reload now reads the evaluated fitting value when it commits real magazines and their deadlines. Dramiel, Chremoas and Mekubal therefore use their catalog 75% projectile reload reduction: a ten-second base reload becomes 2.5 seconds. The same graph supplies supported turret, missile, ore-mining, local repair, shield boost, afterburner, MWD, tackle and armor-plate bonuses. No new drone, probe, hacking, energy warfare, remote repair, electronic warfare, cloak, cyno, ice/gas mining, salvage, entosis, smartbomb, disintegrator, Vorton or interdiction system is claimed.

The generated [per-hull capability record](tech-one-hulls.json) lists each admitted type ID once with acquisition, required skills, rack/hardpoint limits, personal cargo/mining-hold capacities, effect IDs and deferred capabilities. Drone-bay hulls retain a drone dependency even when they lack an explicit drone bonus. Equipment families and role systems already listed as unavailable remain unavailable; assembly and flight do not imply those operations work. Zephyr's probe launcher and Echelon's analyzer remain among these role dependencies. The common gaps for rigs and heat also remain.

`Export-HullCatalog.py --cohort small` reproduces the 99-hull definitions and recursive equipment/skill prerequisites in `SmallHullCatalog.json` (165 types). Re-exporting it and the unchanged 275-ID inventory produced byte-identical files. The fixture SHA-256 is `1901ffaf4c3d07b4a02d48d2188126d65a3cd802f6eac32910bc73f49848404f`. The read-only server command `--export-hull-coverage <catalog> <output>` regenerates the capability record from the same gameplay rules used in production. It does not load or mutate a world.

Automated acceptance passed pinned tool/solution restore, build with zero warnings/errors, and all **1,248 .NET tests** in **21 minutes 56 seconds**. The cohort contributes 100 lifecycle/audit cases, 99 catalog bonus cases and nine operation cases. Every hull's lifecycle covers acquisition, assembly identity/splitting/retries, atomic malformed-batch rejection, foreign/remote denial, boarding skills, native fitting limits, saved cargo/modules/magazines/health, flight initialization, undocking and redocking. Every supported bonus is compared with its catalog operator, target and earned skill. Operation cases cover paid reloads and saved deadlines, actual webbed player motion, afterburner cycles, ore yield, hold rejection/capacity and shield restoration. Cormorant fits seven turrets and one launcher within real resources and rejects extra turrets and modules. After adding explicit probe/analyzer dependencies for Zephyr and Echelon, the cohort audit passed again on the final build. Both Python suites passed (three catalog setup and nine formatting-policy cases), and formatting verification passed across 372 files with the 140-character policy. Copied-world native acceptance is recorded below.

Native observations use Eden Pilot, the unchanged build-3662168 client and the same private settings profile/copied Perimeter save as milestone 1. Packaged hulls and equipment were staged only in that copy; the native client performed the assembly, naming, boarding and fitting described here. Dramiel, Garmur, Consortium and Fleet Thrasher instead used assembled developer-fixture hulls, explicitly identified below. Qualified fixture skills were trained in the copy. On login, Inventory still showed the named Rifter and its 400 Nuclear S from milestone 1.

The station's Board my Corvette control recovered a new racial Ibis while retaining the previous ships. Inventory selected Ibis and Fitting showed its online civilian gun and miner. Reboarding Consortium retained 2,573 Veldspar in Mining Hold before the final copied-world host/client restart.

After that restart, Eden Pilot selection retained Venture Consortium Issue. Ibis recovery was observed separately. Other cohort hulls have automated coverage only. This verifies milestone 2's small-hull scope; larger hulls, specialized utility holds and the final 275-ID audit remain outstanding.

The unchanged Carbon Blue/Destiny revisions and pinned code.ccp hash from milestone 1 remain applicable. This cohort adds no serialization or movement-engine format.

## Tech I hull expansion milestone 3

The combat cohort adds **101** catalog entries: 36 cruisers, 35 battleships, 21 combat battlecruisers, six attack battlecruisers, Moracha in Force Recon and Fiend/Laelaps in Heavy Interdiction Cruiser. Their own published Tech I metadata controls eligibility. Together with milestone 2 this admits 200 of the 275 qualifying hulls. Stratios Emergency Responder has assembly/bonus coverage through an isolated fixture; its opt-in developer acquisition and native acceptance belong to milestone 5.

Gameplay now classifies medium/large hybrid, projectile and energy selectors, launcher-group modifiers, battleship shield-extender and bulkhead roles. Existing fitting and operation owners consume the evaluated values. Shield extenders receive the hull's capacity bonus, plates their armor bonus and bulkheads the additive structure multiplier; the module's own passive effect then changes the ship's defenses. Attack battlecruisers use catalog reductions to large-weapon CPU, grid and capacitor costs. No artificial hull fitting resources are introduced.

Additional explicit deferred dependencies include command bursts, medium/large disintegrators and Vorton projectors, drone subfamilies, remote capacitor transfer, burst jammers and heavy interdiction field generators. Nestor's 5,000 m³ maintenance bay needs the separate assembled-ship storage service and remains listed as unavailable. The fitting restrictions on Small/Medium Breacher Pod Launcher (85084/85085) identify Tholos/Cenotaph respectively; both hulls now explicitly list breacher pods as unavailable. Large/Medium Micro Jump Drive (4383/33915) fitting restrictions identify the qualifying battleship/battlecruiser groups, which retain an unavailable micro-jump role. These dependencies have no hull modifierInfo entries. Their presence does not grant fitting or activation support. Cenotaph's web resistance continues through the existing incoming-tackle owner.

Algos Navy Issue's Propulsion Jamming selector modifies drone health. Its coverage record now attributes that dependency to drones rather than interdiction; this corrects the role label without enabling either system. Apart from that correction and Tholos's explicit breacher-pod gap, the other 97 small-hull records are unchanged.

Automated acceptance passed pinned tool/solution restore, build with zero warnings/errors and all **1,477 .NET tests** in **36 minutes 31 seconds**. The new cohort contributes 101 lifecycle cases, 101 catalog bonus cases, one cohort audit and 26 operation cases. Complete weapon batteries fit the unmodified catalog budgets of Moa, Megathron, Tempest, Apocalypse, Oracle, Naga, Talos, Tornado and Anhinga. Paid firing and missile launches consume real ammunition and commit evaluated costs, cycles, payloads and flight. Battleship tank roles reach actual defenses; a paid incoming 60% web reduces qualified Cenotaph speed by 45%, with its 25% hull resistance. Medium/large bonuses exclude other sizes and families. CPU/grid assertions honor the existing two-decimal fitting precision.

Individual hull cases load their own hull, the original Ibis and all fixture equipment/skills; complete cohort audits still load every hull. The 220-type combat fixture reproduces byte-for-byte with SHA-256 `51288e42c3eb0870c9b569f124c738d734c021f90801c25e90337b86bebd95af`. Final reporting-only additions for breacher pods and micro-jump drives passed both cohort audits again on the final build. The Python suites passed all three catalog setup and nine formatting-policy cases. Formatting verification passed across 374 files with the 140-character policy. The generated capability record contains 200 unique IDs, including 62 micro-jump dependencies and both breacher-pod hulls.

Copied-world native acceptance flew Moa, Caracal, Oracle, Raven, Anhinga and Cenotaph. All six began as assembled developer-fixture hulls with explicit equipment/cargo and qualified skills. Those preparations are not native acquisition or assembly observations. Initial login retained Consortium's 2,573 Veldspar from milestone 2. For the two-pilot incoming-web check, only the copied accounts were changed to local Omega clone grade after the stock client rejected Alpha aggression; the two accounts remained separate. Both native profiles used Window mode.

No new transport, Blue or Destiny serialization was introduced, so the previously recorded matching Carbon revisions remain the reference.

## Tech I hull expansion milestone 4

The utility cohort adds **35** types: 18 haulers, three mining barges, six freighters, two industrial command ships, Odysseus in Expedition Command and five special-edition yachts. Together with the earlier cohorts, assembly admission reaches **235 IDs**. Capital admission and both capsule exceptions remain milestone 5 work.

Typed hold definitions now cover all **19 positive native hold flags** present across the 275-type catalog. These include personal use of drone/fighter bays, maintenance bays, fleet hangars, fuel, mining, gas, ice, mineral, ammunition, command-center, planetary, Quafe, corpse, mobile-depot, infrastructure and expedition holds. Inventory uses their evaluated dogma capacities and the pinned `inventoryFlagsCommon.ShouldAllowAdd` category/group/type filters. Unknown or absent holds cannot fall back to ordinary cargo. Personal fleet-hangar storage is separate from fleet access, and stored fighters/drones do not gain combat operations.

The frigate escape bay has a distinct unit rule. Build 3662168 `invCacheContainer.GetFrigateEscapeBayCapacity` (lines 1407–1413) returns capacity 1.0 and normalizes any nonempty bay to used 1.0. Attribute 3020 also uses native Units rather than cubic metres. Eden counts one assembled eligible frigate there; maintenance bays measure assembled hull volume. The generated capability record includes each hold's native flag, capacity attribute, base capacity and unit.

World version **38** adds an assembled hull's container/flag and a stored-ship loss manifest. Loading, hauling and unloading retain hull identity, name, modules, magazines, paid deadlines and damage. Stored hulls are excluded from station ship lists, assembly retries, boarding and corvette recovery until unloaded. The existing inventory calls permit read-only inspection of owned carried contents, with location checks on every call. A stale binding cannot retain access after its carrier moves to another station. The stock client also inspects an assembled wreck survivor before attempting a transfer; that read requires its owner, a visible wreck, an open flight and the same 2,500-metre transfer range. It never grants permission to edit the survivor in the wreck. Personal fleet hangars also accept assembled hulls where their full assembled volume fits. Nested ships must be unloaded first.

The stored ship can retain fitted equipment, drones/fighters in their bays, and permitted cargo: charges (including nanite paste), boosters, liquid ozone, strontium and subsystems. This follows [CCP's published escape-bay design and shared SMA/SMB cargo restriction](https://forums.eveonline.com/t/frigate-escape-bay-feedback-thread/221753/2). Other cargo is rejected; ammunition in a specialized ammunition hold may remain. Loading the escape bay checks the frigate's required pilot skills, while a maintenance bay may transport an unqualified hull without granting permission to board it.

Stock `shipConfigSvc.GetShipConfig` calls `ship.GetShipConfiguration(shipID)`, and `TreeViewEntryAccessConfig` reads four booleans to initialize the active ship's fleet-hangar and maintenance-bay access buttons. Eden returns the native dictionary keys `FleetHangar_AllowFleetAccess`, `FleetHangar_AllowCorpAccess`, `SMB_AllowFleetAccess` and `SMB_AllowCorpAccess`, all false, for the current active ship only. Attempts to change them are rejected. This initializes personal holds without introducing fleet/corporation sharing; it adds no new public client API. Native `config.GetMultiLocationsEx` also returns carried hull names, using the same ownership/location rule as inventory reads, plus surviving hulls in the owner's visible wrecks.

On carrier loss, each assembled child is rolled once as a complete stored ship. Survivors keep their fit/state in the owned wreck and can be recovered into a compatible personal maintenance bay. Ordinary cargo and in-space frigate-escape-bay loading reject them. Destroyed children and expired wreck contents remove their fitted items, magazines, cooldowns and damage without leaving orphaned records. Automatic frigate escape, fleet access and fleet refitting remain explicit role dependencies.

Ordinary **Strip Miner I (17482)** and **ORE Strip Miner (28754)** reuse the existing mining effect 67 and paid ore cycle, with the catalog's allowed ship groups. They consume high slots without turret hardpoints. Ice harvesters share group 464/effect 67, so their required Ice Harvesting skill explicitly excludes them from ordinary ore support. Mining crystals, deep-core hazards, ice/gas operations and industrial cores remain unavailable. MiningRules owns Mining/Astrogeology yield; Astrogeology's direct barge prerequisite no longer adds a second graph multiplier. The source bonus calculation also covers Advanced Spaceship Command's intermediate agility attribute. Native fitting reconstructs its own catalog graph: the pilot brain now carries the same Mining, Astrogeology, Precision and Exploitation modifiers, selecting equipment that requires Mining. Boarding and corvette recovery explicitly send `OnServerBrainUpdated`; the stock `clientDogmaIM.GetDogmaLocation(charBrain=...)` reuses an existing location and otherwise retains the old hull's skill selectors. This was observed as a docked Covetor tooltip of 155 m3/16 km while the paid operation used 269.53125 m3/19.5 km. After correction, its docked and in-space tooltips both show the rounded 270 m3/20 km. Regressions reconstruct all six barge/miner fitting values from native brain literals and catalog bases independently of effective module primes.

At Mining Barge V, Mining V and Astrogeology V, Strip Miner I yields **269.53125 m³ / 33.75 seconds / 17.25 GJ / 19.5 km** on Covetor; Retriever yields **296.484375 m³ / 45 seconds / 23 GJ / 15 km**, with **34,375 m³** mining hold; Procurer yields **257.8125 m³ / 45 seconds / 23 GJ / 15 km**. ORE Strip Miner uses its own 200 m³ base yield, 18.75 km base range and 50 CPU. Neither profile enables Mercoxit or crystals. Primae and Noctis also now accept an omitted zero-valued turret-hardpoint field during pilot initialization.

The native inventory fixture is reproducible with `tools/Export-ShipInventory.py` against the hash-checked stock script archive. It evaluates the inert client cargo predicate on **362 cargo probes**, records all 275 hull volumes/hold capacities and covers the packaged-volume groups used by other market ships. No client code is modified or live game process started by the exporter. Repeated exports matched SHA-256 `b772994fff506ab9b499f0fac4ea41ec4df443a1587d67f15cbc36d30ca0854f`. `Export-HullCatalog.py --cohort utility` separately reproduced the 223-type utility fixture byte-for-byte, SHA-256 `2d9a913e7271adfad63a1ba24f597fa1b988fd20ac9427b5408f011b045d32b1`.

Native acceptance also exercises the stock ship-menu and drone-panel initialization calls. `menucheckers.itemCheckers.ItemChecker.IsInsurable` (line 1347) requests `insuranceSvc.GetInsurancePrice(typeID)` before cargo actions become available. Eden returns a zero quote, leaving insurance unavailable. `godma.StateManager.ChangeDroneSettings` (line 2711) forwards client-owned aggression/focus-fire preferences, attribute IDs 1275 and 1297. `CharSettingBool.is_enabled` returns its raw catalog default, including floating 1.0/0.0 values; Eden accepts boolean, integer or floating zero/one values only. Eden validates and acknowledges those preferences so a loaded drone bay can initialize; launch and combat calls remain unsupported. The native drone tracker also calls `dogmaIM.GetLayerDamageValuesByItems(set(itemIDs))` and reads KeyVal fields `shieldInfo` (fraction, recharge time constant and sample timestamp), `armorDamage` and `hullDamage`. Only drones in the active ship's own Drone Bay can be queried. They remain pristine inventory while launch/combat are unavailable. Both direct calls and a first call during native binding have regression coverage.

Large-hull acceptance exposed a launch-clearance error: the old position was station radius plus 500 metres, omitting the hull radius. Orca has a 550-metre catalog radius, Bowhead 600 and Charon 800. Typed `DockingDefinitions.UndockPosition` now includes both radii plus clearance. This follows the sum of both collision radii in [Carbon Destiny `Ballpark::Potential`, lines 2560–2565](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp#L2560-L2565), revision `27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f`. The motion integration and wire format remain unchanged. The all-hull lifecycle checks assert clearance, and synthetic large-hull regressions verify that an undocked hull can accelerate away from the station.

The expanded storage/native-rule regression suite passed **321 cases** in **5 minutes 46 seconds**, covering the 362-probe fixture, consumables/subsystems, specialized ammunition, personal fleet-hangar storage, version-37 migration, nested/non-permitted cargo rejection, carried-inventory binding checks, loss/recovery and expiry. A separate **161-case** run passed in **21 minutes 17 seconds**, exercising personal holds on all 160 admitted hulls with additional holds and a carried-inventory binding. After the native fitting correction, **26 focused checks** passed, including all six barge/miner combinations and the native boarding notification. The recovery session regression now also checks the additional brain notification before the inventory changes. The complete repository suite passed **2,041 tests in 35 minutes 50 seconds** before these final native compatibility corrections. Those corrections then passed **17 focused tests**, the mining/boarding correction passed **26**, and the adjusted recovery notification regression passed **one**. The subsequent full rerun exposed that old recovery-message expectation; it was corrected and verified directly. A duplicate full rerun was stopped when the user requested an end to extended acceptance. A fresh completed 2,054-case run is therefore not claimed. Tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; the final catalog tests (**3**), formatting-policy tests (**9**) and `Format.ps1 -Check` (**384 files**, 140-column check) passed in repository order. All 235 admitted hulls have catalog-driven lifecycle/bonus coverage from the completed suite, with all-hold capacity/filter/restart coverage for the 160 hulls with extra holds. No Blue codec, Destiny serialization or movement step has changed.

### Milestone 4 stock-client observations

Computer Use selected **Eden Pilot** in stock build 3662168 against the copied `t1-hulls-20261103` world on November 3–4, 2026. The copied accounts were qualified Omega test pilots. Hoarder and Nestor were purchased for 1 ISK, assembled, named and boarded through the native market and Ship Hangar. Other utility examples used explicitly assembled developer fixtures in the copied save. No fixture was applied to the normal world.

| Hull | Native action and visible result |
| --- | --- |
| Hoarder 651 | Purchased and assembled **hoarder trial**; packaged volume changed from 20,000 to 400,000 m³. Gas Hold accepted 4,500 Amber Cytoserocin at **45,000/45,000 m³**. Another unit was rejected with 10 m³ required and zero free; Tritanium was rejected as incompatible cargo. Ammo Hold accepted 1,000 Iron Charge S at **2.5/61,500 m³**. Undocked, flew at 99.6 then 152 m/s and redocked with both holds retained. |
| Kryos 654 | Boarded **kryos trial**. Mineral Hold accepted 7,500,000 Tritanium at **75,000/75,000 m³**; Ice Hold accepted 45 Blue Ice at **45,000/45,000 m³**. Flight has automated coverage only. |
| Epithal 655 | Boarded **epithal trial**. Command Center Hold accepted six Barren Command Centers at **6,000/6,000 m³**. Planetary Commodities Hold accepted 355,263 Water against **67,500 m³** capacity. Flight has automated coverage only. |
| Miasmos Quafe Ultra Edition 4363 | Boarded **quafe trial**, loaded 10,000 Quafe at **1,000/10,000 m³**, then unloaded the bay to zero. The Quafe subsequently travelled in Orca's fleet hangar. This hull's flight has automated coverage only. |
| Primae 2863 | Boarded **primae trial** despite the omitted zero turret attribute. Fitting showed zero high/medium slots, four low slots, **125 CPU / 218.8 MW** and **100 m³** ordinary cargo. Flight has automated coverage only. |
| Odysseus 89607 | Boarded **odysseus trial** and loaded 10,000 Nanite Repair Paste into Expedition Hold at **100/50,000 m³**. Flight has automated coverage only. |
| Orca 28606 | Loaded the fitted **rifter trial** into personal Fleet Hangar at **27,289/40,000 m³**, rejected boarding while held, then moved it into Ship Maintenance Bay at **27,289/400,000 m³**. Drone Bay showed five Warrior II at **25/200 m³**; Fuel Bay 1,000 Heavy Water at **400/6,400 m³**; Mining Hold 5,500 gas at **55,000/187,500 m³**. Fleet Hangar carried 10,000 Quafe at **1,000/40,000 m³** to Perimeter VII's Poksu Mineral Reserve, where it unloaded to zero. Sharing buttons initialized closed and enabling shared access was rejected. The final drone panel showed five drones with full shield/armor/hull bars and no compatibility modal. Returned to the original station and redocked. |
| Nestor 33472 | Purchased, assembled, named and boarded **nestor trial**. Ship Maintenance Bay accepted **leopard trial** at **5,000/5,000 m³**, then unloaded it. Frigate Escape Bay accepted the fitted Rifter at **1.0/1.0 Frigate**; a second eligible Rifter was rejected with the one-assembled-frigate message. Unloaded the Rifter again. This milestone exercised docked storage; Nestor flight has automated coverage only. |
| Bowhead 34328 | Boarded **bowhead trial**. Ship Maintenance Bay showed its ORE Freighter V capacity of **2,000,000 m³**. Flight has automated coverage only. |
| Covetor 17476 | Fitted Strip Miner I and ORE Strip Miner in two high slots without turret hardpoints. A third miner was rejected with **No compatible module slot is available.** Fitting used **110 CPU / 20 MW**. Warped to ordinary Veldspar in Perimeter II's belt. Tooltips showed **270 m³ / 33.8 s / 20 km** and **359 m³ / 33.8 s / 24 km** respectively. First complete cycles collected 6,289 units; stopping the next partial cycles left **10,331 Veldspar / 1,033.1 m³** in the 9,000 m³ hold. Redocked, restarted host/client, verified both miners and all ore, then unloaded to zero. The corrected docked tooltip matched the operation values. |
| Retriever 17478 | Boarded after Charon without relogging and fitted Strip Miner I. Docked Fitting immediately showed **296 m³ / 45 s / 15 km**. Mining Hold showed **34,375 m³**. Warped to the belt and collected **2,965 Veldspar / 296.5 m³** in the first full cycle; stopping the next partial cycle left 5,104 units. Redocked and unloaded all ore to zero. |
| Charon 20185 | Boarded **charon trial** with **581,250 m³** cargo. Reinforced Bulkheads I used zero CPU under the hull role; Fitting and Inventory both showed **523,125 m³** after its cargo penalty, with no high/medium slots. Undocked at 500 m surface clearance, accelerated at 24.5 then 38.6 m/s, used Orbit to clear station contact at 28 then 41.5 m/s, and redocked. |
| Squall 81008 | Infrastructure Hold accepted **355,263 Water / 67,499.97 of 67,500 m³**. Heavy Missile Launcher I used **4.5 CPU / 10 MW**; docked and in-space tooltips agreed on **28 km / 27.6 DPS / 373 kinetic HP**. Loaded 30 Scourge Heavy from 200 carried rounds, warped to the belt, locked the Rookie and fired. Magazine fell to 29 and the NPC became a wreck. Redocked with all Water and 170 spare rounds, then unloaded the infrastructure hold to zero. |

| Victorieux Luxury Yacht 34590 | Boarded **yacht trial**; Inventory showed **0/30 m³**. Fitting showed **162.5 CPU / 125 MW**, **312.5 m/s**, **8 AU/s** and no fitted weapon. Undocked from the original station and docked at Perimeter VII Poksu Mineral Reserve. Cloaking remains deferred. |
| Metamorphosis 77114 | Purchased for 1 ISK at Poksu, assembled, named and boarded **depot trial**. Mobile Depot Hold accepted one Mobile Depot at **50/100 m³** and rejected Quafe with the cargo-type message. Undocked and cancelled a homeward warp course obstructed by the station, redocked locally and unloaded the depot to zero. The attempted return is not counted as a completed warp. |

After hauling the Rifter, unloaded and boarded it at the destination. Inventory retained **400 Nuclear S** and Fitting retained three 125mm Gatling AutoCannon I with **198 rounds per magazine**. Read-only saved health retained the fixture's **50 armor / 40 structure damage**. The stock carried-ship context menu opened after the insurance compatibility fix but did not offer Open Cargo while held; read-only carried-content RPC inspection has automated coverage only. Native inspection of the unloaded ship and its fitting passed.

A separate, explicitly seeded **owned NPC wreck** tested assembled recovery. Orca opened it at **636 m**, saw **recovered shuttle fixture**, and transferred the assembled Caldari Shuttle into its maintenance bay. The bay then showed the Rifter plus shuttle at **32,289/400,000 m³**. Redocked, unloaded both and boarded the recovered shuttle. This proves the stock recovery interaction; an actual player carrier loss, the whole-child drop roll, destruction and expiry cleanup have automated coverage only.

The Orca restart snapshots matched both pilot rows, all **39 ships**, **106 items**, **14 health rows** and **22 weapon/reload cooldown rows**, including the stored Rifter. The later Covetor restart separately retained its fitted miners and mined ore. Launch clearance now includes the hull radius; automatic obstacle avoidance remains unavailable. In particular, a return course could still point through a station and use the existing warp timeout. These checks do not establish obstacle-free departure routes.

Seven utility hulls completed native flights: Hoarder, Orca, Covetor, Retriever, Charon, Squall and Victorieux Luxury Yacht. The other **28 utility hulls** have automated flight coverage only; six of those also received the docked native checks above. Metamorphosis, Nestor, the transported Rifter and the recovered shuttle exercise additional hold/storage behavior on earlier cohorts. The normal world remained unchanged at SHA-256 `b3a7d0b27117cefce57bca6e8d014ef93e75d9104d28c60c0b30135e33d7ce87`. No capital hull was admitted or tested in milestone 4. The remaining all-275 goal is paused after this milestone.
